ENEMY_ACT_PHASE's own calculateDamage(rawDamage) call (the enemy's intent damage against the player) was deliberately left untagged/untouched — it's part of the phase machine, which the prompt said not to touch, and leaving its sourceType as undefined already safely excludes it from Fervour's 'attack' check by construction, with zero code change required. NOTE: the prompt's tagging list also named "Ordain" among the mod-damage call sites to tag 'attack' — Ordain does not exist in the codebase yet (confirmed by grep, matches CLAUDE.md's own SCOPE notes that it's unbuilt), so there was no call site to tag; flagged here rather than silently ignored. Fervour itself (config.mods['fervour'], added immediately after Zeal) deals no direct damage — effect() only logs [MOD] fervour: attacks double damage this turn and registers a DAMAGE_MULTIPLIER listener ('fervour_double_attacks', clearOn: 'turn') whose fn(sourceType) returns 2 (and additionally logs [MOD] fervour: hit doubled, so the doubling is visible as its own log line rather than only inferable from a bigger number) when sourceType === 'attack', else 1 — leaving poison and anything else at x1. Turn-scoping reuses the exact same clearListeners('turn') mechanism Consecrate's and Vigil's own turn-scoped listeners already rely on; no new expiry logic was needed. Not placed on any die face at init (grep-confirmed; the playerFaces loop still only assigns 'consecrate' to face 10) — loadable via the existing dev load-mod tool. Fervour was NOT removed from DIE_ACTION_EXCLUDED_MOD_IDS in this pass (unlike Zeal in BUILD 044) since this prompt didn't ask for it — it remains excluded from the post-fight Load reward pool for now; flagged as an open question for a future prompt, not decided here. Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html: force-loading Fervour and triggering it logged the trigger line with zero change to enemy.hp (no direct damage); playing Strike that same turn dealt exactly 10 damage with a [MOD] fervour: hit doubled line present, and a second same-turn Strike also dealt 10 with the same doubled-hit line; ending the turn and playing Strike on the next turn dealt exactly 5 (back to normal) with no doubled-hit line, confirming clearOn: 'turn' expired the multiplier correctly; applying poison to both player and enemy during an active Fervour turn and letting it tick through the next START_OF_TURN produced log lines reading exactly [POISON] player takes 6 damage and [POISON] 6 damage, stacks now 5 (not 12), with no [MOD] fervour: hit doubled line appearing for either poison tick — confirming the 'poison' tag correctly excludes poison from the multiplier. Zero JS errors across all checks. The phase machine, every other mod (Consecrate/Smite/Penance/Offering/Blight/Virulence/Sanctuary/Vigil/Zeal), and card definitions beyond the calculateDamage() tagging were not touched — re-verified by re-reading every modified index.html section after editing. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 046.
Stage 1.10 eleventh pool mod — Ordain (BUILD 047), last of the three originally-deferred mods (Zeal, Fervour, Ordain all now built). Deals 10 damage through calculateDamage(10, 'attack') (Law 1, same 'attack' tagging BUILD 046 introduced), then permanently adds 1 weight to the specific face it triggered from, using the exact clone-then-write pattern devLoadMod()/devClearFace() and the post-fight Strengthen action already use: face.weight read fresh, a new faces array built via .slice() + Object.assign({}, face, {weight: newWeight}) for the one changed entry, written back through updateDie() — gameState.die.faces[i] is never mutated in place, keeping this Law 3-compliant per the code audit's explicit warning that face objects are plain mutable objects with no enforcement of that convention. Required one small, necessary infrastructure change: the generic MOD_TRIGGER dispatcher (registered once in init(), 'mod_dispatch') previously called mod.effect() with no arguments, discarding the { modId, faceNumber } data every MOD_TRIGGER listener already receives — Ordain is the first mod that needs to know which specific face it came from (it can't infer this by scanning config.mods for its own id, since the same mod can be loaded onto more than one face at once), so the dispatcher now calls mod.effect(data) instead. Every other mod's effect is declared as function() with no parameters, so this extra argument is silently ignored there — zero behaviour change, confirmed by re-reading Consecrate/Smite/Penance/Offering/Blight/Virulence/Sanctuary/Vigil/Zeal/Fervour's definitions, none of which needed touching. Reading face.weight fresh on every trigger (rather than tracking a separate counter) means Ordain naturally stacks additively with Strengthen and with repeated Ordain triggers on the same face — no special-casing needed, it's just "current weight + 1" every time regardless of how the current weight got there. Logs [MOD] ordain: N damage, face F weight now W with N as the live calculateDamage() result (not hardcoded, matching how every other attack mod logs). Not placed on any die face at init (grep-confirmed; the playerFaces loop still only assigns 'consecrate' to face 10) — loadable via the existing dev load-mod tool. Like Fervour in BUILD 046, Ordain was NOT removed from DIE_ACTION_EXCLUDED_MOD_IDS in this pass since this prompt didn't ask for it — it remains excluded from the post-fight Load reward pool for now, the same open flag as Fervour's. Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html: force-loading Ordain onto face 6 (starting weight 1) and triggering it twice produced [MOD] ordain: 10 damage, face 6 weight now 2 then face 6 weight now 3, each trigger dealing exactly 10 enemy damage; Restart Fight confirmed weight persisted at 3 (same mechanism as Zeal's accumulatedBonus and every loaded face already relies on — resetFight() doesn't touch gameState.die); rollDie()'s own weighted-pool construction was exercised directly and confirmed face 6 appears exactly 3 times in the pool, and the State Inspector's JSON dump independently confirmed weight: 3, satisfying the "confirm the new weight is picked up on the next roll" success clause both ways named in the prompt. A separate run confirmed additive stacking with Strengthen specifically: Strengthen taken via the post-fight die action screen brought face 6 from weight 1 to 2, then triggering Ordain on that same face brought it to 3 — stacking on top of Strengthen's contribution, not overwriting or ignoring it. Zero JS errors across all runs. calculateDamage()'s existing call sites, the phase machine, Strengthen's own logic (dieActionPickStrengthenFace(), untouched), and every other mod were not touched — re-verified by re-reading every modified index.html section after editing. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 047.
Verification check, not a new substage — reward pool eligibility for Fervour and Ordain (BUILD 048). Confirmed a real gap: DIE_ACTION_EXCLUDED_MOD_IDS still read ['consecrate', 'fervour', 'ordain'] going into this check — both flagged as open questions in BUILD 046's and BUILD 047's own CONFIRMED WORKING notes, since neither of those prompts asked for their removal. Fix (minimal, one line): DIE_ACTION_EXCLUDED_MOD_IDS reduced to ['consecrate'] — Consecrate remains excluded as the class anchor per SCOPE — V1; Zeal, Fervour, and Ordain are now all removed, matching Zeal's own removal in BUILD 044. Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html using the real Skip to Die Action -> Load path (not a structural array check alone): drew the reward pool 60 times, collecting every mod id offered across all draws. All ten non-anchor mods (smite, penance, offering, blight, virulence, sanctuary, vigil, zeal, fervour, ordain) appeared at least once; consecrate never appeared in any of the 60 draws. Zero JS errors. No other logic was touched — this was a one-line array fix plus its explanatory comment. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 048.
Bug fix, not a new substage — Restart Fight stuck in a loss loop (BUILD 049). Root cause was not a call-ordering bug (run.status is already set to 'active' inside resetFight() before startFreshTurnPaused() runs, confirmed by re-reading — that part of the reported hypothesis was wrong) — it was that resetFight() never restored player.hp at all, on any path, per the previously-documented "hp carries over" FIGHT RESET behaviour. That's fine after a win (hp genuinely should carry over into the next fight), but a loss can only ever happen with hp <= 0, so "carrying it over" after a loss meant hp was still <= 0 the instant resetFight() flipped run.status back to 'active' — the very next runPhase('START_OF_TURN') call (from startFreshTurnPaused() immediately after resetFight() in the Restart Fight click handler) hit the existing top-of-function win/loss guard again with status now 'active' and hp still <= 0, re-triggering the loss immediately: [INIT] fight reset followed by [LOSS] player defeated, forever, never reaching START_OF_TURN's own logic. Fix: resetFight()'s updatePlayer() call now includes hp: gameState.player.maxHp alongside the fields it already reset. FLAG FOR PLANNING: this intentionally changes previously-documented behaviour — CLAUDE.md's FIGHT RESET section (line 436, not editable by Claude Code) currently reads "hp carries over," and CORE NUMBERS documents "Player HP 70. Carries between fights" under a stated Permadeath design. This fix makes Restart Fight always restore full HP regardless of win or loss, which is what the prompt's own success criteria explicitly required (70/70 after Restart Fight in both the win and the loss case) and is also the only way Restart Fight can function as the repeatable dev/testing reset it's been used as throughout this whole build (five-consecutive-clean-fights verification, the various "restart and retest" success criteria across many prior builds) — but it does mean a loss is no longer truly unrecoverable via this button, which may be intentional only for the dev/testing period rather than final run design. The win/loss guard itself, CHECK_WIN_LOSS, and die state persistence were not touched, per the prompt. Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html across both paths: (1) forcing player.hp to 0 and driving a real loss through runPhase(), then clicking Restart Fight — no [LOSS] line reappeared, player.hp read 70/70, run.status was 'active', turn.phase correctly reached START_OF_TURN (pausing there per the existing BUILD 037 setup-pause behaviour) with round reset to 1, and clicking Begin Roll afterward settled cleanly into CARD_PHASE with a 5-card hand and End Turn enabled — the game was genuinely playable again, not just superficially reset; (2) the same sequence repeated after a forced win produced the identical clean result with no [WIN] or [LOSS] line reappearing. Zero JS errors across both. The win/loss guard's own logic, CHECK_WIN_LOSS, die state persistence, and every other resetFight() field were not touched — re-verified by re-reading the modified index.html section after editing. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 049.
Stage 1.11 — card reward screen (BUILD 050), first substage of Stage 1.11. Screen only, using three throwaway placeholder cards; no real card pool designed yet. New config.cardPool holds TEST_A (1 soul, 7 damage), TEST_B (1 soul, 7 block), TEST_C (2 soul, 7 damage + 7 block) — each also registered as the same object reference in config.cards (not a copy), so once added to a deck they're playable through the exact same getCard()/drawCards()/playCard() path every real card already uses; no changes were needed to any of those three functions. The reward screen (openCardRewardScreen()/cardRewardStep/cardRewardOptions, same non-gameState module-level-variable convention the die action screen already uses) draws 3 ids at random from Object.keys(config.cardPool) with replacement — duplicates allowed, no exclusion list, per the prompt. Ordering requirement ("appears... after the die action panel is resolved, not before it") is satisfied by a single added line at the end of closeDieActionScreen() calling openCardRewardScreen() — that function is the one place all three die-action resolution paths (Skip/Load-a-face/Strengthen-a-face) funnel through, so this fires after any of them, whether the die action screen was reached via a real win or the existing Skip to Die Action dev shortcut; nothing else in the die action screen's own logic, rendering, or exclusion list was touched. Picking a card (cardRewardPickCard()) writes to two fields in one updatePlayer() call: deck (this fight's draw pile, so the count reflects the pick immediately) and a new persistent field, gameState.player.ownedCards (the player's full permanent card collection, added to the STATE SCHEMA — starts as a copy of the Ordained starting deck). This was necessary because resetFight() previously always rebuilt deck by reshuffling the fixed startingDeck constant, which would have silently discarded any reward cards on the very next Restart Fight; resetFight() now reshuffles from gameState.player.ownedCards instead (one line changed, comment added) — this is the mechanism that makes reward additions survive Restart Fight, matching how die state already persists (BUILD 038). init()'s own deck setup now also seeds ownedCards alongside the existing deck shuffle. Skip (cardRewardSkip()) logs and closes with no state change. Log formats match exactly: [CARD REWARD] added TEST_A to deck, deck now N cards (N = gameState.player.deck.length, the live draw-pile count, post-add) and [CARD REWARD] skipped, deck still N cards. Rendering: a new #cardRewardPanel <div>, placed immediately after #dieActionPanel in the HTML and given its own CSS rule with properties byte-identical to #dieActionPanel's (gold border, flex column) as a fully separate rule/element rather than editing #dieActionPanel's own rule or reusing its DOM node, so "same place and style" is satisfied without touching the die action panel at all; the three card buttons and Skip button reuse the die action panel's existing .die-action-title/.die-action-row/button CSS classes verbatim (zero new button styling needed). #startGameBtn.disabled now also checks cardRewardStep !== null (extending the existing pattern already used for dieActionStep) and #endTurnBtn's existing disabled condition gained the same && cardRewardStep === null clause — both are the same symmetric protection the die action panel already had, extended to cover the new panel, not a change to what protection dieActionStep itself provides. A new DEV ONLY #devSkipToCardRewardBtn sits beside #devSkipToDieActionBtn in #devStepControl and calls openCardRewardScreen() directly, the same function the real chain calls — mirrors the existing Skip to Die Action dev button's own pattern exactly. Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html: the dev shortcut reached the panel without playing a fight, showed 3 buttons plus Skip with duplicates visibly possible (all three offered were TEST_A in one draw), and Restart Fight was confirmed disabled while the panel was pending; picking TEST_A logged the exact expected line and increased both gameState.player.deck.length and ownedCards.length by 1 each; a fresh Restart Fight afterward correctly reshuffled from the now-11-card ownedCards collection (confirmed both by direct length checks and by total deck+hand+discard summing to 11 throughout, persisting through the reset) and TEST_A was drawn into the next fight's hand and played for exactly 7 enemy damage — the core "added, persists, playable" requirement, fully confirmed; a real forced win was also driven through runPhase(), confirming cardRewardStep stayed null while the die action panel was still up (not offered before it), then automatically appeared the instant Skip was clicked on the die action panel, and Skip on the card reward screen itself logged correctly with zero deck change. NOTE on the prompt's own "10 to 11" example: verified precisely that the true total card count (deck+hand+discard, and ownedCards) goes 10->11 exactly as stated and persists correctly — but the literal on-screen "Deck" stat specifically displays gameState.player.deck.length, the draw pile only (pre-existing, unrelated behavior, unchanged by this build), which in practice is never observably 10 at the moment a reward can be picked, since START_OF_TURN's own drawCards(5) has always already run by the time CARD_PHASE (where a win is first detectable) is reached — so the live panel number at pick time will typically read something other than 10 (e.g. 5, or 6 after the pick), while the true total and ownedCards figures are always exactly correct. Flagged rather than silently assumed away. Zero JS errors across all runs. Die action panel logic/rendering/exclusion list, the mod pool, calculateDamage(), the phase machine, drawCards()/playCard() (the draw/discard cycling), and the enemy path were not touched — re-verified by re-reading every modified index.html section after editing. Build stamp now STAGE 1.11 / BUILD 050.
Stage 1.11 substage 2 — real card pool, tier 1 (BUILD 051). TEST_A/B/C (BUILD 050) deleted entirely from both config.cards and config.cardPool — grep-confirmed zero remaining code references (two explanatory comments mention the old ids by name only, describing the deletion itself, not using them). Added the eight tier-1 cards named in the prompt (rebuke 0/4dmg, censure 2/14dmg, judgement 3/20dmg, vestment 2/13block, litany 2/7dmg+7block, scripture 1/draw2, communion 1/+2soul, censer 1/+4poison), each registered in config.cards exactly like strike/ward/rite and referenced (same object, not copies) from config.cardPool — no changes needed to getCard()/drawCards()/playCard(), all eight are playable through those functions unmodified. Every damage card routes through calculateDamage(N, 'attack') (Law 1, same tagging every other attack source uses since BUILD 046); Vestment/Litany's block through generateBlock() (Rite's own pattern for a combined card); Scripture calls the existing drawCards(2) directly, the same function Offering's mod effect already calls; Communion grants soul via updatePlayer({ soul: gameState.player.soul + 2 }), the exact same call shape the Offering mod uses; Censer applies poison via updateEnemy({ poisonStacks: gameState.enemy.poisonStacks + 4 }), the exact same field the Blight mod and the dev poison applier already write to — no new phase logic, no changes to the decay tick anywhere. Rebuke's 0 soul cost needed no code change to play at 0 soul or avoid dimming: both the affordability check (gameState.player.soul >= card.soulCost) and playCard()'s own refusal check (gameState.player.soul < card.soulCost) already treat cost 0 as always satisfied at any soul value including 0, by ordinary arithmetic (0 >= 0 is true, 0 < 0 is false) — this substage only had to confirm that already-correct behaviour extends to a real 0-cost card, which it does. CARD_EFFECT_TEXT gained entries for all eight, exact strings from the prompt (4 damage / 14 damage / 20 damage / 13 block / 7 damage 7 block / draw 2 / 2 soul / 4 poison) — hand cards render name, cost badge, and this line automatically via the existing renderCardButtons(), no changes needed there. Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html: config.cardPool now holds exactly the eight new ids and none of TEST_A/B/C; drawing the reward pool 40 times via the real Skip to Card Reward -> offer path surfaced all eight and never a TEST_ id; picking Censure persisted it, and after Restart Fight it was drawn into the next fight's hand and played for exactly 14 damage; Rebuke was confirmed playable at soul 0 with no 'unaffordable' class and no disabled attribute, logging [CARD] played Rebuke (cost 0) and dealing exactly 4 damage while soul stayed at 0; Scripture increased hand size by exactly 2 and dropped deck by exactly 2; Censer set enemy poisonStacks to exactly 4, and four direct calls to the existing runPhase('START_OF_TURN') poison-tick branch (untouched) correctly decayed it 3, 2, 1, 0, confirming Censer feeds the existing, unmodified decay model correctly; Judgement at 3 soul dealt exactly 20 damage and left soul at exactly 0; all eight ids were confirmed present in gameState.player.ownedCards after Restart Fight. Zero JS errors across all five scenarios. NOTE on the prompt's own "Communion takes soul from 2 to 4" example: verified this does not happen, and correctly so — Communion costs 1 soul to play, and playCard() (untouched, as instructed) deducts that cost before running the card's effect, so starting from soul 2 the actual sequence is 2 -> 1 (cost paid) -> 3 (effect grants 2) — a net +1, landing at 3, not 4. This matches Communion's own stated design ("1 soul, gain 2 soul") exactly; reaching net +2 would require either giving Communion 0 cost (contradicts "1 soul") or having its effect grant 3 instead of 2 (contradicts "gain 2 soul" and the required "2 soul" display text) — neither was done, since both would mean overriding an explicit spec to chase an inconsistent example number, the same category of discrepancy flagged in BUILD 044 (Zeal) and BUILD 050 (the "10 to 11" deck figure). Die action panel, the card reward screen's own flow logic (only its data source changed), the mod pool, calculateDamage()'s existing call sites, the phase machine, the poison decay model, and the enemy path were not touched — re-verified by re-reading every modified index.html section after editing. Build stamp now STAGE 1.11 / BUILD 051.
Stage 1.11 substage 3 — Communion fix and tier-3 state-read conditionals (BUILD 052). Communion's soulCost dropped from 1 to 0 (its effect, grant 2 soul, is unchanged); this was a wash (net +1 soul for a card slot) below the 6-8 per soul band, now a clean 0-cost +2. Added three of the four state-reading cards flagged after BUILD 051 (Retribution deliberately excluded, per the prompt): Purge (1 soul, calculateDamage(6, 'attack') normally or calculateDamage(10, 'attack') if gameState.enemy.poisonStacks > 0, read at play time), Interdict (1 soul, generateBlock(5) normally or generateBlock(10) if gameState.enemy.intent >= 12, reading the same intent field the stat panel already displays), and Reckoning (1 soul, calculateDamage(3 + 2*stacks, 'attack') where stacks = gameState.enemy.poisonStacks read at play time, after any poison already applied earlier that turn — no special ordering needed since it's just an ordinary live read, not a snapshot). All three registered in config.cards and referenced into config.cardPool exactly like the BUILD 051 eight, with no exclusion list — getCard()/drawCards()/playCard() and the reward screen's Object.keys(config.cardPool) draw needed no changes. Each branch is logged explicitly rather than left inferable from the number alone: [CARD] purge: 6 damage / [CARD] purge: 10 damage, enemy poisoned; [CARD] interdict: 5 block / [CARD] interdict: 10 block, intent 12+; [CARD] reckoning: N damage, M poison stacks (Reckoning logs the stack count every time since its scaling isn't binary). CARD_EFFECT_TEXT gained entries for all three. Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html: Communion (cost 0, type unchanged) took soul 3 to 5 in one play; Purge dealt exactly 6 against a clean enemy and exactly 10 against a 4-poison enemy, each with the matching log line; Reckoning dealt exactly 11 with 4 stacks and exactly 3 with 0 stacks; playing Censer (4 poison) then Reckoning in the same simulated turn dealt exactly 11, confirming Reckoning reads stacks applied earlier in the same turn rather than a stale value; Interdict granted exactly 5 block at intent 8 and exactly 10 at intent 12; all eleven cardPool ids (including purge/interdict/reckoning) were present with no exclusion list. Zero JS errors across all checks. Build stamp now STAGE 1.11 / BUILD 052. Not yet eyeballed in a real browser. Poison decay model, the mod pool, calculateDamage() itself, damage source tagging, the die action panel, the card reward screen's own flow logic, and all seven BUILD 051 cards other than Communion were not touched — re-verified by re-reading every modified index.html section after editing.
Mid-CARD_PHASE victory fix (BUILD 053). Root cause: killing the enemy with a card only ever lowered gameState.enemy.hp — no phase transition happens while cards are being played, so the win/loss guard at the top of runPhase() (which runs before any phase logic on every transition, per BUILD 013) never got a chance to see hp <= 0 until End Turn eventually drove the chain through ENEMY_ACT_PHASE/CHECK_WIN_LOSS, letting further cards be played into a dead enemy in the meantime. Fix: playCard(), immediately after its existing '[CARD] played ...' log line, now checks gameState.run.status === 'active' && gameState.enemy.hp <= 0 and, if true, calls runPhase(gameState.turn.phase) — i.e. re-enters runPhase with whatever phase is already current (CARD_PHASE). This is not a second victory path: it is the identical runPhase() guard CHECK_WIN_LOSS already relies on, which checks enemy.hp before doing anything else, sets run.status to 'win', logs '[WIN] enemy defeated', calls openDieActionScreen() (the same function the existing win path calls, so the card reward screen still follows afterward via its own existing chain), and returns immediately without touching turn.phase or running any phase-specific logic — so CARD_PHASE's own logic never re-fires. Exactly-once firing needed no new guard: the check is gated on run.status === 'active', and runPhase()'s own guard is gated on the same field, so once status flips to 'win' any later call (from End Turn, another card, or a second copy of this same check) is a no-op by construction — verified directly (see below). No loss check was added here, per the prompt (the player cannot take damage during CARD_PHASE). Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html: reducing enemy hp to 4 and playing a 5-damage Strike immediately logged exactly one '[WIN] enemy defeated' line, left enemy.hp at -1 (not a larger negative), set run.status to 'win', and opened the die action panel (dieActionStep non-null) — all without End Turn being clicked; the hand-card buttons rendered by renderCardButtons() were confirmed disabled (btn.disabled = !active, pre-existing logic, unchanged) once run.status left 'active', and endTurnBtn.disabled read true (pre-existing !(status === 'active' && ...) condition, unchanged) — matching the same halt mechanism already used for the loss case, not a new guard; calling playCard() a second time directly (bypassing the disabled button, which a real player cannot do) still executed the card's own effect since playCard()'s phase/soul checks are unchanged by this prompt, but did NOT produce a second '[WIN]' line or reopen/re-trigger anything, confirming the victory path itself only ever fires once — flagged here since it means the exactly-once guarantee lives in runPhase()'s status check, not in playCard() refusing the play outright, which is consistent with how the pre-existing loss halt already worked (button-disable only, no function-level status guard) and was not something this prompt asked to add. A standalone call to runPhase('CHECK_WIN_LOSS') with enemy.hp at 0 (unrelated to any card) was also re-verified to still log exactly one '[WIN]' line, confirming the existing End Turn / ENEMY_ACT_PHASE / poison win path is untouched. Zero JS errors across all checks. Build stamp now STAGE 1.11 / BUILD 053. Not yet eyeballed in a real browser. The loss path, the phase machine's ordering, the poison decay model, the mod pool, calculateDamage(), the die action panel's own contents, the card reward screen, and every card definition were not touched — re-verified by re-reading every modified index.html section after editing.
Logging fix, not a new substage — card reward count and Nat 1/20 not-implemented lines (BUILD 054). Two independent fixes, both log-only. (1) cardRewardSkip() and cardRewardPickCard() previously logged gameState.player.deck.length — the current fight's draw pile, which fluctuates with draws/discards and is a different number from the true collection size — instead of gameState.player.ownedCards.length, the persistent collection every future card build is (per the prompt) verified against. Both now read ownedCards.length; cardRewardPickCard()'s line is logged after its existing updatePlayer() call so it reads the post-add length. No change to what's written to either field — deck and ownedCards are still updated exactly as before; only which field the log line reads from changed. (2) resolvePlayerRoll()'s NAT_TWENTY and NAT_ONE branches, and resolveEnemyRoll()'s ENEMY_NAT_ONE branch, called callListeners() for a hook with no registered listener (onNatTwenty/onNatOne on the Ordained class are stub functions, never passed to registerListener()) and produced no log output at all — a rolled face that did nothing was indistinguishable from one that never ran. Each now also logs a not-implemented line immediately after its callListeners() call: '[ROLL] Nat 20 not yet implemented', '[ROLL] Nat 1 not yet implemented', '[ENEMY] Nat 1 not yet implemented' — matching the existing '[ENEMY] Nat 20 not yet implemented' line (ENEMY_NAT_TWENTY's branch) in phrasing ("Nat N not yet implemented") and left completely untouched, per the prompt. INTERPRETATION FLAG: the prompt asked the two new player-side lines to match ENEMY_NAT_TWENTY's line "in style and prefix" — read literally that would mean reusing the bracket tag '[ENEMY]' for a player-rolled face, which would be semantically backwards (there is no existing '[PLAYER]' tag anywhere in LOG_PREFIX_CLASSES or the codebase). Instead, '[ROLL]' was used for the two player lines — the same tag resolvePlayerRoll() already uses one line above for its own face-announcement, exactly mirroring how '[ENEMY]' is the general enemy-event tag used one line above ENEMY_NAT_TWENTY's branch inside resolveEnemyRoll() (which itself logs under '[ENEMY ROLL]'). This preserves "style" (identical phrasing, logged as a follow-up line under the side's own established general-event tag) without misattributing a player event to '[ENEMY]' or inventing a new CSS log class beyond what the prompt (log lines only) asked for. No behaviour was added to any of the four branches — callListeners() is still called for NAT_TWENTY/NAT_ONE/ENEMY_NAT_ONE exactly as before (still a no-op, since no listener is registered), and ENEMY_NAT_TWENTY's own existing line and behaviour were not touched at all. Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html: seeding ownedCards to 13 entries and picking a reward logged 'added Censure to deck, deck now 14 cards' (not the draw-pile figure); skipping with ownedCards at 14 logged 'skipped, deck still 14 cards'; forcing resolvePlayerRoll() with face 20/modId NAT_TWENTY logged '[ROLL] Nat 20 not yet implemented'; face 1/NAT_ONE logged '[ROLL] Nat 1 not yet implemented'; forcing resolveEnemyRoll() with face 1/ENEMY_NAT_ONE logged '[ENEMY] Nat 1 not yet implemented'; face 20/ENEMY_NAT_TWENTY still logged exactly its pre-existing '[ENEMY] Nat 20 not yet implemented' line, unchanged; player.hp and enemy.hp were confirmed unchanged across all four Nat rolls, confirming no behaviour was added to any face. Zero JS errors. Build stamp now STAGE 1.11 / BUILD 054. Not yet eyeballed in a real browser. ownedCards itself, the reward pool draw, the card reward screen's own flow/behaviour, the roll system beyond these four new log lines, the blank passive, and every card and mod were not touched — re-verified by re-reading every modified index.html section after editing.
Stage 1.11 substage 4 — tier 4, Retribution (BUILD 055). Last of the four state-reading cards flagged after BUILD 051; the anti-passivity hybrid. Retribution (1 soul, attack) reads gameState.player.block live at the moment it is played — exactly the same live-read pattern Purge and Reckoning already established for poison stacks — computes rawDamage = min(block, 12) (capped at 12, a stated taste call: uncapped, a Consecrate turn reaches the mid-20s to mid-30s off one 1-soul card, trivialising the current test enemy while going the opposite direction — irrelevant — against later, larger enemy HP pools, since block numbers stay fixed while enemy HP is the axis that scales across acts), then routes that through calculateDamage(rawDamage, 'attack') (Law 1, same 'attack' tagging every other damage card uses) and applies it to enemy.hp via updateEnemy, firing ON_DAMAGE_DEALT with source: 'retribution' exactly like every other attack card. Registered in config.cards and referenced (same object, not a copy) into config.cardPool alongside the other eleven, with no exclusion list — getCard()/drawCards()/playCard() and the reward screen's Object.keys(config.cardPool) draw needed no changes. Logs distinguish the capped and uncapped cases exactly as specified: '[CARD] retribution: N damage (block M)' when block <= 12 (the cap wasn't the binding constraint), and '[CARD] retribution: 12 damage (capped, block M)' whenever block > 12. CARD_EFFECT_TEXT gained a 'damage = block, capped at 12' entry. Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html: at 0 block, dealt 0 damage, logged uncapped; playing one Ward (5 block) then Retribution dealt exactly 5, logged uncapped; two Wards (10 block) then Retribution dealt exactly 10, logged uncapped; force-triggering Sanctuary (16 block) then playing Retribution dealt exactly 12, logged with the capped variant naming the true 16 block it was read from; retribution appeared in config.cardPool alongside all eleven existing ids with no exclusion list. Zero JS errors across all five scenarios. Build stamp now STAGE 1.11 / BUILD 055. Not yet eyeballed in a real browser. calculateDamage() itself, the block-granting path every other card/mod uses (generateBlock(), Ward/Vestment/Litany/Interdict/Sanctuary/Vigil/Penance/consecrate's own block-per-card listener), the poison decay model, the die action panel, the card reward screen, and all eleven existing cards were not touched — re-verified by re-reading every modified index.html section after editing.
Stage 2.8 — run-outcome guards on the two remaining unguarded dev paths (BUILD 077). enterSlot() — the function every real "current node" click (opening, lane, boss) dispatches through — now returns immediately if gameState.run.outcome !== 'active', the same one-line guard devJumpToSlot() already had; this closes the gap BUILD 076 reported but left open ("the genuinely-current node's own real click path... has no run.outcome/status guard"). devRestartFightBtn's click handler gained the identical status/outcome guard BUILD 076 gave Skip to Die Action, and its refreshInspector() disabled line now reads !(screen==='fight' && status==='active' && outcome==='active') instead of just screen!=='fight', so it visibly disables rather than silently no-opping once a run is won or lost. Traced, not fixed: dev-jumping away from an unresolved rite (Heal/Die-action panel still open) leaves it stuck on screen — root cause is riteStep, a module-level variable outside gameState, which openRiteScreen()/closeRiteScreen() set but devJumpToSlot() never touches; renderRiteScreen() renders purely off riteStep with no check against run.currentSlot or run.screen, so the panel keeps rendering (and its buttons stay live) after the player has jumped to and even won an unrelated fight. Side effect also traced: endTurnBtn's disabled condition requires riteStep === null, so it stays disabled for the rest of the run once this happens. Smallest fix identified, not applied: reset riteStep (and, for the same reason, dieActionStep/cardRewardStep) to null at the top of devJumpToSlot(), so refreshInspector() dismisses any transient panel the moment a dev-jump fires. Verified via node --check on the extracted inline scripts (zero syntax errors) — browser tooling (headless or windowed) was unavailable this session, so this build is unverified in an actual browser; both guard fixes and the rite trace are code-read/reasoned, not click-tested. Build stamp now STAGE 2.8 / BUILD 077.
Stage 2.9 — the riteStep dev-jump fix identified in BUILD 077's trace, applied (BUILD 078). devJumpToSlot(), immediately after its existing run.outcome guard, now sets riteStep = null; dieActionStep = null; cardRewardStep = null before doing anything else — these three module-level (non-gameState) flow variables are what refreshInspector()'s render* functions gate on to show the rite panel / die action panel / card reward panel, and none of them were ever touched by a dev-jump before this. A jump away from any unresolved panel now dismisses it (refreshInspector() runs later in the same function via updateRun()) instead of leaving it stuck rendered and clickable, and endTurnBtn no longer stays permanently disabled afterward (its guard requires riteStep === null among others). Nothing else in devJumpToSlot() was touched. Verified via node --check on the extracted inline scripts (zero syntax errors) — browser tooling (headless or windowed) was unavailable this session, so this build is unverified by an actual run/click-through, same caveat as BUILD 077. Build stamp now STAGE 2.9 / BUILD 078.
Stage 2.10 — the die action screen's own twenty-row die becomes the Load/Strengthen face picker, replacing both separate face-button screens (BUILD 079). renderDieList() gained an optional 4th arg, pickConfig ({ isEligible(face), onPick(faceNumber), showBecomes }) — every existing caller (fight screen, map previews) passes nothing and renders exactly as before, byte-for-byte; only renderDieActionPanel()'s own call site now passes it, and only for the load_pick_face/strengthen_pick_face steps. Eligibility filters are unchanged copies of what each deleted button loop used (Load: f.modId === null; Strengthen: face 1 never, face 20 always, otherwise f.modId !== null) — no eligibility rule changed. showDiePreview is now also true for load_pick_face (previously only load_pick_mod/strengthen_pick_face showed the die), so the die is visible throughout both pick steps, not just appearing after landing on Strengthen. Eligible rows get .die-row-pickable (cursor:pointer, outline: 1px solid var(--nat), hover background var(--nat)/#1a1500 text — same --nat "pick me" colour and hover pair .die-action-row button and .map-node-current already use) and a click listener calling the same dieActionPickLoadFace()/dieActionPickStrengthenFace() functions the old buttons called, unchanged; ineligible rows get .die-row-pick-inert (opacity 0.45, same figure .map-node-inert already uses). outline (not border/padding) so an eligible row's box never changes size — confirmed nothing shifts when the picker state or its hover-tip appears, since .hover-tip was already position:absolute. During Strengthen only (showBecomes), an eligible row's existing BUILD 071 hover-tip gains a second line built by calling the exact same faceHoverText() function against a cloned face object with weight: face.weight + 1 — the identical +1 arithmetic dieActionPickStrengthenFace() itself applies — so the "becomes" line is the same MOD_DESCRIPTION/NAT_DESCRIPTION string and the same ×N suffix notation the row's own hover and weight badge already produce, no authored copy added; Load's picker shows no becomes line (showBecomes false), matching the prompt's scope. Strengthen's header corrected from "Choose a loaded face to strengthen" (never true — face 20 has always been offered despite carrying no mod) to "Choose a face to strengthen"; Load's own header ("Choose a blank face for <mod>") needed no fix, already accurate. Verified via a live jsdom drive of the real index.html (not just node --check, since browser tooling was unavailable this session but jsdom lets the actual DOM/click/eventing run): forced a win, clicked Load, picked an offered mod (Sanctuary), confirmed the separate button row was empty (0 children) and the 20-row die was the only interactive surface, confirmed face 1's row carried die-row-pick-inert (not pickable) while a blank row carried die-row-pickable, clicked that row and confirmed the mod landed on the correct face number and the screen advanced straight to the card reward step (dieActionStep null, cardRewardStep 'choose') — one screen, no intermediate face-button screen at any point; separately opened Strengthen, confirmed the header read "Choose a face to strengthen", confirmed face 20 carried die-row-pickable and face 1 carried die-row-pick-inert, read face 20's hover-tip and got exactly two lines — the existing NAT_TWENTY description, then the same description suffixed (×2) — confirmed weight went 1→2 on click. Zero JS errors across the whole run. Dev tools active/off question re-confirmed unchanged (no flag exists; nothing player-facing to turn off) — not modified, per the prompt's "report, do not change." Mod/card pools, roll and weight mathematics beyond the pre-existing +1 strengthen line (untouched), the map, run state, dev-jump, and the fight phase order were not touched — re-verified by re-reading every modified index.html section after editing. Build stamp now STAGE 2.10 / BUILD 079.
Stage 2.11 — a presentation pass on the BUILD 079 die-row picker fixing three browser-observed bugs (BUILD 080), CSS only. Root cause of all three, found by measuring the box model before changing anything (the BUILD 040/041 habit): .die-row-pickable used outline: 1px solid var(--nat) with outline-offset: 2px, which paints up to 3px OUTSIDE the row's own border box on every edge, regardless of offset sign. With rows stacked flush (zero gap between them), two adjacent eligible rows' outlines each sat their own 2-3px outside their box rather than meeting at the shared boundary, reading as the reported "broken grid of doubled lines"; the same overhang was also liable to be clipped by an ancestor's overflow on the right edge, which is what made eligible rows measure differently in the browser from inert/loaded/nat-twenty rows that never painted outside their box at all — one root cause explains all three symptoms. Fix: .die-row-pickable now uses box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 1px var(--nat) — box-shadow, unlike outline, is never drawn outside the box it's set on, so it reads as one continuous frame between adjacent eligible rows and can never be clipped short of a neighbour; like outline it doesn't affect the box model (box-sizing: border-box is set globally at the top of the stylesheet), so it changes nothing about row width — this alone satisfies "all twenty rows share one width, identical to the fight screen's die rows" since nothing distinguishing pickable/inert/loaded/nat-twenty rows ever touched box dimensions in the first place. Separately, the hover rule's own bug: .die-row-pickable:hover previously set background: var(--nat) and tried to recolour .die-mod/.face-btn/.die-weight to #1a1500, but .loaded and .nat-twenty rows each carry a higher-specificity colour rule (e.g. .die-col.player-die .die-row.loaded .die-mod, .die-row.nat-twenty .die-mod — 4-5 class selectors vs. this hover rule's 3) that painted straight back over the override; on face 20 specifically that's --nat text rendering on a --nat fill, i.e. the exact "mod name disappears into it" reported. Fixed by removing the text-colour override entirely rather than winning the specificity fight — hover now applies background: rgba(251, 191, 36, 0.22) (the same rgba-of-a-css-variable technique the pre-existing fx-pulse-blue keyframes already use for --player-mod, just static and low-alpha instead of animated — --nat's hex fbbf24 is 251,191,36) plus a heavier box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 2px var(--nat), both still fully inside the box — a row's own text is now exactly as readable hovered as at rest regardless of which colour family (--nat/--player-mod/--blank) it belongs to, and the ×N badge is never crowded toward the edge since nothing paints outside the row. No new palette; no eligibility, flow, or weight-arithmetic code touched — renderDieList()/renderDieActionPanel()'s JS is byte-for-byte unchanged from BUILD 079. Verified: node --check on the extracted scripts (zero syntax errors) plus a re-run of the same live jsdom drive used for BUILD 079 (win → Load → pick mod → click blank die row → mod lands, advances to card reward; Strengthen → face 20 pickable, two-line hover unchanged, weight 1→2; face 1 unpickable in both) — identical results to BUILD 079, confirming the CSS-only change didn't touch behaviour, with zero JS errors. The actual visual fix (doubled borders, hover contrast, row widths) could not be confirmed by eye — browser tooling (headless or windowed) was unavailable this session, so this build's CSS correctness rests on box-model reasoning from the stylesheet's own numbers, not a rendered screenshot. Build stamp now STAGE 2.11 / BUILD 080.
Stage 2.12 — widened the Load/Strengthen picker's die rows to stop mod-name/badge clipping (BUILD 081), one CSS rule. Browser tooling was available this session after all (standalone Playwright/Chromium via npm, distinct from the claude-in-chrome extension declined in an earlier session) and was used to actually measure rather than guess, per the prompt: canvas measureText() against the die rows' own computed font (16px Consolas monospace) found Consecrate — the longest name across every mod ever loadable onto a player face (Smite, Penance, Offering, Blight, Virulence, Sanctuary, Vigil, Zeal, Fervour, Ordain, Consecrate) — renders at ~88px, and the widest realistic two-digit ×N weight badge (×10 through ×99) at ~26px, with .die-mod-wrap's own pre-existing 8px gap between them: ~122px needed just to stop clipping. getBoundingClientRect() on a real render (forcing a win, opening Strengthen, reading the live Consecrate row on face 10) found #dieActionDieList was actually only ~134px wide despite its CSS already declaring max-width: 260px/width: 100% — the ceiling was never reached because .die-action-die-preview/#dieActionPanel are both auto-sized flex contexts, so the container was shrink-wrapping to its bare content (face-btn 34 + row gap 12 + an ~88px mod-wrap with zero room for a badge) rather than growing toward 260px; explained exactly why "Consecrate" sat flush against the row's own right edge. Fix: added min-width: 220px to #dieActionDieList's existing rule (covers the ~122px content need plus visible clear space) — min-width is an absolute floor flex layout must honour regardless of the shrink-to-fit ambiguity that made width/max-width alone ineffective. Re-measured after the change (same live-render method, now also forcing face 10's weight to a synthetic ×12 to stress-test a two-digit badge alongside the longest name): mod name and badge both fully inside the row with margin to spare, zero clipping against either the row or the container, and all twenty rows in the list measured exactly 220px wide (uniform, confirmed programmatically, not eyeballed). #playerDieList/#enemyDieList (fight screen) and #mapPlayerDieList/#bossDiePreviewList (map) are each their own separate ID selector, unrelated to #dieActionDieList's rule — grep-confirmed neither shares it — so per the prompt's own conditional ("unaffected unless they share the rule") they were correctly left untouched; no consistency edit was needed anywhere else. Re-ran the exact same jsdom drive used for BUILD 079/080 afterward and got byte-for-byte identical results (Load/Strengthen flow, eligibility, the two-line Strengthen hover) with zero JS errors, confirming the CSS-only change touched no behaviour. Build stamp now STAGE 2.12 / BUILD 081.
Stage 2.13 — the act grows from three lane slots to five, and the elite grants two die actions instead of one (BUILD 082), a run-structure change with no new slot types or systems. Confirmed and reported first, per the prompt: before this build the elite was mechanically identical to a normal fight — SLOT_HANDLERS.fight dispatches by slot.type only, never by slot.label, so an elite's win ran through the exact same openDieActionScreen()->closeDieActionScreen()->openCardRewardScreen() chain as any other fight, granting exactly one die action and one card reward, nothing more. buildAct() rebuilt to eleven slots: opening (unchanged, 50 HP/4-12) -> fork -> upper [fight 70, rite, fight 85, elite 100/12-22, rite] -> lower [fight 70, rite, fight 78, fight 85, rite] -> boss (unchanged, 100 HP/16-28, hasDie true). Counting the opening as the run's first fight, the upper lane's elite lands as the run's fourth fight (opening, upper[0], upper[2], upper[3]-elite) exactly as specified; lower's own fourth fight (opening, lower[0], lower[2], lower[3]) is a plain fight, matching the prompt's shape (no lower-lane elite). Upper's two normal fights reuse the same 70/85 endpoints every prior build already used; lower's three normal fights needed a middle value the prompt didn't specify, so 78 was picked as a roughly-even step between 70 and 85 and flagged explicitly in the buildAct() comment as a taste call, not a formula. Elite reward: added a module-level dieActionsRemaining counter (declared beside the existing dieActionOrigin flow-state variable) — set to 2 for an elite win, 1 for every other reward-origin win, immediately before the single call site that opens the panel on a real fight win (runPhase()'s top-of-function win guard, reading the just-won slot's own label off gameState.run.currentSlot/gameState.run.act). closeDieActionScreen() — already the single choke point every Load/Strengthen/Skip choice funnels through — now decrements this counter on the 'reward' path and, if any remain, reopens the same panel instead of proceeding to the card reward; a rite's die-action choice (origin 'rite') returns before ever touching the counter, so rites stay exactly one action, unaffected. The dev Skip-to-Die-Action shortcut explicitly resets the counter to 1 before opening, so a stale 2 left over from a prior elite win can never leak into it. Also found and fixed in the same build, required for five-slot lanes to work at all: advanceRun()'s slot-advancement branch had a hardcoded `cs.index < 2 ? nextIndex : 'boss'`, correct only for the old three-slot lane (indices 0-2) — a five-slot lane would have jumped to the boss after just its third slot. Now reads `cs.index < laneArr.length - 1`, so it never needs touching again if a lane's length ever changes. Map rendering (renderMapScreen()) and devJumpToSlot() were both already fully dynamic (forEach over gameState.run.act[lane], no hardcoded length or index assumptions anywhere in either) — grep-confirmed the only other hardcoded lane-length assumption in the whole file was the one just fixed — so eleven nodes render and dev-jump reaches every one of them with no further code changes. Verified via a full headless jsdom drive of the actual index.html playing BOTH lanes start to finish: confirmed the freshly-built act's shape (5/5 slot lengths, correct type/label sequence and HP per slot, opening/boss both unchanged) programmatically; played the entire upper lane through the real click path (enterSlot, not dev-jump) — opening granted 1 die action, upper[0] 1, upper[2] 1, upper[3] (the elite) exactly 2, each rite resolved via Heal — and confirmed currentSlot became 'boss' after upper's fifth slot, not early; then dev-jumped through the entire lower lane confirming lower[2]/lower[3] each grant exactly 1 die action (not elite-doubled) and again correctly reached 'boss' after its fifth slot; finally won the boss and confirmed run.outcome ends 'won'. Zero JS errors across the whole run. Re-ran the standing die-action-picker jsdom drive (BUILD 079-081's own test) afterward and got byte-for-byte identical Load/Strengthen results, confirming this build's changes (all in buildAct(), advanceRun(), the die-action-screen close/open functions, and the win-guard in runPhase()) never touched the picker, eligibility, weight arithmetic, enemy dice, or the card/mod pools. Additionally — unlike BUILD 077 through 080, which shipped on code-reasoning/jsdom alone — this build's map was actually eyeballed via a real Playwright/Chromium screenshot at both 1400x900 and 1600x1080 (after winning the opening and landing back on the fork, so both full 5-slot lanes are visible at once): 13 .map-node elements render (Start, opening, 5 upper, 5 lower, boss — the "eleven" in the prompt counts only the 5+5 lane slots plus boss, matching), composition measured centred in the panel to within 0px at both widths, no horizontal overflow at either, connections/fork/merge lines legible, boss preview and both dice unchanged and rendering correctly. Build stamp now STAGE 2.13 / BUILD 082.
Stage 2.14 — every piece of dev chrome behind one toggle, closed by default, and the BUILD 036/037 setup pause made optional (BUILD 083). Dev chrome only, per the prompt's own framing — no game logic and no numbers were touched; the one player-visible change is that a fight now starts rolling without a dev click. The toggle: a single new #devChromeToggleBtn sits directly above the State Inspector toggle inside .controls-row and reuses that toggle's exact pattern rather than inventing a second one — a container hidden by CSS (#devChrome { display: none }), an .expanded class that shows it, one click handler that calls classList.toggle('expanded') and retitles the button (Dev Tools ▸ / Dev Tools ▾). The four dev blocks that used to be direct children of .controls-row — #inspector, #devStepControl (Next Phase, Begin Roll, Skip to Die Action, Skip to Card Reward, Restart Fight), #devModLoader and #devPoisonApplier — are now children of #devChrome, which is flex-column with the same 8px gap .controls-row itself uses, so every bar sits exactly where it sat before when the container is open. Closed on every page load: the markup carries no .expanded, and init() additionally resets the container class, the button label, the checkbox and both flags, so a browser restoring form state across a soft reload cannot desync the checkbox from the flag behind it. The setup pause: a new #devPauseBeforeRollCheckbox ("Pause before first roll", in its own DEV SETUP bar inside the dev chrome) is unchecked by default and is the only writer of devPauseBeforeFirstRoll, which is read in exactly one place — startFreshTurnPaused(), still the one shared entry path BUILD 037 established, now gated rather than forked. That function still calls runPhase('START_OF_TURN') unconditionally and then, when the box is unchecked, makes the identical autoAdvance() call #devBeginRollBtn's own handler makes, so an unpaused entry and a paused entry followed by Begin Roll execute the same code in the same order; checked, it stops after START_OF_TURN and waits for Begin Roll exactly as BUILD 037 did. Because beginFightFromSlot() is the sole caller besides Restart Fight, this covers init(), New Run, every map slot entry and Restart Fight through one line. ROLL_PHASE timing is untouched — ROLL_PHASE_PAUSE_MS is still 1800 and autoAdvance()'s own wait still arms exactly as before. The two dev inputs that live outside the container and therefore cannot be hidden with it go inert instead, both reading devChromeOpen: force-roll on the twenty die face rows (renderDieList() no longer wires the click at all while closed, and forcePlayerRoll()/forceEnemyRoll() carry a matching early return covering any other caller), and the BUILD 076 map dev-jump nodes (one added clause on attachDevJumpIfEligible()'s existing guard, so a closed dev chrome renders them fully disabled the same way a won or lost run already does — not a second code path). The toggle handler ends with one refreshInspector() so both re-render with their dev wiring attached or removed. The BUILD 079 Load/Strengthen die-row picker is untouched and unaffected: it is player-facing, passes forceRollFn null, and wires its click on the row via pickConfig, never on the face button. Verified two ways in headless jsdom against the real index.html, both with zero console errors: (1) a 29-check drive of every success criterion — fresh load shows the stamp, both combat panels, the middle strip, the hand, New Run/End Turn and one dev control with all five dev bars hidden; the opening fight entered by the real click path leaves START_OF_TURN unaided and resolves exactly one [ROLL] line; a face click and a direct forcePlayerRoll() during ROLL_PHASE both add nothing to the log; every non-current map node renders disabled with no dev-jump wiring; opening the toggle reveals all five bars with the checkbox unchecked and re-enables the dev-jump nodes; ticking the box and clicking Restart Fight stops at START_OF_TURN until Begin Roll, after which a forced face click produces a single [ROLL] and no second roll follows; unticking and restarting rolls immediately; closing hides everything and re-inerts the outside inputs; and a won fight still opens the die action panel whose die rows still strengthen a face with the chrome closed. (2) A full run driven start to finish with the chrome closed and Begin Roll never clicked — 21 rolls resolved across two slots to a natural loss, with the fight never once stalling in START_OF_TURN. The whole diff removes 11 lines: the build stamp, two guard conditions widened, and startFreshTurnPaused()'s now-outdated doc comment.
Stage 2.15 — Nat 1 fires once per fight, and Penitence lasts 3 turns instead of the rest of the fight (BUILD 084). Both rulings came from BUILD 082's played run, where a boss fight rolled two Nat 1s and the second was a dead roll — a log line and nothing else. Once per fight: two new fight-scoped fields sit on gameState.player alongside penitenceActive — natOneFiredThisFight (false) and penitenceTurnsRemaining (0) — and both reset in clearFightScopedState(), the shared reset resetFight() calls, in the same updatePlayer() call that already cleared poisonStacks and penitenceActive; startNewRun() clears them in its own updatePlayer() for the same reason. onNatOne() now checks natOneFiredThisFight first: if true it calls callListeners('BLANK_ROLL', {}) and returns, which is the identical dispatch resolvePlayerRoll() makes for a genuinely blank face — same hook, same ordained_blank_passive, same 2 block, same [BLANK] 2 block generated line, no distinguishing tag, so in the log a spent face 1 reads exactly like any other blank; if false it sets all three fields in one updatePlayer() and logs the onset line. The guard reads natOneFiredThisFight and deliberately NOT penitenceActive: now that Penitence expires, checking the old flag would let a face 1 rolled after expiry re-arm it from scratch, which is the stacking the ruling removes. BUILD 067's '[ROLL] Penitence already active' line is gone with the branch it belonged to. A spent Nat 1 is a blank to CARDS as well, not only in its effect and its log line: resolvePlayerRoll()'s rollOutcome assignment for NAT_ONE became gameState.player.natOneFiredThisFight ? 'blank' : 'nat_one'. Ordering is what makes that one-liner correct — natOneFiredThisFight is still false when rollOutcome is computed on the fight's first face 1, because onNatOne() sets it later during the dispatch, so it reads 'nat_one' exactly once per fight and 'blank' for every face 1 after, matching onNatOne()'s own branch one for one. Orison is the card this exists for: it reads gameState.turn.rollOutcome directly and would otherwise take its 5-damage branch on a face 1 the player has just watched pay out 2 block. Nothing else in the file reads the string 'nat_one' — the assignment was its only occurrence — so this changed Orison and nothing more. Three turns: a new module-level const PENITENCE_TURNS = 3 (named, not inlined, because onNatOne() sets the counter from it and the START_OF_TURN tick decrements it, and the two must not drift) is written into penitenceTurnsRemaining at onset. The START_OF_TURN tick is the only place the counter moves: inside the existing if (penitenceActive) branch, after the unchanged deduction (still 1 soul, still Math.max(0, soul - 1), still immediately after the soul reset, still in the same position in START_OF_TURN), it decrements the counter and, when that hits 0, clears penitenceActive and logs [START] Penitence expires once. Because onset happens in ROLL_PHASE — after that turn's tick has already run — the three ticks land on the three START_OF_TURNs following onset, the third both deducts and clears, and the fourth never enters the branch. natOneFiredThisFight is untouched by expiry, so face 1 keeps rolling as a blank for the rest of the fight exactly as the ruling says. Two strings were reworded to stop them stating a duration the game no longer has: the onset log line and NAT_DESCRIPTION.NAT_ONE (the player-facing face-1 hover), both now reading "for the next 3 turns" — flagged rather than silent, since the prompt asked for the onset line to log as it currently does and this is the one place that was not taken literally. Verified in real headless Chromium (Playwright, full chromium build, index.html over file://), 20 checks, zero console errors and zero page errors, plus a screenshot: the stamp reads STAGE 2.15 / BUILD 084; the dev chrome opens; the first face 1 logs one onset line, sets all three fields, fires no blank passive and records rollOutcome 'nat_one'; tick 1 takes soul 3 to 2; a second face 1 in the same fight logs a plain [BLANK] 2 block generated with no onset line, no "already active" line and no Nat 1 tag, grants 2 block, leaves the counter at 2, and flips rollOutcome to 'blank'; Orison played on that second roll deals 9, not 5, and logs [CARD] orison: 9 damage, blank roll (the same card played on the FIRST face 1 correctly deals 5, checked separately in jsdom); tick 2 leaves the counter at 1 with no expiry line; turn 4 deducts a third time, clears the flag and logs the expiry line exactly once while natOneFiredThisFight survives; turn 5 deducts nothing, logs nothing, and a face 1 rolled there still comes out a blank that never re-arms, for three ticks total across the fight; Restart Fight resets both flags and a fresh face 1 fires Penitence from scratch; and Nat 20 fires on three consecutive passes, three loaded faces each, in ascending face order (smite 5, consecrate 10, sanctuary 15) every time. Also re-run headless in jsdom: BUILD 084's own 16-check suite, a dedicated 7-check Orison suite covering both the 5 and the 9 branch, BUILD 083's 29-check dev-chrome drive (all still passing bar its own build-stamp assertion, which now correctly reads 2.15/084), and a full run start to finish to a natural loss with no errors.
Stage 2.16 — the boss intent band drops from 16-28 to 10-20, average 15 (BUILD 085). One number pair, in buildAct()'s boss slot only: intentMin 16->10, intentMax 28->20. Motivated by three full played runs all dying to the boss on arithmetic — roughly 15 points of cards a turn spent as either damage or block, never both, against a boss averaging 22 under the old band; Fergus's call was to move the band to 10-20 (average 15) and retune after play, not to touch the boss's die, its faces, or its 100 HP. Confirmed both downstream reads already go through the slot config with no second literal to find: the map's boss preview (renderMapScreen(), 'Intent ' + bossEnemy.intentMin + '-' + bossEnemy.intentMax) reads gameState.run.act.boss.enemy directly, and the in-fight roll (inline in runPhase()'s START_OF_TURN branch: Math.floor(Math.random() * (gameState.enemy.intentMax - gameState.enemy.intentMin + 1)) + gameState.enemy.intentMin) reads gameState.enemy, which beginFightFromSlot() populates from slot.enemy.intentMin/intentMax on every fight entry — no hardcoded 16 or 28 existed anywhere else in the file (grep-confirmed), so this was a single-site data change with two already-correct readers. Found and fixed a stale comment this build's own history left behind: the multi-line comment directly above buildAct() (added when the five-slot lane structure landed) still read "boss 16-28 — unchanged this build" and had not been updated when the intentMin/intentMax values themselves were already changed to 10/20 earlier in this same session's edit history — corrected to state the new band and the reasoning, so the comment and the data it describes no longer disagree. Re-read buildAct() and its surrounding comment, the map preview site, beginFightFromSlot(), and the intent-roll formula after editing to confirm the change landed and nothing else moved: opening 50 HP/4-12, normals 70/78/85 at 6-18, elite 100 HP/12-22, boss 100 HP/10-20/hasDie true — all byte-identical to BUILD 084 except the one number pair. node --check equivalent (extracted inline script parsed via `new Function()`) passed with zero syntax errors. Browser tooling (Playwright/Chromium) was not available this session, so the map preview and in-fight roll were not eyeballed in an actual rendered browser; instead, the exact roll formula and exact numeric bands from the file (boss 10-20, elite 12-22, normal 6-18, opening 4-12) were reproduced verbatim in a standalone Node script and run 10,000 times per band with zero out-of-range results, confirming the arithmetic guarantee rather than a click-through. Build stamp now STAGE 2.16 / BUILD 085.

Stage 2.17 — the elite intent band drops from 12-22 to 10-18, average 14 (BUILD 086). One number pair, same shape as BUILD 085: in buildAct()'s upper-lane elite slot only, intentMin 12->10, intentMax 22->18. Motivated by BUILD 085 leaving the run's climb backwards — moving the boss to 10-20 (average 15) put it below the elite's still-12-22 (average 17), so the mid-run fight threatened harder on paper than the final one; Fergus's call was 10-18 for the elite (average 14), landing it between normal fights' 12 and the boss's 15, restoring the intended climb. Confirmed there is no separate elite preview to route: grep across the file found only one rendered intent preview (#bossPreview, boss-only, added before BUILD 085) — no equivalent elite panel exists on the map, so there was no second literal to find or fix, unlike the boss/map-preview check BUILD 085 did. The in-fight roll (the same inline formula in runPhase()'s START_OF_TURN branch BUILD 085 already confirmed: Math.floor(Math.random() * (gameState.enemy.intentMax - gameState.enemy.intentMin + 1)) + gameState.enemy.intentMin) is untouched and reads gameState.enemy, which beginFightFromSlot() still populates from slot.enemy.intentMin/intentMax on every fight entry — so this build only needed the one buildAct() edit. Updated the same multi-line comment above buildAct() that BUILD 085 had already brought current for the boss, now also stating the elite's new band and the reasoning (kept below the boss's 15, above normal fights' 12, correcting BUILD 085's inversion) rather than leaving it to go stale the way it briefly did last build. Re-read buildAct() and its comment, and grep-confirmed dieActionsRemaining (elite's two-die-action reward, gated on wonSlot.label === 'Elite') and the elite's HP/position (100 HP, fourth slot in the upper lane) are untouched — the elite object's only changed fields are intentMin and intentMax. node --check equivalent (new Function() over the extracted inline script) passed with zero syntax errors. Browser tooling (Playwright/Chromium) was not available this session, so the elite was not dev-jumped and eyeballed in an actual rendered browser; instead, the exact roll formula and the exact four numeric bands as they appear in the file (boss 10-20, elite 10-18, normal 6-18, opening 4-12) were reproduced verbatim in a standalone Node script and rolled 10,000 times per band with zero out-of-range results. Build stamp now STAGE 2.17 / BUILD 086.

Stage 2.18 — New Run moved away from End Turn and gated behind a confirm when a run is live (BUILD 087). Presentation and one safety check only, per the prompt — no run logic changed. Motivated by Fergus's own playtest: New Run and End Turn sat side by side as visually identical buttons, and New Run discards the current run permanently (permadeath), so a misclick mid-run was unrecoverable. Moved #startGameBtn out of .controls-buttons (where it shared a row and a big-primary-action style with #endTurnBtn) into a new .top-bar div alongside the build stamp, at the very top of the page — different position, different row, different visual language entirely, not just a spacing tweak. Restyled it small (11px, 4px/10px padding, the same footprint as an ordinary dev button) with a red border and red text (#dc2626) rather than End Turn's 17px/16px-40px gold "primary action" look, and split the old shared `#startGameBtn, #endTurnBtn` CSS rule into two separate ones so End Turn's own styling is untouched. End Turn's own #endTurnBtn:disabled rule, its click handler, and its position/styling inside .controls-buttons were not touched at all. The safety check: startGameBtn's click handler now reads gameState.run.outcome first — the same field enterSlot()/devJumpToSlot() already gate on — and if it's 'active' (a run genuinely in progress) calls the native confirm('Abandon this run and start a new one?') before doing anything else, returning immediately (and logging '[CLICK] New Run — cancelled at confirm') if the player declines; only past that gate does it log '[CLICK] New Run' and call startNewRun() exactly as before. Chose confirm() over a two-click toggle per the prompt's own "whichever is less code" — one line, no new state, no second render pass to reason about. Once run.outcome is 'won' or 'lost' the check is skipped entirely and New Run fires in one click, unchanged from BUILD 086, since confirm's own && short-circuits before evaluating. startNewRun() itself, the phase machine, and every other piece of run state were not touched — the entire change is the one added conditional plus its two log lines. Re-read the new .top-bar markup and CSS, the trimmed .controls-buttons (End Turn now its only child), the split button rules, and the click handler after editing — all landed as intended. node --check equivalent (new Function() over the extracted inline script) passed with zero syntax errors. Browser tooling (Playwright/Chromium) was not available this session, so the actual confirm() dialog (cancel leaving HP/deck/die/run state untouched, accept resetting exactly as before, and the position/styling separation) was not eyeballed in a rendered browser — verified instead by reading the guard's logic directly against gameState.run.outcome's three possible values and confirming no other code path calls startNewRun() or touches the confirm. Build stamp now STAGE 2.18 / BUILD 087.

Stage 2.19 — a third rite option, Remove a card, alongside Heal and Take a die action (BUILD 088). Motivated by the reward flow only ever adding cards/mods and never removing, bloating the deck and diluting draws by the boss. riteStep gained a second non-null value, 'remove_pick_card', rendered inside the same #riteScreenPanel by renderRiteScreen() — same choose-then-advance shape dieActionStep already uses for its own load_pick_mod/strengthen_pick_face sub-steps, not a new pattern. Clicking the new "Remove a card" button (riteChooseRemoveCard()) does not itself commit the rite — it only switches the step, since a card still has to be picked; the picker renders gameState.player.ownedCards (the permanent collection, not the current fight's draw pile, since a rite is only ever reached from the map between fights) as one button per card, reusing the exact per-card shape (name + cost, CARD_EFFECT_TEXT hover-tip) renderCardRewardPanel() already builds. Rite (classRestriction: 'ordained') renders with btn.disabled = true per ruling 7 (unique class cards cannot be discarded) — a new .die-action-row button:disabled CSS rule (opacity 0.4, cursor not-allowed, hover reverted to rest state) makes this visible, not just inert; riteRemoveCard() also guards on card.classRestriction and returns early as the source of truth, not trusting the disabled attribute alone. Picking a real card (riteRemoveCard(index)) is the actual commit point, mirroring riteChooseHeal()'s single updatePlayer() call: splices that one index out of ownedCards, and removes the first matching instance of that same card id from deck/discard/hand if present, in one updatePlayer() call, then logs '[RITE] removed CardName from deck, deck now N cards' (N = ownedCards.length, matching the reward screen's own ownedCards-based count convention), calls closeRiteScreen(), and calls advanceRun() exactly as Heal and Take a die action both already do. No back button was added at any step, matching Heal/Take a die action's own irreversible-on-click behaviour — neither of those is reversible either, so there is nothing to be consistent with by adding one. Verified via a live jsdom drive of the actual index.html: dev-jumped to a rite slot, confirmed all three buttons render ("Heal 20 HP", "Take a die action", "Remove a card"), clicked Remove a card and confirmed the picker rendered all 10 owned cards with Rite's button disabled=true and every other button enabled, clicked a Strike button and confirmed ownedCards dropped from 10 to 9 (one specific Strike instance gone, the other four untouched), deck no longer contained the removed instance, riteStep returned to null, and the run advanced to the next slot (currentSlot moved from the rite to the following slot) — the same close-and-advance behaviour Heal already has. Zero JS errors. Heal and Take a die action's own functions/log lines/CSS were not touched — re-read after editing and confirmed byte-for-byte identical to BUILD 087. Not verified this session: real windowed-browser eyeballing (jsdom only, no Playwright/Chromium available) and a full subsequent fight actually drawing hand/discard across several turns to visually confirm the removed card never reappears — confirmed instead by direct inspection that the removed id is absent from ownedCards/deck/discard/hand immediately after removal, which is what every future draw/shuffle/reshuffle reads from. Build stamp now STAGE 2.19 / BUILD 088.

Stage 2.20 — the class card can now be removed at a rite; one restriction dropped (BUILD 089). Fergus's call: ruling 7 (unique class cards cannot be discarded) governs the in-fight discard mechanic — Shove, Crush, Take It, none of which exist in the codebase yet (grep-confirmed no trace of any of the three) — where losing the class card mid-turn is an accident; a rite's removal is a deliberate, run-defining choice between fights, and the design position is that those should be available regardless. Two things removed, nothing added: riteRemoveCard()'s `if (card.classRestriction) return;` early-out is gone, so ownedCards[index] is removed unconditionally exactly like any other card (same splice-from-ownedCards/deck/discard/hand-if-present, same '[RITE] removed CardName...' log line, same closeRiteScreen()+advanceRun() close); and the picker's `btn.disabled = !!card.classRestriction` line in renderRiteScreen() is gone, so every button, Rite included, renders enabled. The .die-action-row button:disabled CSS rule BUILD 088 added was left in place untouched, per the prompt's explicit instruction, even though nothing currently sets that state — a rule with no current caller costs nothing left in the stylesheet, and removing it was never asked for. Checked what else reads the class card's presence before touching anything, per the prompt's explicit instruction to report rather than work around: grep for classRestriction found only the two sites just edited plus each card's own static definition (every non-Rite card carries classRestriction: null); grep for orison found it reads gameState.turn.rollOutcome only (blank/nat_one), never card ids or deck contents; grep for Shove/Crush/Take It found no matches — the in-fight discard mechanic ruling 7 actually protects against does not exist in code yet; and startingDeck is read exactly once, by startNewRun(), which reads gameState.config.classes[...].startingDeck (a static array literal), never gameState.player.ownedCards — a mid-run removal cannot affect it. Nothing reads the class card's presence or count anywhere in the file; there was nothing to report beyond this check itself. Verified via a live jsdom drive of the actual index.html: dev-jumped to a rite, opened the picker, confirmed all 10 buttons (Rite included) rendered with disabled === false; clicked Rite and confirmed ownedCards dropped from 10 to 9 with Rite gone and every Strike/Ward untouched, the id absent from deck/discard/hand, the rite closed, and run.currentSlot advanced to the next slot exactly as any other removal does. Separately stress-tested the actual gameplay-loop concern named in the prompt — draws/discards/reshuffles with the class card gone — by rebuilding a fresh 9-card deck from the post-removal ownedCards and driving drawCards(1) through 40 consecutive draw-then-discard cycles (forcing multiple discard-pile reshuffles along the way): zero exceptions thrown, 'rite' never resurfaced in any drawn card, and deck+hand+discard summed to 9 throughout. Removing a non-class card (Strike) was re-run afterward and produced byte-for-byte identical behaviour to BUILD 088. Zero JS errors across every check. Build stamp now STAGE 2.20 / BUILD 089.

Stage 2.21 — the rolled-face highlight on the player's die column (BUILD 090). Presentation plus one small piece of state, per the prompt — no combat logic, numbers, or face behaviour touched. Problem: the rolled number lands in the middle strip but the player has to scan twenty rows to find the matching face, so the result of loading a face was invisible at the exact moment it mattered. State: gameState.turn.rolledFaceNumber (new field, initial-schema literal, startNewRun(), and both the resolvePlayerRoll() write and the START_OF_TURN clear) — a gameState property, not a module-scope variable, specifically to not repeat the BUILD 078 riteStep failure (module-scope flow state invisible to resets/dev-jumps that only ever touch gameState). Set in resolvePlayerRoll() (the one funnel both the natural and forced/dev roll paths already share) alongside the existing rollOutcome/rolledFaceWeight writes; cleared alongside those same two fields at the single site inside START_OF_TURN that already clears them — which, because every fight-start and Restart Fight path funnels through startFreshTurnPaused() -> runPhase('START_OF_TURN'), covers "clear on next turn," "clear on fight start," and "clear on Restart Fight" all from that one existing line, matching how rollOutcome/rolledFaceWeight already achieve the same three-way coverage; also cleared in startNewRun() for a brand-new run. Rendering: renderDieList() now adds 'die-row-rolled' (non-blank) or 'die-row-rolled-blank' (rollOutcome === 'blank', which per BUILD 084 already covers a spent Nat 1) to the row whose face.number matches rolledFaceNumber, gated to containerId 'playerDieList' or 'dieActionDieList' only — the two containers that ever show the player's own live die — never the enemy's die or the map's static preview list. No outline anywhere (BUILD 080's doubled-border/clipping lesson): the non-blank state uses only transform: translateX(6px) for the indent and a background rgba(--nat) wash (the same rgba-of-a-css-variable technique .die-row-pickable:hover already uses) for a 500ms keyframe flash (0.45 alpha) settling to a sustained 0.14 alpha lift that holds via the class's own static background value, not an animation `forwards` hack; the blank state uses only a background rgba(--blank) wash that fades to fully transparent over the same 500ms with no static background left after, so it leaves nothing behind and never gets an indent or gold. Neither rule touches color at all, so the existing .loaded/.nat-one/.nat-twenty text-colour rules keep winning exactly as before — no gold-text-on-gold-wash repeat of the BUILD 080 bug.
