Coexistence with the BUILD 079 Load/Strengthen picker was confirmed deliberately, not assumed: the rolled classes and the picker's own die-row-pickable/die-row-pick-inert classes are added independently via classList.add() and touch different CSS properties (background/transform vs. the picker's box-shadow), so both apply to the same row without clobbering each other. Verified in real windowed-mode Chromium via Playwright (installed into the project this session per Fergus's request, left in place — node_modules/package.json now live alongside index.html): force-rolled face 10 (a loaded mod face) and confirmed exactly one row (10) carried die-row-rolled immediately after the roll and still carried it, unchanged, once the auto-advance chain settled into CARD_PHASE (the sustained hold); clicked End Turn and confirmed rolledFaceNumber read null and zero rows carried any rolled class the instant the next turn's START_OF_TURN ran, before the next roll; force-rolled face 2 (unloaded) and confirmed it alone carried die-row-rolled-blank with zero rows carrying the non-blank class; measured all twenty row widths (474px, identical set) before and after both rolls; and opened the real Load picker mid-flow (dieActionChooseLoad() -> dieActionPickMod()) to confirm face 2's row carried die-row-rolled-blank AND die-row-pickable simultaneously, screenshotted showing the picker's gold inset frame and the fading blank wash on the same row at once. Screenshots also confirm no text-colour change on the rolled row (Consecrate's mod name stayed in its own loaded-face colour) and the indent visibly offsetting row 10 to the right of its neighbours. Zero console errors and zero page errors across the whole session. Build stamp now STAGE 2.21 / BUILD 090.

Stage 2.22 — the file split: index.html's single ~3,217-line inline `<script>` is now nine files in /js/, loaded via ordinary `<script src>` tags in explicit order (BUILD 091). Structural only, per the prompt — zero behaviour/logic/number/visual changes; read the whole script end to end first (last read end to end at BUILD 059, 2,778 lines, now 31 builds and ~1,900 lines stale) to plan the split and run the requested code audit. Split chosen (stated to Fergus before executing, per the prompt): state.js (gameState schema + the five mutation helpers + shuffle()), listener-registry.js (registerListener/callListeners/clearListeners + PIPELINE_HOOKS), pipeline.js (damage/block math + the roll system), cards-mods.js (getCard/getCardCost/drawCards/playCard + the entire init() function — cards and mods are not actually separable files in this codebase, they're all built inline inside one init() function alongside class registration and the dev-chrome reset, and init() was kept intact rather than decomposed into buildCards()/buildMods()/buildClasses() helpers, since that would be a real refactor of working code, not a file split), run-and-map.js (die/act builders, clearFightScopedState/startNewRun, slot dispatch, devJumpToSlot, resetFight/startFreshTurnPaused), phase-machine.js (PHASE_ORDER/runPhase/nextPhase/autoAdvance + playerRollResolved/enemyRollResolved), rendering.js (log(), refreshInspector(), every render* function, the die-action/card-reward/rite screen-flow state machines — their flow variables are UI-flow bookkeeping per the code's own original comments, not game state — the map renderer, and the four text lookup tables consumed only by render functions), dev-tools.js (devChromeOpen/devPauseBeforeFirstRoll, force-roll, dev mod loader, dev poison applier), bootstrap.js (every addEventListener wiring block + the sole `window.addEventListener('DOMContentLoaded', init)` kickoff). A static scan (extracting every top-level function/const/let name from the original inline script and from all nine new files) confirmed all 109 original top-level names are accounted for in exactly one new file each — zero missing, zero duplicated across files, zero names introduced that weren't already there; no collision existed before the split (also grep-confirmed directly against the original single file) and none was introduced by it, since the split renamed nothing.

Ordering trap (item 4a), resolved explicitly rather than inherited: read the entire script hunting for every place a function body reads a value declared later in file order (the refreshInspector()/dieActionStep pattern named in the prompt is one instance of a much broader pattern). Found that init() itself is never called eagerly — it is registered via `window.addEventListener('DOMContentLoaded', init)`, the single kickoff in the whole codebase — and confirmed by re-reading the complete script that the ONLY top-level (immediately-executing) statements anywhere in it are self-contained data literals and `addEventListener(...)` registrations whose callback bodies defer everything to click-time; nothing anywhere calls a game function immediately at parse time. Because every classic `<script src>` tag on a page shares one global lexical scope, and nothing invokes any of this code before every file has finished loading (DOMContentLoaded fires only once the whole document, including every script tag, has been parsed and executed), file order cannot break a function-body reference regardless of which file declares what — this is what actually makes the split safe, not hoisting luck. Documented two concrete instances of this rather than leaving them implicit: (1) state.js's five mutation helpers all call refreshInspector() (rendering.js, the second-to-last file loaded) — the same shape as the original dieActionStep case, just one level more foundational; (2) rendering.js and run-and-map.js call each other bidirectionally (screens open/advance slots; slot dispatch opens screens) — a genuine circularity inherent to the existing design, not introduced by this split and not resolvable by any choice of file order. Chosen order is still dependency-first for readability: state → listener-registry → pipeline → cards-mods → run-and-map → phase-machine → rendering → dev-tools → bootstrap.

Code audit (requested, not fixed — this build touches file structure only). The four previously-recorded items, re-checked directly against the current code: (1) registerListener has no dedup — STILL LIVE; it unconditionally pushes `{id, fn, clearOn}` on every call with no check against existing entries for the same hook+id. (2) dealBlock has no multiplier stage — NO LONGER ACCURATE AS STATED; generateBlock() (which dealBlock() calls) now has both a BLOCK_FLAT_ADDITION and a BLOCK_MULTIPLIER stage, structurally identical to calculateDamage()'s two stages — but BLOCK_MULTIPLIER is never registered by any current card or mod (grep-confirmed only DAMAGE_MULTIPLIER has a real registrant, Fervour), so the stage exists in full but is presently unexercised — a refinement of the original finding, not a match to it. (3) healPlayer and ON_HEAL may not exist despite rites healing 20 — STILL LIVE, confirmed absent by grep (zero matches for either name anywhere in the codebase); riteChooseHeal() heals via a direct `updatePlayer({ hp: ... })` call with no listener hook, so nothing can currently observe or modify a heal. (4) clearOn 'fight' is registered nowhere and has never been exercised — STILL LIVE; clearListeners('fight') is called (clearFightScopedState(), startNewRun()) but grep across every registerListener(...) call site found only 'permanent' and 'turn' ever passed as clearOn — the 'fight' branch has always been dead code. No new items are being added to this list per the prompt's own instruction not to fix or expand scope beyond what was asked — the four recorded items are the full audit deliverable for this build.

/audio/ and /art/ created, both empty, per the prompt — the single-file rule is dropped as of this build; they are destinations for future asset builds, not populated by this one.

Verified in real windowed-mode Chromium via Playwright, opening file:///.../index.html directly (not via any local server) exactly as Fergus runs the game: zero failed requests (all nine /js/ files loaded cleanly under file:// with no CORS/module errors), build stamp reads STAGE 2.22 / BUILD 091, the BUILD 090 rolled-face highlight re-confirmed working unchanged (force-rolling face 10 still marks exactly that row die-row-rolled), and a full fight played start to finish through the real UI (roll, play every affordable card each round via real button clicks, End Turn, four rounds to a win), followed by the real post-fight flow — the die action panel (Load/Strengthen/Skip, Skip clicked) then the card reward panel (three real pool cards offered, Skip clicked) — returning correctly to the map with the fight slot marked completed. Zero console errors, zero console warnings, zero page errors across the entire run. Playwright/Chromium remain installed in the project (node_modules/package.json, from BUILD 090) for future browser checks. Build stamp now STAGE 2.22 / BUILD 091.

Stage 2.23 — three engine fixes carried over from the BUILD 091 code audit (BUILD 092). No new features, no numbers, no visual changes — plumbing for systems work coming after this. (1) registerListener() (listener-registry.js) gained a dedup guard: before pushing a new entry it now checks whether an entry with the same hook AND the same id already exists (`.some()` over that hook's array), and if so logs '[LISTENER] duplicate registration skipped: HOOK ID' and returns without pushing — visible rather than silent, per the prompt. No existing registration call site was touched; every one of the eight real call sites already used a stable, unique id per hook (confirmed by re-reading each), so the guard never fires for current gameplay and changes nothing observable today — it only stops a future compounding synergy mod (one that calls registerListener() from inside its own effect(), the shape the prompt named) from accumulating duplicate listeners on every trigger. (2) healPlayer(amount) + an ON_HEAL hook, both new, added to pipeline.js next to dealDamage()/dealBlock(): computes newHp = Math.min(hp + amount, maxHp) — a direct clamp, deliberately not routed through calculateDamage()/generateBlock()'s flat-addition/multiplier stages, since the prompt explicitly excluded a healing multiplier — applies it via the existing updatePlayer() helper, fires callListeners('ON_HEAL', { amount: healedAmount }) with the actual post-cap amount healed, and returns that same amount (same shape as dealBlock() returning the real block generated). riteChooseHeal() (rendering.js) now reads hp before and after calling healPlayer(20) and logs '[RITE] healed N HP (before to after)' from those two reads — byte-for-byte the same log line format and content as before, still exactly 20 HP capped at maxHp; the only change is that a heal is now observable through the registry instead of being a bare updatePlayer({hp:...}) call nothing could hook. (3) clearListeners('fight') deleted from both call sites that had it — clearFightScopedState() and startNewRun() (run-and-map.js) — two single-line removals, nothing else: clearListeners('turn') in startNewRun() is untouched and still live (Consecrate/Vigil/Fervour really do register with clearOn: 'turn'). Grep across every registerListener() call site in the codebase confirmed only 'permanent' and 'turn' are ever passed as clearOn — 'fight' was never once used, so both calls were dead no-op filters from the day they were written; nothing else needed removing around them, well under the prompt's "stop and tell me" threshold. (4) Verification only, no code change, per the prompt: confirmed generateBlock()'s BLOCK_MULTIPLIER stage actually works by registering a throwaway 'test_throwaway_block_x3' listener (×3) inside a disposable Playwright page.evaluate() call, calling dealBlock(10, ...) before/during/after — 10 baseline, 30 with the multiplier registered, 10 again once the throwaway listener was filtered back out of the registry — confirming the stage (present but unexercised by any real mod since BUILD 091's audit) computes correctly when something does register against it, which is what Obelisk's design will need. The throwaway registration was never added to any file; it existed only inside that one page.evaluate() call and left no trace in gameState.registry.listeners afterward. Verified in real windowed-mode Chromium via Playwright, opening file:///.../index.html directly: build stamp reads STAGE 2.23 / BUILD 092; a direct console check confirmed the dedup guard skips a genuine second registerListener() call with the same hook+id (array length stays at 1, the listener fires exactly once) while a real heal still fires ON_HEAL correctly (spied via a throwaway 'test_spy' listener, cleaned up the same way); a full fight was played start to finish through the real UI to a win (7 rounds), followed by the real post-fight flow (die action Skip, card reward Skip) back to the map; hp was lowered by 15 for visibility, then a rite's Heal 20 HP was taken via a real click and confirmed to land correctly (47 to 67, capped by the same Math.min as before) with the log line '[RITE] healed 20 HP (47 to 67)' appearing exactly as before. Zero console errors, zero page errors throughout. Build stamp now STAGE 2.23 / BUILD 092.

Stage 2.24 — three independent UI/audio items, verified and reported separately per the prompt (BUILD 093). ITEM 1 (highlight re-trigger fix): diagnosis confirmed against the code exactly as described before fixing — renderDieList() rebuilds every row from scratch (innerHTML='' + createElement) on every render, so a class carrying a CSS animation (.die-row-rolled, .die-row-rolled-blank) always restarted that animation on a freshly created element regardless of whether the underlying roll actually changed; loading a mod mid-turn calls updateDie() -> refreshInspector() -> renderDieList() with no new roll, which is exactly what re-triggered it. Fix: the CSS classes are split into a sustained, non-animated .die-row-rolled (indent+wash, no @keyframes reference) and an animated .die-row-rolled-flash (same properties plus the keyframe); blanks keep only .die-row-rolled-blank-flash, with no sustained equivalent at all, matching "fades once, never reappears." renderDieList() now tracks a per-container 'last committed roll signature' (rolledFaceNumber + rollOutcome) and only applies the -flash class when the signature differs from what was last committed; every later re-render of the same roll gets the plain sustained class (or, for blanks, no class at all). The commit itself is deferred via queueMicrotask() rather than written synchronously in the render call — resolving one roll triggers a synchronous CASCADE of multiple renders (resolvePlayerRoll()'s own updateTurn(), then a mod's effect() calling updatePlayer() again, e.g. Consecrate's soul grant), and an earlier synchronous-commit version of this fix caused the flash to never appear at all, because the first of those cascaded renders committed the signature immediately, so every subsequent render in that same cascade — including the one actually left on screen — already read as "seen." Deferring the commit to a microtask lets every render within one roll's synchronous resolution still see it as new, while a genuinely later, separate render (a dev-tools click, its own task) correctly sees it as already committed. No change to indent, wash width, colours, or timing, per the prompt. Indent/wash/timing/colour values themselves are untouched — confirmed by diffing the CSS, only the animation-vs-sustained split and the class names changed. ITEM 2 (synthesised roll sound): a new js/audio.js, same shape as the listener registry — one AudioContext plus a SOUND_TABLE mapping event names ('roll', 'roll_blank') to sound functions (playRollSound/playBlankRollSound), both short square-wave clicks under 200ms via one shared playClick(freq, durationMs, peakGain) synth, reference RuneScape small clicks. Game logic never calls a sound function directly: pipeline.js's resolvePlayerRoll() calls playAudioEvent(rollOutcome === 'blank' ? 'roll_blank' : 'roll') at the same funnel point the die-row highlight already uses, so a forced dev roll gets the identical sound a natural one does. unlockAudioOnce() resumes the AudioContext (constructed lazily, starts 'suspended' per browser autoplay policy) and is wired via `document.addEventListener('pointerdown', unlockAudioOnce, { once: true })` in bootstrap.js — resumes once on the page's first real click, self-removes, never runs again; since a fight can only be entered by clicking a map node first, the unlock always lands before the first possible roll. A mute checkbox (#devMuteAudioCheckbox, default unchecked/unmuted) was added to the existing DEV SETUP bar in the dev drawer, writing the one audioMuted flag playClick() checks before ever touching the AudioContext. No audio files anywhere — /audio/ stays empty this build, confirmed by directory listing. ITEM 3 (die face order flip): renderDieList() now iterates faces.slice().reverse() instead of faces directly to decide DOM append order — gameState.die.faces (and gameState.enemy.die.faces) themselves are never mutated or reordered, only a reversed COPY drives which order rows are drawn in, so every row still closes over its own real face object and face.number is completely unaffected by display position; applies uniformly to every caller (fight screen, the map's static previews, and the Load/Strengthen picker) since they all funnel through this one function. Verified in real windowed-mode Chromium via Playwright, three separate fresh page loads (opening file:///.../index.html directly, no server), one per item, per the prompt's "verify each separately" instruction: ITEM 1 — forcing a roll on face 10 showed die-row-rolled-flash immediately; loading a mod on a different face (twice, via separate dev-tool actions) left face 10 holding plain die-row-rolled both times, never re-flashing; a genuine new roll on face 10 in a later turn correctly flashed again; a forced blank roll showed die-row-rolled-blank-flash once, and a subsequent re-render (another mod load) left that row fully plain with no rolled class at all — all six checks true. ITEM 2 — AudioContext read 'no context yet' before any interaction, 'running' immediately after the first real click; a forced non-blank roll invoked the 'roll' branch and created exactly one oscillator; a forced blank roll invoked 'roll_blank' and also created exactly one oscillator (a different, correctly-branching sound); with the mute checkbox checked, a forced roll still announced the event but created zero oscillators. ITEM 3 — player die, enemy die, and both map preview dice (player and boss) all read 20 at the top descending to 1 at the bottom; the critical check — opening the Load picker and clicking the row displaying face 17 (sitting at DOM position 3, deliberately far from its own face number, to rule out a position-based mistake) — logged '[DIE ACTION] loaded ... onto face 17', the exact number clicked, never a mirrored position; the identical check repeated for Strengthen also logged 'strengthened face 17'; hover text read from the clicked row matched that face's own actual mod description in both cases. Zero console errors and zero page errors across all three fresh-page verification runs. Build stamp reads STAGE 2.24 / BUILD 093.

Stage 2.25 — the sound pass: twelve new synthesised sounds on the BUILD 093 audio module (BUILD 094). No new systems, no gameplay/number/visual changes — every sound is announced from a place the game logic already knows the event occurred, per the prompt's explicit constraint; none of the twelve introduced a new detection point anywhere. Wiring, one per event: card play attack/block/hybrid announced from playCard() (cards-mods.js) right after soul is spent, ahead of card.effect() — classified by card.type (attack/block) with rite and litany special-cased to 'hybrid' since grep confirmed they're the only two cards whose effect() calls both dealDamage() and dealBlock(); utility cards (draw/soul/poison, no damage or block) get no card-play sound at all, since the prompt names only the three categories and utility fits none. Damage-landing-on-enemy/damage-landing-on-player share one hook inside dealDamage() itself (pipeline.js) — the single place both card/mod damage and the enemy's own attack already flow through — gated on damage > 0 so a fully-blocked hit or a 0-damage Retribution never plays a "landed" sound for a hit that didn't land; poison ticks were already confirmed to bypass dealDamage() entirely (both poison branches call calculateDamage() directly), so they get no sound with zero extra filtering needed. Block-absorb is announced in ENEMY_ACT_PHASE (phase-machine.js) from Math.min(intent, block) gated >0, placed before the dealDamage() call so it fires first when a hit is partially blocked — damage-landing follows immediately after for whatever gets through, so a fully-blocked hit fires block-absorb alone, an unblocked hit fires damage-landing alone, and a partial block fires both exactly once each in that order. End turn is announced from the endTurnBtn click handler (bootstrap.js). Nat 20/Nat 1 are new dedicated events split out of resolvePlayerRoll()'s existing rollOutcome branch (pipeline.js) — previously both shared the generic 'roll' click; a spent Nat 1 still correctly falls through to the ordinary roll_blank click, matching BUILD 084's "reads exactly like any other blank" rule. Fight won/fight lost are announced inside runPhase()'s existing win/loss guard (phase-machine.js), which the BUILD 053/063 re-entry pattern already guarantees fires exactly once per fight (run.status stops being 'active' the instant the branch runs, so no re-entrant call can reach the sound line twice). Die action confirmed is announced from both dieActionPickLoadFace() and dieActionPickStrengthenFace() (rendering.js), right after each one's own updateDie() commits the mod/weight change. Twelve new SOUND_TABLE entries in audio.js, built on a shared playTone(waveform, freqStart, freqEnd, durationMs, peakGain) synth (playClick() is now a thin wrapper over it, byte-identical behaviour to BUILD 093) so every sound is a WebAudio oscillator with an exponential decay, optionally frequency-swept; distinguishability was designed explicitly rather than assumed, varying waveform (square/triangle/sawtooth/sine), register, sweep direction, single-vs-multi-click shape, and (for Nat 1) a deliberately detuned second oscillator for an audible "wrong" beating no other sound has — documented per-sound in each function's own comment, including which neighbouring sound each was tuned away from. Nat 20 (three ascending clicks, ~260ms, loudest) and Nat 1 (a 260ms harsh detuned descending sweep) are the only two allowed past 200ms, per the prompt. All ten of the rest measured under 200ms by construction (their own durationMs arguments: 35-140ms). The mute flag (audioMuted) is checked once, inside playTone() — the one place every sound, including the multi-call cascades (Nat 20, End Turn, Fight Won), ultimately schedules its oscillator — so the existing mute checkbox silences all twelve with no per-sound duplication. No audio files; /audio/ confirmed still empty by directory listing. Verified in real windowed-mode Chromium via Playwright, opening file:///.../index.html directly, using a SOUND_TABLE spy (every entry wrapped to record its name into an ordered call log, plus an AudioContext.prototype.createOscillator counter) rather than trying to hear anything: playing Strike fired exactly ["card_attack","damage_enemy"] in that order; playing Ward fired exactly ["card_block"]; playing Rite fired exactly ["card_hybrid","damage_enemy"] with no block_absorb; an enemy attack with block partway between 0 and intent fired ["block_absorb","damage_player"] in that order, each exactly once; the same attack with block ≥ intent fired block_absorb alone; the same attack with block = 0 fired damage_player alone; forcing face 20 fired exactly ["nat_20"] (never "roll"); forcing face 1 fresh fired exactly ["nat_1"], and forcing face 1 again in the same fight (already spent) fired exactly ["roll_blank"] with no nat_1; forcing enemy hp to 0 (with the same runPhase() re-entry the real win path uses) fired fight_won exactly once, and forcing player hp to 0 the same way fired fight_lost exactly once; confirming a Load pick and a Strengthen pick each fired die_action_confirmed exactly once; checking the mute box and forcing Nat 20 (the three-click cascade, including its two setTimeout-scheduled follow-ups) still announced the event but created zero oscillators. A full fight played to a win through the real UI (6 rounds, real button clicks) produced 44 total sound events with fight_won appearing exactly once and fight_lost zero times, no duplicate/orphaned firings observed, and the full set of event names used across the fight matched the twelve/fourteen-entry table with nothing unexpected. Zero console errors, zero page errors throughout. Build stamp reads STAGE 2.25 / BUILD 094.

Stage 2.26 — the sound chain and five new sounds (BUILD 095). Same rules as BUILD 094 throughout: synthesised only, /audio/ stays empty, game logic never calls a sound function directly, every sound announced from a point the code already knows the event occurred, mute silences everything. PART 1, the rising chains: two independent, Balatro-style pitch chains living entirely inside audio.js — Chain A (cards played) and Chain B (mods triggered) — sharing no counter, each capped at 8 steps, both reset by a new resetSoundChains() called directly (not a sound, so not through playAudioEvent()) from runPhase()'s START_OF_TURN branch (phase-machine.js). The chain mechanism is fully internal to audio.js: playAudioEvent() itself checks whether the announced event name is in CARD_CHAIN_EVENTS (card_attack/card_block/card_hybrid) or MOD_CHAIN_EVENTS (mod_trigger), passes the current step as the sound function's one argument, and advances that chain's counter (Math.min(step+1, 8)) afterward — so every existing call site (playCard(), the mod_dispatch listener) is completely unaware chains exist and calls playAudioEvent() exactly as it already did. The step becomes a frequency ratio (2^(steps/12), true equal-temperament semitones) multiplying every frequency argument a chained sound's own playTone() call already used — timbre (waveform/duration/envelope) is untouched, confirmed by re-reading each function; only pitch moves. Chain B needed a genuinely new event, mod_trigger, since mods had no single existing "this mod just went off" identity sound to pitch (only some mods deal damage, which already has its own damage_enemy/damage_player sound); it's announced from the existing 'mod_dispatch' MOD_TRIGGER listener in init() (cards-mods.js) — the one funnel both a natural single-face roll and Nat 20's onNatTwenty() loop already dispatch every mod trigger through, so this needed no new detection logic, only a new sound identity for something that already had a single dispatch point. PART 2, five new sounds: fight start (normal/elite/boss, keyed off slot.label) announced from beginFightFromSlot() (run-and-map.js) — the boss variant is a slow-attack low consonant sine drone (110/165Hz, ~320ms) explicitly allowed past 200ms, "to carry weight" per the prompt, the same exception the Nat sounds already have. Boss defeated is a new, bigger, distinct sound (four ascending sine tones plus a low foundation, ~400ms) branched inside runPhase()'s existing win guard (phase-machine.js) so the boss case gets boss_defeated INSTEAD OF (not in addition to) the fight_won every other fight still gets, fixing the fact that killing the boss and killing a normal enemy previously sounded identical. Load a mod onto a face gets its own genuinely spooky sound — two sine tones 8Hz apart (an audible detuned beating, the same technique Nat 1 uses) with a slow 40ms fade-in (playTone() gained an optional 6th argument, attackMs, for this — every existing caller omits it and keeps the exact instant-attack envelope it always had) — deliberately not a UI confirm tone, since the die is the one object in this game allowed to feel wrong. Strengthen gets its own plain, instant-attack, non-detuned descending tone, so Load ("adds," eerie) and Strengthen ("deepens," solid) read as different in kind, not just pitch; this replaces BUILD 094's single shared die_action_confirmed event and sound entirely (both removed) rather than living alongside it. Card reward taken is now split: Strike/Ward get a plain single square click (card_reward_basic), everything else a richer two-tone consonant chord (card_reward_rich), branched in cardRewardPickCard() (rendering.js) — worth recording: gameState.config.cardPool never actually contains 'strike' or 'ward' (grep-confirmed against its own literal definition in init()), so the basic branch is implemented exactly as specified but is currently unreachable through real play; this was reported rather than silently worked around, and the pool's own contents were not touched, since changing what a reward can offer would be a gameplay change outside this build's scope. Verified in real windowed-mode Chromium via Playwright, file:// direct, using the same SOUND_TABLE-wrapping spy as BUILD 094 extended to also record each call's argument: playing five cards in one turn (three attack, one block, one attack) fired card_attack/card_attack/card_attack/card_block/card_attack with offsets 0,1,2,3,4 exactly, confirming both the increment and that timbre still tracks each card's own type regardless of chain position; a manually fired mod trigger in the same test did not touch the card chain's own counter (confirmed independent); the next turn's first card fired at offset 0, confirming the reset; loading 10 mods onto the die (9 dev-loaded plus face 10's own Consecrate) and forcing Nat 20 fired mod_trigger 10 times with offsets 0,1,2,3,4,5,6,7,8,8 — climbing exactly one step per firing mod and correctly holding at the cap rather than continuing to 9; fight-start on a plain/elite/boss slot fired fight_start_normal/fight_start_elite/fight_start_boss respectively, each exactly once; defeating a boss fired boss_defeated exactly once with no fight_won; Load and Strengthen fired their own distinct single event each; picking a real reward card fired card_reward_rich, and picking a test-aliased 'strike' (temporarily added to cardPool for this one call only, then removed — a test-time monkeypatch, not a production change) fired card_reward_basic, confirming the branch logic itself is correct even though real play can't reach it; muting and forcing the boss fight-start sound still announced the event but created zero oscillators. A full fight played to a win through the real UI (6 rounds) produced 46 total sound events including fight_start_normal exactly once, fight_won exactly once, and zero boss_defeated/fight_lost, with no duplicate or orphaned firings. Zero console errors, zero page errors throughout. Build stamp reads STAGE 2.26 / BUILD 095.

Stage 2.27 — a tone pass on the existing sounds, no new sounds/events/gameplay/numbers/visuals (BUILD 096). Structural cause fixed first, per the prompt: almost every sound was built on the shared playTone() with a square wave and a near-identical short instant-attack envelope, differing mainly by frequency — pitch separation alone can't make same-waveform-same-envelope sounds feel distinct. Retuned every sound except the four the prompt named as already working (Load, Strengthen, Nat 20, Nat 1 — each left byte-for-byte unchanged, confirmed by diff, with a comment on each stating so and that it was reviewed against the new set and doesn't sit oddly); fight_start_boss and boss_defeated were also reviewed (not named exempt) but left unchanged since neither was ever square, tinny, or frequent. Register lowered substantially on every frequently-fired sound (roll 1400->620Hz, card_attack 1800->480Hz, damage_enemy 2200->820Hz, mod_trigger 1100->420Hz, end_turn 550->200Hz, etc.). Waveform reassigned per the prompt's rule — square/sawtooth reserved for the sounds that should feel harsh (Nat 1 unchanged; damage_player kept sawtooth; fight_lost explicitly CHANGED from sine to sawtooth this build, since the prompt named it as one of the three that should feel harsh and it was the one of the three still soft) — everything else moved to sine or triangle: roll and card_attack (square->triangle), roll_blank/card_block/mod_trigger/end_turn/fight_won/card_reward_basic/card_reward_rich (square or plain->sine), damage_enemy and card_hybrid and fight_start_elite (square/sawtooth->triangle). Short fades added to soften what used to be instant clicks (roll 6ms, card_attack 4ms, card_block 12ms, card_hybrid 8ms, mod_trigger 5ms, damage_enemy 2ms, block_absorb 18ms, end_turn 10ms, fight_won 6ms, fight_start_normal 10ms, fight_start_elite 5ms, card_reward_basic 6ms, card_reward_rich 8ms) — damage_player and fight_lost deliberately kept instant-attack, since a hit and a loss should feel abrupt, not eased in. Each sound given its own flavour across at least two of {waveform, envelope shape, attack time, duration, single-tone-vs-interval} per the prompt's explicit rule: card_attack (triangle, single tone, 4ms/50ms, decisive) vs card_block (sine, single tone, 12ms/110ms, dull) vs card_hybrid (triangle, TWO simultaneous tones — a fourth apart — 8ms/90ms) now read as three different actions, not three notes on the same voice; damage_enemy (triangle, bright, 2ms attack, "good news") vs damage_player (sawtooth, falling, instant attack, "bad news," unchanged harshness) are unmistakably different; block_absorb gained real body (18ms fade, stretched to 170ms) instead of reading as a click; end_turn lost its old square double-tick entirely and became one quiet 200Hz sine blip at gain 0.045, the quietest sound in the table, "close to unnoticeable" as specified, since it fires every turn. A full comment-block spec table was written at the top of audio.js recording every sound's waveform, frequency range, envelope, and duration, so the next tone pass starts from a written record rather than from re-reading the code, per the prompt's explicit instruction. No SOUND_TABLE entries added/removed/renamed, no announce-site changed in any other file (grep-confirmed only audio.js has a newer timestamp this session), no change to the two BUILD 095 pitch chains, their mechanism, or the 8-step cap, no change to mute. Verified in real windowed-mode Chromium via Playwright, file:// direct: SOUND_TABLE's key set matches BUILD 095's exactly (no additions/removals/renames); playing Strike/Ward/Rite in one turn fired card_attack/damage_enemy/card_block/card_hybrid/damage_enemy in that exact sequence with Chain A offsets 0,1,2 across the three card sounds, confirming both mechanisms untouched; forcing Nat 20 across 10 loaded faces still produced mod_trigger offsets 0,1,2,...,8,8, climbing and capping exactly as before; muting a sound (boss fight-start) still announced the event but created zero oscillators; a full fight played to a win through the real UI (5 rounds) produced 47 total sound events using the same category of event names BUILD 095's own full-fight run used (card/damage/roll/block/end-turn/fight-start/fight-won/mod-trigger), fight_start_normal and fight_won each exactly once, zero fight_lost/boss_defeated, no duplicate or orphaned firings. Zero console errors, zero page errors. Build stamp reads STAGE 2.27 / BUILD 096.

Stage 2.28 — the boss die's enemy half, the first real enemy mechanic in the project (BUILD 097). No changes to the player's die, cards, mods, or any player-facing number. Wired the enemy POISON face (die faces 5/10/15, loaded since BUILD 075 but never dispatched to anything): a new permanent listener, 'enemy_buff_dispatch' on ENEMY_BUFF_TRIGGER (cards-mods.js's init(), registered unconditionally rather than tied to gameState.player.classId like the three Ordained passives beside it, since this is the enemy's own die mechanic, not a player class passive), applies exactly 5 poison stacks to the player through the same updatePlayer({poisonStacks:...}) field every other poison source (Blight/Virulence/Censer/the dev poison applier) already writes to — no second poison system, confirmed by re-reading the existing START_OF_TURN player-poison tick, which needed zero changes to decay these stacks identically and route the damage through calculateDamage() tagged 'poison' per Law 1. Enemy Nat 20: a new 'boss_nat_twenty_passive' listener on ENEMY_NAT_TWENTY, a deliberate mirror of the Ordained's own onNatTwenty — filters gameState.enemy.die.faces for loaded, non-Nat faces (already in ascending order) and fires ENEMY_BUFF_TRIGGER for each, same loop-safety approach, not capped, not once per fight, matching the player exactly; on the current boss die this fires the three poison faces ascending, 15 total stacks. Enemy Nat 1: a new 'boss_nat_one_passive' listener on ENEMY_NAT_ONE — two effects at once: sets a new turn-scoped gameState.turn.enemyAttackCancelledThisTurn flag (checked at the very top of ENEMY_ACT_PHASE in phase-machine.js, which now returns immediately when it's true, before intent/block are even read — no damage, no intent resolution, no block-absorb/damage sounds), and self-poisons the enemy via updateEnemy({poisonStacks:...}) — the exact same field player cards already poison the enemy through — 5 stacks per loaded 'enemy_buff_poison' face on its own die (15 on the current boss die), never a flat number. Gated on a new gameState.enemy.natOneFiredThisFight (added to state.js, mirroring the player's own BUILD 084 field exactly), reset in clearFightScopedState()/startNewRun() (run-and-map.js) alongside the enemy's other fight-scoped fields, so Restart Fight and a fresh run both clear it; once true, resolveEnemyRoll()'s own ENEMY_NAT_ONE branch (pipeline.js, now a real dispatch instead of the old "not yet implemented" stub, restructured to mirror resolvePlayerRoll()'s dispatch shape exactly) logs the same plain '[ENEMY ROLL] blank' line a genuinely blank face already produces, with no distinguishing tag and no re-triggered effect. enemyAttackCancelledThisTurn is cleared at the next START_OF_TURN alongside rollOutcome/modTriggeredThisTurn, the same single clear site every other per-turn flag already uses. All three new log lines use the existing '[ENEMY]' prefix (already colour-coded in LOG_PREFIX_CLASSES), matching the format convention every other enemy-side log line already follows — no new prefix, no new colour. Verified in real windowed-mode Chromium via Playwright, file:// direct, dev-jumped straight to the boss slot (the only enemy with hasDie: true): forcing faces 5, 10, and 15 individually each landed exactly 5 player poison stacks, and a subsequent START_OF_TURN tick correctly decayed 5→4 while dealing the matching HP loss; forcing an enemy Nat 20 (face 20) fired all three poison faces ascending with the log reading '[ENEMY] Nat 20: 3 loaded faces trigger' followed by three '[ENEMY] applied 5 poison to player, stacks now 5/10/15' lines, landing exactly 15 total stacks; forcing an enemy Nat 1 (face 1) left player HP completely unchanged after running ENEMY_ACT_PHASE (confirming the attack never resolved) while logging '[ENEMY] attack cancelled by its own Nat 1', applied exactly 15 self-poison stacks to the enemy, and a following START_OF_TURN tick correctly dropped the enemy's own HP by 15 and its stacks from 15 to 14; forcing face 1 again in the same fight produced zero additional poison and logged the plain '[ENEMY ROLL] blank' line, confirming once-per-fight; clicking Restart Fight reset gameState.enemy.natOneFiredThisFight back to false. Dev-jumping to the opening (normal) and elite slots confirmed gameState.enemy.hasDie reads false for both, so none of this is reachable outside a boss fight. Zero console errors, zero page errors. Build stamp reads STAGE 2.28 / BUILD 097.

Stage 2.29 — the Nat 1 correction plus distinct normals and elites, closing the enemy half (BUILD 098). PART 1: boss_nat_one_passive (cards-mods.js) corrected from 5 self-poison stacks PER loaded poison face (15 on the boss, ~65 total decay damage — roughly two thirds of the boss's own HP from a single 5% roll) to a flat 5 regardless of how many poison faces the die carries; the cancelled-attack half is untouched, and the player-facing amount (5 per loaded face, via enemy_buff_dispatch) was already correct and is unchanged. PART 2: buildEnemyDieFaces() (run-and-map.js) generalised from a hardcoded boss-only layout into buildEnemyDieFaces(poisonFaceNumbers, includeNats) — includeNats puts ENEMY_NAT_ONE/ENEMY_NAT_TWENTY on faces 1/20, false leaves them as ordinary blanks with no Nat behaviour at all. buildAct() now gives the elite its own static die (hasDie: true, poison on faces 7 and 14 — chosen specifically not to reuse the boss's 5/10/15, so the elite reads as a genuinely different die at a glance) alongside the boss's unchanged die (5/10/15 + both Nats); normals and the opening fight keep hasDie: false, unchanged, and their die object is simply omitted from their static config since it's never read. Both static die objects are built once by buildAct() and never mutated. beginFightFromSlot() now copies whichever slot's own die onto the live gameState.enemy.die when a fight begins, instead of always reusing one shared die object — this only works because a genuine correctness bug was caught and fixed in the same pass: the map's boss-preview die read live gameState.enemy.die.faces directly, which — once two different slot types could carry two different dice — would have shown whichever fight was most recently entered under the "BOSS DIE" label the moment an elite had ever been fought. Fixed by having both the boss and a new elite map preview read their own slot's static enemy.die.faces (gameState.run.act.boss.enemy.die.faces / the upper lane's Elite slot, found via .find(s => s.label==='Elite') rather than a hardcoded index) instead of the live gameState.enemy. The elite preview (#elitePreview/#eliteDiePreviewList, new markup + two small CSS rule extensions) sits between the player and boss previews on the map, built with the identical title/HP/intent/die-column shape the boss preview already uses — no new rendering pattern. The fight screen's own enemy panel needed zero code changes: its existing hasDie-keyed conditional (refreshInspector(), rendering.js) already generically shows/hides the die column, so giving the elite hasDie:true and a real die.faces array made it start rendering correctly there automatically. Verified in real windowed-mode Chromium via Playwright, file:// direct: forcing the boss's Nat 1 now lands exactly 5 self-poison stacks (not 15), which decay 5→4→3→2 against the boss's own HP on successive ticks; the cancelled attack still leaves player HP untouched; a second face-1 the same fight still resolves as a plain blank; Restart Fight still resets the flag. Forcing each of the elite's two poison faces (7 and 14) individually landed exactly 5 player stacks each with correct decay afterward. Forcing face 20 and face 1 on the elite die both produced a bare '[ENEMY ROLL] blank' log line with modId: null confirmed directly from gameState — no ENEMY_NAT_TWENTY/ENEMY_NAT_ONE dispatch, no enemyAttackCancelledThisTurn, no self-poison. Normal and opening slots confirmed hasDie: false, unaffected. The elite's die was confirmed rendering with exactly faces 7 and 14 marked loaded both in the map's new #eliteDiePreviewList (20 rows) and in the real fight screen's #enemyDieList while actually fighting the elite (visible, 20 rows, same two loaded faces); a normal fight's #enemyDieList confirmed hidden (the empty slot), and the boss's own map preview confirmed still showing exactly 5/10/15, unaffected by the elite's addition. Zero console errors, zero page errors. Build stamp reads STAGE 2.29 / BUILD 098.

Test rename: tests/ki16_mechanics_check.test.js renamed to tests/lean_mass_check.test.js (its content verifies Strengthen/Zeal/Covenant/Fervour composing — Lean Mass, D-45 — not KI-16, which is closed and unrelated). Read-only audit session, no build number, no behaviour change: js/ untouched, confirmed byte-identical to a pre-audit backup; tests/mods.test.js (11/11) and tests/facts.test.js (23/23) unchanged before and after; the renamed test passes identically to its pre-rename run.

BUILD 106 (closes KI-16) — landed. UNVERIFIED. Source note (BUILD 112): no CLAUDE.md CONFIRMED WORKING line was ever written for this build — the only in-repo trace is two passing mentions inside the BUILD 107 write-up ("first real-browser exercise of BUILD 106's calculateDamage()/callListeners() routing" and "BUILD 106 was accepted on tests/mods.test.js alone and has no Playwright evidence on record"). This entry is reconstructed from the Notion "1 State" page, section "2.1 Confirmed working," not from a CLAUDE.md line, since none exists; verified against the current code before being written (BUILD 112 grep, below), not copied on trust. calculateDamage() and generateBlock() (pipeline.js) no longer read gameState.registry.listeners directly — DAMAGE_FLAT_ADDITION, DAMAGE_MULTIPLIER, BLOCK_FLAT_ADDITION and BLOCK_MULTIPLIER all dispatch through callListeners(), which now returns each listener's fn(data) result as an array (this is the same callListeners() return-value change BUILD 106's own comment in pipeline.js credits to "KI-16 fix" and cross-references at listener-registry.js's callListeners() itself). The PIPELINE_HOOKS special-case branch is gone. The block path (BLOCK_FLAT_ADDITION/BLOCK_MULTIPLIER) was unified in the same pass, wider than the original prompt scoped, verified by the same check. All six of tests/mods.test.js's damage numbers were identical before and after, 11/11 passing both runs. Backups on record: backups/pipeline.js.bak_20260911_110056, backups/listener-registry.js.bak_20260911_110056. BUILD 112 GREP CONFIRMATION (before writing this entry): `grep -rn "PIPELINE_HOOKS" js/` returned zero hits, confirming the branch is genuinely gone, not just renamed; `grep -n "DAMAGE_FLAT_ADDITION\|DAMAGE_MULTIPLIER\|BLOCK_FLAT_ADDITION\|BLOCK_MULTIPLIER\|callListeners" js/pipeline.js` confirmed all four hooks dispatch through callListeners() and pipeline.js's own header comment there is independently dated "BUILD 106 (KI-16 fix)"; both named backup files confirmed present on disk at the paths above. Verification level: UNVERIFIED — tests/mods.test.js only, no Playwright evidence was ever recorded for this build (107's own write-up says so directly, and no build112_-prefixed or other screenshot/script naming this build exists in tests/).

Stage 2.37 — die size to GAME_CONFIG, one per-entity value; Strengthen/Ordain's weight write folded into one helper (BUILD 107, closes KI-12). GAME_CONFIG.DIE_SIZE = { PLAYER, ELITE, BOSS } (js/config.js) replaces the bare literal 20 at all nine sites a pre-build grep found (six in run-and-map.js, one in rendering.js, two in dev-tools.js) — buildEnemyDieFaces() gained a third dieSize argument so the elite and boss each pass their own GAME_CONFIG.DIE_SIZE entry rather than sharing one constant, per D-11. New strengthenFace(faceNumber) (pipeline.js) is now the one place any face's weight is ever written; dieActionPickStrengthenFace() (rendering.js) and Ordain's effect (cards-mods.js) both call it instead of each cloning-and-incrementing gameState.die.faces independently. config.js's stale F14 comment corrected to include the base "every fight win grants 1" clause it had been missing (comment only, behaviour unchanged). Verified: tests/mods.test.js 11/11 and tests/facts.test.js 23/23, both unchanged before and after; a real Chromium/Playwright run (dev-jumped to the boss) confirmed the player die renders 20 DOM rows, the boss die renders 20 DOM rows, Strengthening face 10 raised gameState weight 1→2 with the on-screen ×2 badge updating to match, and zero console/page errors throughout — BUILD 106 was accepted on tests/mods.test.js alone and has no Playwright evidence on record; 107 required and has it.

Stage 2.37 — twelfth mod, Anthem (BUILD 113) — landed. One change: 6 damage plus 4 per point of weight on its own face (weight 1 -> 10, weight 2 -> 14, weight 3 -> 18). Reuses two existing paths, writes no new ones: dealDamage('enemy', 6 + (4 * weight), 'attack', 'anthem') is Smite's own call shape, tagged 'attack' so Fervour's DAMAGE_MULTIPLIER doubles it identically; weight is gameState.turn.rolledFaceWeight, Covenant's own read (never re-derived from gameState.die.faces). No registerListener() call — like Smite/Penance, Anthem fires through the existing MOD_TRIGGER mod_dispatch listener, which already announces the 'mod_trigger' sound and bumps the trigger-count badge for every mod; no new sound was added. Anthem only reads weight; nothing writes it. PRE-EDIT GREP: 'anthem'/'Anthem' found zero times anywhere in js/ before this build — zero collisions on add. Config: GAME_CONFIG's F24 comment (js/config.js) corrected 11 -> 12; tests/facts.test.js's own F24 assertion updated to match (hardcoded, not GAME_CONFIG-derived — would otherwise have failed at 12 mods). tests/mods.test.js gained one test: forces face 2 for Anthem (10 damage, weight 1), calls strengthenFace() directly on that face, drives a full real second-round phase cycle via nextPhase() (End Turn's own transitions, not a shortcut), forces the same face again (14 damage, weight 2). VERIFIED: node tests/mods.test.js 11/11 before this build's edits, 12/12 after; node tests/facts.test.js 23/23 before and after. Real Chromium via Playwright (first real-browser exercise of BUILD 106's calculateDamage()/callListeners() routing — no mismatch found; base-case only, no Fervour loaded in this pass, that combination is unit-covered by tests/mods.test.js's own Fervour test): dev-jumped to a normal fight (lower lane slot 0, label 'Fight'), dev-loaded Anthem onto face 12 via the real devLoadMod(), forced it — log read exactly '[MOD] anthem: 10 damage', enemy HP 70->60, screenshot tests/build113_step9a.png. Ended the round via the real End Turn button, reached round 2's ROLL_PHASE, used the real Skip to Die Action shortcut (openDieActionScreen()), chose Strengthen, picked face 12 via dieActionPickStrengthenFace(12) — weight 1->2, screenshot tests/build113_step9b_strengthen.png. dieActionOrigin defaults to 'reward' so closing the die action panel opened the card reward screen next (the same real flow a genuine fight win reaches); dismissed via cardRewardSkip(), then forced face 12 again — log read exactly '[MOD] anthem: 14 damage', enemy HP 60->46, confirmed in both the assertion and the log panel visible in screenshot tests/build113_step9b_weight2.png (the reward-flow close had by then advanced gameState.run.screen to 'map', so that screenshot's main panel shows the map, not the fight card — a pre-existing consequence of the reward-flow shortcut, not something this build touched; the numbers were read from gameState directly, not off that panel). Zero console errors, zero page errors across both pages. Separately, on a fresh page (nothing dev-loaded), replicated dieActionChooseLoad()'s own eligible-pool filter directly (DIE_ACTION_EXCLUDED_MOD_IDS, a real top-level const) rather than calling it and hoping a random 3-slice included Anthem: the pool held exactly 11 entries (all mods but the excluded anchor Consecrate), Anthem among them — screenshot tests/build113_step9c.png. Zero console/page errors on that page too.

Stage 2.41 — the run record (BUILD 109) — landed. PRE-BUILD GREP (per the prompt's explicit gate, posted and approved before any edit): the prompt described "widening the existing copy-run-line button," but `grep -rn "copy\|clipboard\|Copy\|run.line\|runLine" js/*.js index.html` found zero real hits (three matches were unrelated comment text) and `grep -rn "localStorage" js/*.js index.html` found zero hits anywhere — no button, no clipboard code, no persistence layer of any kind existed. Flagged to Fergus before writing any code; approved to build the whole thing new, exactly as the rest of the prompt specified, rather than "widen" something nonexistent. Everything else the prompt asked to locate was real and as expected: Load offer generation/pick/skip (dieActionChooseLoad()/dieActionPickMod()/dieActionPickLoadFace()/dieActionChooseSkip(), rendering.js) and run-scoped state (gameState.run, state.js).

Design: a new top-level gameState.runRecord namespace (STATE SCHEMA) with its own updateRunRecord() helper (state.js), following the exact pattern of every other update*() helper rather than nesting inside gameState.run (which startNewRun() itself rebuilds — a record for the run ending needs to be read/flushed before that rebuild, not live inside it) or creating a parallel array keyed by anything (the same carry-forward rule BUILD 108 exists to enforce). Per-mod trigger counts are never accumulated in the record itself — collectTriggerCountsByMod() (run-and-map.js) reads gameState.die.faces fresh every time the record is serialized, per the prompt's explicit "not from any other source"; since Load never offers a mod already on the die in either slot (MULTI-MOD FACES), a mod id can only ever occupy one slot on one face at a time, so a face's second-slot mod (modId2) is keyed by its own id exactly like a first-slot one, with nothing to merge.

Changed per file: state.js — gameState.runRecord (started/flushed/source/node/arrivalHpAtBoss/outcome/fightRounds/dieActionEvents) and updateRunRecord(). run-and-map.js — resetRunRecord(), describeSlot(), collectTriggerCountsByMod(), recordFightRoundEnd(), buildRunRecordLine(), RUN_RECORD_CSV_HEADER/RUN_RECORD_STORAGE_KEY, flushRunRecord() (guarded by flushed/started; an abandon mid-fight calls recordFightRoundEnd() once more for the still-unfinished fight before serializing), collectAllRunRecordLines(), copyTextToClipboard()/copyTextToClipboardFallback(); enterSlot() now sets runRecord.started/node on every entry and runRecord.arrivalHpAtBoss the one time laneName is 'boss'; startNewRun() calls resetRunRecord() at its own end (reset only — the New Run click handler is responsible for flushing an abandoned prior run first, not this function). phase-machine.js — both the boss-win and player-death branches in runPhase() now call recordFightRoundEnd(gameState.enemy.id) the instant the fight ends (gameState.turn.round hasn't reset yet at that point) and then flushRunRecord('won'|'lost'); a normal (non-boss) fight win also calls recordFightRoundEnd() but does not flush, since the run continues. rendering.js — dieActionChooseLoad() pushes a `{type:'load', offered, picked:null}` event the moment the offer is shown; dieActionPickLoadFace() patches that same entry's picked field (searching from the end for the most recent unresolved 'load' entry, not assuming it's literally the last one) once a mod is actually committed to a face, in either slot; dieActionChooseSkip() pushes a `{type:'skip'}` event. bootstrap.js — #startGameBtn's click handler now calls flushRunRecord('abandoned') before startNewRun() (a no-op if the run already ended and flushed itself); new #copyRunRecordBtn click handler; new window 'beforeunload' listener calling the same flushRunRecord('abandoned'). index.html — new #copyRunRecordBtn next to the build stamp (wrapped in a new .top-bar-left flex group so #startGameBtn/New Run stays alone on the right, per BUILD 087's own reasoning); build stamp text corrected from STAGE 2.35/BUILD 104 (stuck there since BUILD 104 itself, eleven builds ago) to STAGE 2.41/BUILD 109, matching this build's own stage/build numbers exactly; this is the only change to the stamp element itself, no other presentation change.

tests/facts.test.js: five new tests (28/28, up from 23/23) — an offer and its pick (openDieActionScreen()/dieActionChooseLoad()/dieActionPickMod()/dieActionPickLoadFace() called directly, then dieActionEvents inspected), a Skip recorded as a skip, a mod in a second slot recording its own count (via collectTriggerCountsByMod() after a real forced two-mod roll), an abandoned run keeping its offers (an offer shown via dieActionChooseLoad() but never resolved, then flushRunRecord('abandoned')+startNewRun() called directly rather than via #startGameBtn — that button is deliberately disabled while a die-action panel is open, so the only realistic way this exact shape happens is a closed tab, which calls the identical two functions), and round counts per fight and for the run (14 real nextPhase() calls to drive two full rounds, then a forced win, asserting both fightRounds[0].rounds and buildRunRecordLine()'s totalRounds column against the live turn.round). tests/mods.test.js: unchanged, 20/20 before and after (no mod behaviour touched this build).

VERIFIED, real Chromium via Playwright, one continuous run, zero console/page errors: build stamp read exactly "BUILD 109" with no trace of "BUILD 104". Fight 1 (opening, real win-detection path via runPhase(), not a shortcut) resolved through a genuine Load offer — clicked Load, saw a real 3-mod offer, picked one, picked a pickable face — recording `{type:'load', offered:[...3], picked:<the mod>}`. Fight 2 (dev-jumped to an upper-lane fight, same real win path) resolved via Skip, recording `{type:'skip'}` — dieActionEvents after both fights read exactly `[load(picked), skip]`. Fight 3 (dev-jumped to the boss): entering it alone populated arrivalHpAtBoss (70, the untouched starting HP); winning it flushed automatically. The one stored line's every column was populated (source=human, node=boss, arrivalHpAtBoss=70, outcome=won, fightRounds=3 semicolon-free "label:rounds" entries, totalRounds=3, dieActionEvents holding both the load and the skip, triggerCounts holding at least Consecrate's own entry) — verified both by parsing the raw stored line and by clicking #copyRunRecordBtn and reading the OS clipboard back (Playwright, clipboard permissions granted), which matched collectAllRunRecordLines()'s own output byte-for-byte (Windows normalizes \n to \r\n on the clipboard — an OS quirk accounted for in the comparison, not a bug). Finally, New Run clicked mid-run (after entering a second run's opening fight but before finishing it) produced a second stored line with node=opening, outcome=abandoned, confirming the flush-before-reset ordering works from the real button, not just a direct function call.

Stage 2.38 — thirteenth mod, Elevation (BUILD 114) — landed. One change: 10 damage; then reads the face directly above the triggering face (data.faceNumber + 1) off gameState.die.faces and, only if that face's modId is not null and its number is not 20, calls strengthenFace(aboveNumber) (pipeline.js, the sole function that ever writes face.weight — the same one Strengthen's own die action and Ordain's effect already call) to add +1 weight permanently. If the face above is blank or is face 20, only the 10 damage happens: no weight write, no branch that could error. No registerListener() call — like Smite/Penance/Ordain/Anthem, Elevation fires through the existing MOD_TRIGGER mod_dispatch listener, which already announces the 'mod_trigger' sound and bumps the trigger-count badge for every mod; no new sound was added. PRE-EDIT GREP: 'elevation'/'Elevation' found zero times anywhere in the project outside node_modules before this build — zero collisions on add; POST-EDIT GREP confirmed the only face.weight write site in js/ remains strengthenFace() (pipeline.js) — Elevation adds no second write path. Config: GAME_CONFIG's F24 comment (js/config.js) corrected 12 -> 13; tests/facts.test.js's own F24 assertion updated to match (hardcoded, not GAME_CONFIG-derived — would otherwise have failed at 13 mods). tests/mods.test.js gained one test: loads Elevation on face 2 and Smite on face 3 (the face above), forces face 2 — asserts exactly 10 damage and face 3's weight going 1 -> 2. VERIFIED: node tests/mods.test.js 12/12 before this build's edits, 13/13 after; node tests/facts.test.js 23/23 before and after. Real Chromium via Playwright, four cases, zero console/page errors on every page: (1) Elevation on face 2, Smite (loaded) on face 3 — forced face 2, log/gameState read exactly 10 damage, face 3 weight 1 -> 2; (2) Elevation on face 2, face 3 left blank — forced face 2, exactly 10 damage, face 3 weight unchanged at 1; (3) Elevation on face 19 (face above is face 20) — forced face 19, exactly 10 damage, face 20's weight unchanged; (4) on a fresh page, replicated dieActionChooseLoad()'s own eligible-pool filter directly (DIE_ACTION_EXCLUDED_MOD_IDS, a real top-level const) — the pool held exactly 12 entries (all mods but the excluded anchor Consecrate), Elevation among them.

Stage 2.39 — multi-mod faces (BUILD 115) — landed. Structural change: DIE FACE OBJECT STRUCTURE gained a second field, modId2 (null by default, on every face of both dice, for shape symmetry) — see the MULTI-MOD FACES section (CLAUDE.md) for the full mechanic. PRE-BUILD GREP (per the prompt's explicit gate): `.modId` property reads plus `modId:` object-literal writes came to ~41 sites across 5 files (cards-mods.js, pipeline.js, rendering.js, dev-tools.js, run-and-map.js), not "~20 across six files" as the prompt estimated, and no 6th file (state.js checked, zero hits) carries the token at all. Flagged to Fergus before writing any code; approved to proceed with the full list as an updated count rather than a wrong-pattern signal — every hit was a genuine face-modId read/write in exactly the expected files, consistent with the codebase having grown (Nat 20 enemy mirror, Elevation, hover/trigger-badge rendering) since the ~20 estimate was made. Changed per file: run-and-map.js — both face builders (buildFreshPlayerDieFaces, buildEnemyDieFaces) now push modId2: null. pipeline.js — resolvePlayerRoll()'s MOD_TRIGGER dispatch fires modId then, if present, modId2, both synchronous so each resolves fully before the next; resolveEnemyRoll() left untouched (enemy die never gets a modId2 — see MULTI-MOD FACES). cards-mods.js — onNatTwenty()'s loadedFaces.forEach now dispatches both slots per qualifying face, in the same iteration; ENEMY_NAT_TWENTY's own loop left untouched, same reasoning. dev-tools.js — devLoadMod() now fills modId if blank, else modId2 if empty, else refuses and logs without writing; devClearFace() clears both slots. rendering.js — dieActionChooseLoad()'s loadedModIds now collects from both slots across every face (a mod already on the die in either slot is still excluded from future offers); dieActionPickLoadFace() mirrors devLoadMod()'s same blank-then-second-then-refuse logic; load_pick_face's isEligible only offers an already-loaded face once gameState.die.faces has no blank left, and even then excludes Nat faces and already-full faces; renderDieList()'s modWrap renders a new .die-mod-stack (two stacked .die-mod spans) instead of the single span when both slots are filled, single/blank faces unchanged; faceHoverText() appends the second mod's MOD_DESCRIPTION. index.html — two new CSS rules (.die-mod-stack, .die-mod-stack .die-mod) for the stacked display; every colour/weight rule already keys off the plain .die-mod class selector, so both stacked spans inherit loaded/nat colouring with no new selectors needed. POST-BUILD GREP: every remaining single-modId read (grep -n "modId" js/*.js) was checked by hand; the two genuine single-mod-only sites left unconverted are cards-mods.js's ENEMY_NAT_TWENTY loop and pipeline.js's resolveEnemyRoll() — both deliberate, since no enemy action ever writes an enemy face's modId2, not gaps. tests/mods.test.js: three new tests (16/16, up from 13/13) — order (Fervour loaded first, Smite second, on the same face: 32 damage, proving Fervour's multiplier was registered before Smite's damage was calculated, not just that both fired), the cap (devLoadMod() three times on one face, third refused, first two slots unchanged), and Nat 20 (a two-mod face's both mods — Smite's 16 damage, Blight's 6 poison — fire via the Nat 20 loop). tests/facts.test.js: unchanged, 23/23 before and after. Real Chromium via Playwright, four cases, zero console/page errors on every page: (1) dev-loaded Penance then Sanctuary onto one face via the real devLoadMod() twice, forced it — exactly 8 damage + 24 block (8+16), and the #log's two [MOD] lines read penance before sanctuary, confirming load order from the log itself; (2) forced a Nat 20 with Smite+Blight loaded on one face — exactly 16 damage and 6 poison, both mods fired; (3) devLoadMod() three times on one face (smite, penance, offering) — the third attempt logged "already holds two mods, refused" and left modId/modId2 at smite/penance, unchanged; (4) #playerDieList still rendered exactly 20 .die-row elements with a two-mod face showing a .die-mod-stack of ["Smite", "Penance"], and strengthenFace() on that same face updated its ×N badge to ×2 with the row count still 20 afterward.

Stage 2.40 — per-mod trigger counts (BUILD 108) — landed. Structural change: gameState.die.triggerCounts (BUILD 103's separate array, indexed by face number, fight-scoped) removed entirely; STATE SCHEMA's die block now just `{ faces: [] }`. Counts live in each face's own modData instead — see the TRIGGER COUNTS subsection under MULTI-MOD FACES (CLAUDE.md) for the full mechanic. PRE-BUILD GREP (per the prompt's explicit gate, posted and approved before any edit): `grep -rn "triggerCounts" js/*.js` — 7 sites across 4 files (cards-mods.js, rendering.js, run-and-map.js, state.js); `grep -rn "modData" js/*.js` — 5 sites across 2 files (cards-mods.js, state.js). In line with expectations, no stop needed on the count itself, but two things were flagged and approved before writing code: (1) the old array was reset in BOTH clearFightScopedState() AND startNewRun() — the fight-scoped reset had to be dropped entirely, not just moved; (2) Zeal's own effect (cards-mods.js) replaced modData wholesale (`{ modData: { accumulatedBonus } }`), which would silently erase a face's trigger count the moment Zeal fired on it, so that line needed a merge fix, not just the count logic. Changed per file: state.js — removed die.triggerCounts from the schema, updated the comment. run-and-map.js — clearFightScopedState() no longer resets any trigger-count state at all (gameState.die is now untouched by a fight reset); startNewRun()'s updateDie() call dropped its triggerCounts argument, since buildFreshPlayerDieFaces()'s faces already carry no modData, which is what wipes both the counts and Zeal's own accumulatedBonus on a brand new run. cards-mods.js — mod_dispatch now reads the triggering face, matches data.modId against face.modId/face.modId2, and merges triggerCount or triggerCount2 (whichever slot matched) into the face's existing modData before calling the mod's effect(); Zeal's effect fixed to merge into face.modData rather than replace it, and its own bonus read changed from `face.modData ? face.modData.accumulatedBonus : 0` to `(face.modData && face.modData.accumulatedBonus) || 0` — the former started returning NaN the moment modData became non-null-but-accumulatedBonus-less (mod_dispatch's own new write), caught by tests/mods.test.js's existing Zeal test failing immediately after the mod_dispatch change, fixed in the same build. rendering.js — the trigger-count badge block now reads face.modData.triggerCount/triggerCount2 instead of gameState.die.triggerCounts[...]; a one-mod face shows the same single ↻N as before, a two-mod face shows ↻N1/N2 (shown whenever at least one is nonzero, so a not-yet-triggered mod reads as ↻0 beside a triggered sibling rather than looking like missing data). tests/build104_verify.js (not a gating test, approved to fix as a courtesy) updated to set modData.triggerCount directly instead of a second triggerCounts array. POST-BUILD GREP: `grep -rn "triggerCounts" js/*.js` — 5 hits remain, all inside comments explaining the removed field for future readers (cards-mods.js:574, rendering.js:417, run-and-map.js:172/203, state.js:107), zero in live code; two more comment-only hits in tests/ (mods.test.js:395, build104_verify.js:26). tests/mods.test.js: four new tests (20/20, up from 16/16) — isolation (dispatching MOD_TRIGGER for only one of a two-mod face's mods, directly through the real mod_dispatch hook, confirms only that mod's own counter moves), fight-reset survival (resetFight() + startFreshTurnPaused(), both counts unchanged), new-run wipe (startNewRun(), face.modData becomes undefined), and Nat 20 (both counts on a two-mod face increment by exactly one each via the sweep). tests/facts.test.js: unchanged, 23/23 before and after. Real Chromium via Playwright, three cases, zero console/page errors on every page: (1) a two-mod face (Smite/Blight) fired twice across two real rounds — modData read {triggerCount:1,triggerCount2:1} after the first fire, {triggerCount:2,triggerCount2:2} after the second, confirming both counts move independently and correctly across a full phase cycle, not just a single forced roll; (2) badge rendering — a two-mod face (non-damage mods, to avoid ending the fight mid-test) fired twice read ↻2/2, a one-mod face fired three times read ↻3, the same single-count format as before this build; (3) #playerDieList still rendered exactly 20 .die-row elements after firing a two-mod face. One flakiness note from writing the Playwright script itself, not a product bug: driving several rounds fast enough to outrun the fight-start autoAdvance() timer risks the exact stale-timer race BUILD 105's autoplay.js already documented and fixed — worked around here the same way, by setting devPauseBeforeFirstRoll before the fight starts and driving every round explicitly via nextPhase().

Stage 2.42 — three fixes in one pass (BUILD 116) — landed. PRE-BUILD GREP (per the prompt's explicit gate, posted and approved before any edit): every site matched expectations exactly — one die-row renderer, renderDieList() (rendering.js:266), called from 6 sites; one weight-badge render site and one trigger-badge render site, both inside it; one dieActionChooseLoad() definition; the second-slot gate living in renderDieActionPanel()'s 'load_pick_face' branch (anyBlankFaceExists, not inside dieActionChooseLoad() itself, as a careful reading of the prompt's own phrasing anticipated). No discrepancy, no stop needed. Fix 1 — trigger badge: the text itself changed from '↻N' ('↻N1/N2' for a two-mod face) to '#N'/'#N1/N2' (rendering.js) — '↻' reads as a reload/refresh icon to most players, not "count of," and collides visually with nothing else in the row, while '#' is unambiguous and distinct from the weight badge's own '×N' beside it. .die-trigger-count's font-size bumped 14px -> 17px (index.html). MEASURED BUG FOUND AND FIXED: the 17px bump alone, with no explicit line-height, made that one face's row 20px tall against every other row's 19px (confirmed via Playwright getBoundingClientRect() on all twenty rows) — the font's default line-height at 17px produces a ~20.4px line box, taller than the row's fixed allocation, and nothing was overriding it (unlike .face-btn and .die-mod-stack .die-mod, which both already set an explicit line-height for exactly this reason). Fixed with `line-height: 1` on .die-trigger-count — re-measured, every row back to a uniform 19px. This is the kind of thing the prompt's own "stop and report if it would push the die column wider or taller" line exists to catch, and it was caught and fixed before this build closed, not reported as a blocker. Fix 2 — weight on the face itself: `.face-weight-fill` (index.html), a bottom-anchored div appended inside each face-btn (rendering.js) when weight > 1, height set per-element via inline style to `(weight - 1) * 25%`, capped at weight 5 (100%) — weight 2/3/4/5+ render as 25%/50%/75%/100%. `background: currentColor` at `opacity: 0.32` — currentColor resolves to whichever identity colour already governs that face-btn's `color` (--blank/--nat/--player-mod/--enemy-mod, via the existing .die-row.* selectors), so no new colour was introduced; only the fill's presence/height (emphasis) carries the weight signal, exactly per the prompt's "colour carries identity... motion or emphasis carries state." face-btn gained `position: relative; overflow: hidden` as the fill's anchor/clip, and the bare number text (previously face-btn's own textContent) moved into a child `<span class="face-num">` (`position: relative; z-index: 1`) so the digit stays legible above the fill at every weight — face-btn's own outer box (34px, row height) is completely unchanged, so this changes no row's size. A weight-1 face (the untouched majority) gets no fill element at all, same "nothing extra at the default" convention the badges already use. Fix 3 — second-slot gate removed: load_pick_face's isEligible (renderDieActionPanel(), rendering.js) collapsed from a two-branch anyBlankFaceExists check to one condition, `f.modId !== 'NAT_ONE' && f.modId !== 'NAT_TWENTY' && !f.modId2` — a blank face (modId null) already satisfies this trivially, so no separate blank-vs-loaded branching is needed; the cap (`!f.modId2`) and the Nat exclusion are both unchanged, and the anchor was never offered by dieActionChooseLoad() in the first place (DIE_ACTION_EXCLUDED_MOD_IDS), untouched by this fix. dieActionPickLoadFace()'s own blank-then-second-then-refuse write logic needed no change at all — it already handled both cases correctly; only which faces the picker offers changed. tests/facts.test.js: three new tests (31/31, up from 28/28) — a Load offer includes an already-loaded face while blanks still exist (loads one mod onto face 2, opens a real Load offer, confirms face 2's row is `die-row-pickable`), the cap still refuses a third mod (face 2 loaded in both slots, confirms `die-row-pick-inert`), and the offer still excludes the anchor with a realistic mixed loaded/blank die (not just the fresh starting die F13 already covers). tests/mods.test.js: unchanged, 20/20 before and after (no mod behaviour touched). VERIFIED, real Chromium via Playwright, zero console/page errors: set up a worst-case die simultaneously — face 5 (Smite, weight 4, 12 triggers), face 8 (Consecrate+Virulence, two mods, 3/11 triggers), face 12 (Blight, weight 2), face 15 (Penance, weight 5) — and confirmed the die column still renders exactly 20 rows. Geometry: every row except face 8 (a PRE-EXISTING BUILD 115 two-mod-stack height outlier, confirmed unchanged against a pre-BUILD-116 backup — not something this build introduced or was asked to fix, called out rather than silently left) measured an identical 19px height; every face-btn measured an identical 34px width — no row grew, no column widened, from this build's own changes. Zero clipped text (scrollWidth vs clientWidth) on any of the worst-case rows. Content: face 5 read '#12' / '×4' / a 75% fill; face 8 read '#3/11' with no weight badge or fill (weight 1); face 12 read '×2' / a 25% fill, no trigger badge; face 2 (untouched) showed nothing extra at all. Two screenshots (tests/build116_die_column.png — full fight screen; tests/build116_die_column_zoom.png — the die column alone at 2x scale) visually confirm the fill is plainly visible (face 15's box reads noticeably more filled than face 10's) and both badges read clearly without hovering. Separately: reset to a die with one loaded (one-slot) face and blanks remaining, opened a real Load offer — confirmed that already-loaded face was `die-row-pickable` while `gameState.die.faces.some(f => f.modId === null)` was still true; picked it (face now holds two mods); opened Load again and confirmed that same, now-full face was `die-row-pick-inert` — the cap still holds.

Stage 2.43 — three die-row fixes plus one investigation (BUILD 117) — landed. PRE-BUILD GREP (per the prompt's explicit gate, posted and approved before any edit): the BUILD 115 two-mod stack (rendering.js's modId2/modStack lines, index.html's .die-mod-stack rules), the BUILD 116 weight fill (.face-weight-fill, fillPct), MOD_DESCRIPTION and its two readers (faceHoverText(), the Load-offer button tooltip), and every dieActionEvents write/flush site — all matched expectations exactly, no discrepancy. Fix 1 — two mods side by side: replaces BUILD 115's vertical `.die-mod-stack` (a real ~28px-vs-19px row-height violation) with `.die-mod-pair`, both names on the row's one line, each with its own trigger count immediately beside it (`.die-trigger-count-inline`) instead of a shared far-right "#N1/N2" badge with no visual link to either mod. MEASURED BUG FOUND AND FIXED, the hard part of this fix: shrinking the two names' font below .die-mod's own base 16px (tried 13px, tried every combination of display/line-height/inline-block) reproducibly shrank that one row to 17-18px against every other row's 19px — real layout behaviour, confirmed with a raw-DOM isolation harness (a dozen minimal test cases swapping one CSS property at a time) that the die column's total height in real layout is effectively summed from each row's own content rather than a fixed total flex-grow evenly divides, so a smaller font on just one row measurably shrinks it. Fix: keep the exact same 16px `.die-mod` already uses everywhere, and fit two names into the row's existing width with a fixed `max-width: 44px` + ellipsis truncation instead of a smaller font — confirmed via Playwright against the worst-case pairing (Consecrate + Virulence, the anchor's own name plus the longest pool name) in both the wide main die column and the much narrower #dieActionDieList (220px), zero row-height regression in either. A second real bug the same verification pass caught: the gap before the second mod's name used `.die-mod-pair .die-mod + .die-mod` (adjacent-sibling), which silently stopped working the moment the first mod had a nonzero trigger count (a `.die-trigger-count-inline` span then sits between the two names, breaking direct adjacency) — fixed with an explicit `.die-mod-second` class set in JS rather than a structural CSS selector. The full mod name is still always available via the row's own hover-tip (unchanged), so truncating the visible label removes no information, only shortens its on-row display, per the prompt's own instruction. Fix 2 — weight visible from weight 2: weight 2 previously rendered a 25%-tall fill at 0.32 opacity — a ~5px sliver at the very bottom of a 19px button, confirmed (not assumed) indistinguishable from weight 1 at a glance. Fixed two ways: the fill-percentage floor for weight 2 raised from 25% to 40% (curve now 40/60/80/100% for weight 2/3/4/5+, rendering.js), and `.face-weight-fill`'s opacity raised 0.32 -> 0.5 (index.html) — same `currentColor` mechanism as BUILD 116, no new colour. Verified with real pixel-level screenshot comparison (Buffer.compare() on a cropped face-btn screenshot, not just computed CSS), confirming weight 1 vs weight 2 and weight 2 vs weight 4 are genuinely different pixels, plus a visual screenshot review. Fix 3 — mod hover text: Elevation had none, as reported, but the prompt's own "check every other mod" instruction caught a second, unreported gap — Anthem was missing too, since neither mod's own build (113/114) had added a MOD_DESCRIPTION entry. Both added now, worded from each mod's own log() line, matching every existing entry's convention. Verified programmatically (`Object.keys(gameState.config.mods).filter(id => !MOD_DESCRIPTION[id])` reads empty) and by actually opening a Load offer for all twelve reward-eligible mods and reading each one's real hover-tip text back.
