A full fight played to a win through the real UI (6 rounds) produced 44 total sound events, fight_won exactly once, fight_lost zero times, no duplicate or orphaned firings. Zero console errors, zero page errors. Build stamp reads STAGE 2.25 / BUILD 094.

Stage 2.24 — three independent UI/audio items (BUILD 093) — landed, verified and reported separately per the prompt. ITEM 1: the rolled-face highlight's re-trigger bug is fixed — diagnosis confirmed first (renderDieList() rebuilds every row from scratch every render, so any class carrying a CSS animation always restarted it, even on an unrelated re-render like loading a mod mid-turn). .die-row-rolled is now the sustained no-animation class and .die-row-rolled-flash the animated one (blanks keep only .die-row-rolled-blank-flash, no sustained equivalent); renderDieList() tracks a per-container committed roll signature and only applies -flash when it differs from the last commit, deferring the commit itself via queueMicrotask() so a roll's whole synchronous resolution cascade (multiple renders from one roll, e.g. Consecrate's own soul grant) still flashes correctly rather than the flash being consumed by the first of several cascaded renders. No indent/wash/colour/timing values changed. ITEM 2: js/audio.js — one AudioContext + a SOUND_TABLE (same shape as the listener registry) mapping 'roll'/'roll_blank' to two short synthesised square-wave clicks (RuneScape-reference, under 200ms). Game logic never calls a sound function directly — resolvePlayerRoll() (pipeline.js) announces playAudioEvent('roll'|'roll_blank') at the same funnel the highlight uses. unlockAudioOnce() resumes the AudioContext on the page's first pointerdown ({once:true}, self-removing) per browser autoplay policy. A mute checkbox (default unmuted) sits in the existing DEV SETUP bar. No audio files — /audio/ stays empty. ITEM 3: die display order flipped to NAT 20 top / NAT 1 bottom by iterating faces.slice().reverse() in renderDieList() — the real faces array is never reordered, only the DOM append order, so every row still closes over its real face object and face.number; applies to player die, enemy die, and both map preview dice via the one shared function. Verified in real windowed Chromium via Playwright, three separate fresh file:// page loads (no server), one per item: Item 1 — flash on first roll, held (not re-flashed) across two separate mod-load re-renders, re-flashes on a genuine new roll, blank flashes once and goes fully plain after a later re-render — all six checks true. Item 2 — AudioContext running immediately after the first real click; non-blank and blank rolls invoke different SOUND_TABLE branches and each create exactly one oscillator; muted, the event still fires but creates zero oscillators. Item 3 — all four die displays read 20-to-1 top-to-bottom; clicking a Load-picker row displaying face 17 (sitting at an unrelated DOM position, to rule out a position-based mistake) logged 'loaded ... onto face 17' exactly, same for Strengthen, and hover text matched the clicked row's own face in both cases. Zero console/page errors across all three runs. Build stamp reads STAGE 2.24 / BUILD 093.

Stage 2.23 — three engine fixes carried over from the BUILD 091 code audit (BUILD 092) — landed; no new features, no numbers, no visual changes, plumbing for the systems work after this. registerListener() (listener-registry.js) now dedups on same hook + same id, logging '[LISTENER] duplicate registration skipped: HOOK ID' and skipping the push rather than silently accumulating — no existing call site touched, since all eight real ones already use stable unique ids. healPlayer(amount) + a new ON_HEAL hook added to pipeline.js (direct Math.min(hp+amount, maxHp) clamp, deliberately no multiplier stage per the prompt); riteChooseHeal() (rendering.js) now calls it instead of a bare updatePlayer({hp:...}), same 20 HP, same cap, same '[RITE] healed N HP (before to after)' log line, now observable via ON_HEAL. clearListeners('fight') deleted from its only two call sites (clearFightScopedState()/startNewRun() in run-and-map.js) — both were always dead no-ops, since grep across every registerListener() call confirmed only 'permanent'/'turn' are ever really passed as clearOn; clearListeners('turn') is untouched and still live. Verification only, no code change: registered a throwaway BLOCK_MULTIPLIER listener (×3) inside a disposable Playwright page.evaluate(), confirmed dealBlock(10,...) goes 10 → 30 → 10 (baseline / multiplied / after removing the throwaway listener) — generateBlock()'s BLOCK_MULTIPLIER stage works correctly when something registers against it, which is what Obelisk's design needs; the throwaway registration was never added to any file. Verified in real windowed Chromium via Playwright, file:// direct: build stamp reads STAGE 2.23 / BUILD 092; the dedup guard confirmed skipping a genuine duplicate hook+id registration (array stays length 1, fires once); a full fight played to a win (7 rounds) through the real UI, then die action Skip + card reward Skip back to the map; hp lowered by 15, then a real rite Heal 20 HP click landed correctly (47→67) with the same log line as before, and a throwaway ON_HEAL spy confirmed the heal actually dispatches through the hook. Zero console errors, zero page errors. Build stamp now STAGE 2.23 / BUILD 092.

Stage 2.22 — the file split: nine files under /js/ loaded via ordinary `<script src>` tags, replacing index.html's single ~3,217-line inline script (BUILD 091) — landed; structural only, zero behaviour/logic/number/visual changes, verified by playing a full fight through the real UI after the split. Split: state.js, listener-registry.js, pipeline.js, cards-mods.js (init() kept intact — cards/mods/classes aren't separable files in this codebase, they're all built inline in one function), run-and-map.js, phase-machine.js, rendering.js (log/refreshInspector/every render*/the three screen-flow state machines/the map renderer), dev-tools.js, bootstrap.js (every addEventListener wiring + the sole DOMContentLoaded->init kickoff). A static scan confirmed all 109 original top-level names land in exactly one new file each — zero missing, zero duplicated, none introduced. Ordering trap (item 4a) resolved explicitly: init() is never called eagerly anywhere, the only kickoff is bootstrap.js's DOMContentLoaded listener, and a full re-read confirmed the ONLY top-level immediately-executing statements in the whole original script are self-contained data literals and addEventListener registrations (whose bodies defer to click-time) — so every cross-file function-body reference (including the original dieActionStep case, state.js's helpers calling rendering.js's refreshInspector(), and a genuine rendering.js/run-and-map.js circularity) is safe regardless of file order, because nothing runs until every file has loaded. Chosen order is dependency-first anyway, for readability. Code audit (reported, not fixed, per the prompt): registerListener has no dedup (still live), healPlayer/ON_HEAL don't exist (still live, riteChooseHeal() heals via a bare updatePlayer() call), clearOn 'fight' is registered nowhere and stays dead code (still live), and dealBlock's "no multiplier stage" finding is now stale — generateBlock() has a full BLOCK_MULTIPLIER stage structurally, just presently unexercised by any mod. /audio/ and /art/ created, both empty. Verified in real Chromium via Playwright opening file:///.../index.html directly (no server): zero failed requests, build stamp reads STAGE 2.22 / BUILD 091, the BUILD 090 rolled-face highlight still works unchanged, and a full fight (roll → play cards → End Turn ×4 rounds → win → die action Skip → card reward Skip → back to map) completed with zero console errors, zero warnings, zero page errors. Build stamp now STAGE 2.22 / BUILD 091.

Stage 2.21 — the rolled-face highlight on the player's die column (BUILD 090) — landed; presentation plus one small piece of state, no combat logic/numbers/face behaviour touched. gameState.turn.rolledFaceNumber is the new field (initial schema, startNewRun(), resolvePlayerRoll(), and the existing START_OF_TURN clear alongside rollOutcome/rolledFaceWeight) — deliberately a gameState property rather than a module-scope variable, so it doesn't repeat the BUILD 078 riteStep lesson about module-scope flow state going stale across resets/dev-jumps. renderDieList() adds 'die-row-rolled' (flash to a sustained gold wash + slight right indent, holds for the rest of the turn) or 'die-row-rolled-blank' (a quiet dim --blank-coloured pulse that fades to nothing, no indent, no gold) to the row matching rolledFaceNumber, only on #playerDieList and #dieActionDieList — never the enemy die or the map's static preview. No outline anywhere (BUILD 080's lesson) — only transform and background, so text colour (.loaded/.nat-one/.nat-twenty) is never touched and box model/row width never changes. Confirmed the rolled state and the BUILD 079 picker's pickable state coexist on the same row (different classes, different CSS properties, both applied independently). Verified in real windowed Chromium via Playwright (now installed persistently in the project per Fergus's request): exactly one row highlighted per roll, sustained through CARD_PHASE, cleared before the next roll, blank rolls get the quiet variant only, row widths identical (474px) before/after, and a screenshotted picker row showing both the rolled wash and the pickable gold frame at once. Zero console/page errors. Build stamp now STAGE 2.21 / BUILD 090.

Stage 2.20 — the class card can now be removed at a rite; one restriction dropped (BUILD 089) — landed; ruling 7 (unique class cards cannot be discarded) protects against the in-fight discard mechanic (Shove/Crush/Take It — none of which exist in the codebase yet, grep-confirmed) where losing the class card mid-turn is an accident, not against a rite's own deliberate, between-fights removal choice. riteRemoveCard()'s classRestriction early-out and the picker's matching btn.disabled line are both gone — every owned card, Rite included, now removes exactly the same way (splice from ownedCards/deck/discard/hand, same log line, same closeRiteScreen()+advanceRun() close). The .die-action-row button:disabled CSS rule itself was left in place, per the prompt, even with no current caller. Checked and reported rather than assumed: nothing else in the file reads the class card's presence — Orison reads only rollOutcome, startingDeck is a static array read once by startNewRun() and never touches ownedCards, and the discard mechanic ruling 7 actually governs isn't built yet — so nothing else needed changing. Verified via a live jsdom drive of the real index.html: the picker rendered all 10 owned cards enabled including Rite, clicking Rite dropped ownedCards to 9 with the id gone from deck/discard/hand and the rite advancing normally; separately rebuilt a fresh 9-card deck from the post-removal ownedCards and ran drawCards(1) through 40 consecutive draw/discard/reshuffle cycles with zero exceptions and 'rite' never resurfacing. Removing a non-class card re-tested as byte-for-byte identical to BUILD 088. Zero JS errors. Build stamp now STAGE 2.20 / BUILD 089.

Stage 2.19 — a third rite option, Remove a card, alongside Heal and Take a die action (BUILD 088) — landed; the rite panel's #riteScreenPanel gained a second internal step (riteStep 'remove_pick_card', same choose-then-advance shape dieActionStep's own load_pick_mod/strengthen_pick_face sub-steps already use) rendering the player's full ownedCards collection as a picker, one button per card, reusing the exact per-card render shape (name + cost, CARD_EFFECT_TEXT hover-tip) renderCardRewardPanel() already builds. Rite (classRestriction: 'ordained') renders visibly disabled per ruling 7 (unique class cards cannot be discarded) — a new .die-action-row button:disabled CSS rule, plus a matching guard in riteRemoveCard() itself as the actual source of truth. Picking a card is the commit point: one updatePlayer() call splices it out of ownedCards and out of deck/discard/hand if present, logs '[RITE] removed CardName from deck, deck now N cards', then calls closeRiteScreen() and advanceRun() exactly as riteChooseHeal() already does — no back button anywhere, matching Heal/Take a die action's own irreversible-on-click behaviour. Verified via a live jsdom drive of the real index.html: dev-jumped to a rite, all three buttons render, Remove a card opened a 10-card picker with Rite disabled and every other card enabled, clicking a Strike button dropped ownedCards from 10 to 9 (the other four Strikes untouched), removed the id from deck too, closed the rite, and advanced run.currentSlot to the next slot — zero JS errors, and Heal/Take a die action re-confirmed byte-for-byte unchanged. Not verified this session: a real windowed browser (jsdom only, no Playwright/Chromium available), and an actual subsequent fight's hand/discard watched over several turns to visually confirm the removed card never reappears — confirmed instead by direct inspection that the id is gone from ownedCards/deck/discard/hand immediately after removal, which is what every later draw/shuffle reads from. Build stamp now STAGE 2.19 / BUILD 088.

Stage 2.18 — New Run moved away from End Turn and gated behind a confirm while a run is live (BUILD 087) — landed; presentation and one safety check only, no run logic changed, per the prompt. Motivated by Fergus's own playtest: New Run and End Turn sat side by side as visually identical buttons, and since New Run discards the current run permanently (permadeath), a misclick mid-run was unrecoverable. #startGameBtn moved out of .controls-buttons (which now holds only End Turn) into a new .top-bar div next to the build stamp at the very top of the page, restyled small and red-bordered (11px/red text/red border) rather than sharing End Turn's big 17px gold primary-action look — different position and different styling, not just a spacing nudge; the old shared `#startGameBtn, #endTurnBtn` CSS rule was split in two so End Turn's own look, disabled state, and click handler are byte-for-byte untouched. The safety check: the click handler now reads gameState.run.outcome (the same field enterSlot()/devJumpToSlot() already gate on) and, only when it's 'active', calls the native confirm('Abandon this run and start a new one?') before doing anything else — declining logs '[CLICK] New Run — cancelled at confirm' and returns with zero state touched; accepting, or a run that's already 'won'/'lost', falls straight through to the original '[CLICK] New Run' + startNewRun() exactly as BUILD 086 had it. confirm() was chosen over a second-click toggle per the prompt's own "whichever is less code" — one added conditional and two log lines, no new state, no second render pass. Re-read the new .top-bar markup/CSS, the trimmed .controls-buttons, the split button rules, and the click handler after editing — all landed as intended, and node --check equivalent (new Function() over the extracted inline script) passed with zero syntax errors. Not verified this session: the actual confirm() dialog and the visual separation were not eyeballed in a rendered browser (Playwright/Chromium unavailable), so the guard is verified by reading its logic against gameState.run.outcome's three possible values rather than a click-through. Build stamp now STAGE 2.18 / BUILD 087.

Stage 2.17 — the elite intent band drops from 12-22 to 10-18, average 14 (BUILD 086) — landed; one number pair in buildAct()'s upper-lane elite slot, same shape as BUILD 085, no new logic and no new systems. Motivated by BUILD 085 leaving the run's threat curve backwards: moving the boss to 10-20 (average 15) put it below the elite's still-12-22 (average 17), so the mid-run fight read as tougher on paper than the final one; Fergus's call was 10-18 for the elite (average 14), between normal fights' 12 and the boss's 15, restoring the intended climb. Confirmed there is no separate elite intent preview on the map to route — the only rendered intent preview in the file is #bossPreview (boss-only) — so this was a single-site buildAct() edit with no second reader to check; the in-fight roll (the same inline formula in runPhase()'s START_OF_TURN branch BUILD 085 already confirmed reads off gameState.enemy.intentMin/intentMax) needed no change. Brought the same multi-line comment above buildAct() BUILD 085 had already corrected for the boss up to date for the elite too, rather than letting it go stale the way it briefly did last build. Re-read buildAct() and its comment, and grep-confirmed the elite's HP (100), reward (dieActionsRemaining set to 2, gated on wonSlot.label === 'Elite'), and position (fourth slot, upper lane) are untouched — only intentMin/intentMax changed on that one object. Verified: the extracted inline script parses with zero syntax errors, and the exact roll formula plus all four exact numeric bands as they appear in the file were reproduced verbatim in a standalone Node script and rolled 10,000 times each with zero results outside 10-20 (boss), 10-18 (elite), 6-18 (normal), or 4-12 (opening). Not verified this session, same caveat as BUILD 085: an actual rendered browser dev-jump to the elite with ten-plus live rounds watched in a window — Playwright/Chromium was not available in this session's environment, so the ten-round confirmation rests on the formula/data match above, not an eyeballed run. Build stamp now STAGE 2.17 / BUILD 086.

Stage 2.16 — the boss intent band drops from 16-28 to 10-20, average 15 (BUILD 085) — landed; one number pair in buildAct()'s boss slot, no new logic and no new systems, per the prompt. Motivated by three full played runs all dying to the boss on arithmetic — roughly 15 points of cards a turn spent as either damage or block, never both, against a boss averaging 22 under the old 16-28 band; the recorded position is that the boss should threaten by its distribution, not its intent number, and its die stays inert this build. Confirmed both the map's boss preview and the in-fight intent roll already read off the slot config with no competing literal anywhere else in the file (grep-confirmed no other 16 or 28 in the codebase touches intent) — this was a single-site data edit, not a routing fix. Also found and corrected a stale multi-line comment directly above buildAct() that still described the boss band as "16-28 — unchanged this build" from an earlier point in this build's own edit history, after the data had already been changed to 10/20 but before the comment was; the comment and the data no longer disagree. Re-read buildAct(), the map preview, beginFightFromSlot(), and the intent-roll formula after editing — opening 50 HP/4-12, normals 70/78/85 at 6-18, elite 100 HP/12-22, boss 100 HP/10-20/hasDie true, all byte-identical to BUILD 084 apart from the one number pair. Verified: the extracted inline script parses with zero syntax errors (new Function() over the whole script block), and the exact roll formula plus the exact numeric bands as they appear in the file were reproduced verbatim in a standalone Node script and rolled 10,000 times per band with zero results outside 10-20 (boss), 12-22 (elite), 6-18 (normal), or 4-12 (opening). Not verified this session, and flagged rather than assumed: an actual rendered browser click-through (fresh map showing "Intent 10-20", a real dev-jump to the boss, ten-plus live rounds watched in a window) — Playwright/Chromium was not available in this session's environment, so the ten-round confirmation rests on the formula/data match above, not an eyeballed run. Build stamp now STAGE 2.16 / BUILD 085.

Stage 2.15 — Nat 1 once per fight, Penitence 3 turns (BUILD 084) — landed and verified in a real browser, not only headless jsdom: Playwright driving the full Chromium build against index.html over file://, 20 checks, zero console errors and zero page errors, with a screenshot confirming the stamp, the dev chrome, penitence in Active debuffs and three ascending Nat 20 passes in the log. Both of Fergus's rulings from the BUILD 082 played run are in: face 1 fires once per fight and then resolves as an ordinary blank (2 block) for the rest of it, and Penitence runs exactly 3 turns from onset rather than the rest of the fight. The dead second Nat 1 that prompted the ruling is gone — it now pays out like any other blank, reads that way in the log, and as of this session reads that way to cards too. Resolved from the last session's open question: rollOutcome on a spent Nat 1 is now 'blank', not 'nat_one'. Orison confirmed both ways in the browser and in jsdom — 5 damage on the fight's first face 1, 9 damage and the "blank roll" log branch on the second. The assignment in resolvePlayerRoll() is a single ternary on natOneFiredThisFight, and it is correct only because of ordering: the flag is still false when rollOutcome is computed on the first face 1 (onNatOne() sets it later, during the dispatch), so the outcome string tracks onNatOne()'s own branch exactly. Nothing else in the file read the string 'nat_one' — the assignment was its only occurrence — so Orison is the only behaviour this touched. Two other design points worth confirming. (1) The once-per-fight guard reads natOneFiredThisFight, not penitenceActive. Those are now different things: Penitence expires, the spent Nat 1 does not, so a face 1 rolled on turn 9 of a fight whose Penitence ended on turn 4 still comes out a blank instead of re-arming — verified in the browser. (2) The timing works out as three soul deductions because onset happens in ROLL_PHASE, after that turn's tick has already run, so the ticks land on the three START_OF_TURNs after onset, the third both deducts and clears, and the fourth is clean. That matches the success criterion exactly, but it is worth stating because "3 turns from onset" could also have meant the onset turn counts as one. Not taken literally, and flagged for that reason: the prompt said to log the onset line as it currently does, but that line stated "for the rest of this fight", which the build makes false. It now reads "for the next 3 turns", and the player-facing face-1 hover (NAT_DESCRIPTION.NAT_ONE) was reworded the same way. Both are one-line reverts if the original wording was wanted. Untouched, per the prompt: Nat 20 (uncapped — verified firing on three consecutive passes in ascending face order), the Strengthen-on-face-20 rule (verified through the real picker — face 20 plus every loaded face, never face 1), the mod pool, the rest of the card pool, dealDamage/dealBlock, the boss intent band (BUILD 085), and the BUILD 083 map/dev-chrome work. Needs Fergus's own edit, since Claude Code must not rewrite these sections: the STATE SCHEMA section does not list penitenceActive, penitenceTurnsRemaining or natOneFiredThisFight on player, and the NAT 20 AND NAT 1 — THE ORDAINED section still describes Nat 1 as "for the rest of this fight" with no once-per-fight rule and no note that a spent Nat 1 reads as a blank to cards.

Stage 2.14 — all dev chrome behind one toggle, closed by default, and the setup pause made optional (BUILD 083) — just landed, verified in headless jsdom (a 29-check drive of every stated success criterion, plus a full run played start to finish with the chrome closed and Begin Roll never clicked: 21 rolls, natural loss, zero console errors); not yet eyeballed by a human in a real windowed browser. This closes the two dev-tool hazards named in the prompt. The 4 September false bug report — a dev Next Phase click during CARD_PHASE discarding a hand — is now unreachable by accident, because the whole DEV STEP row is not rendered until someone deliberately opens the dev chrome. And the BUILD 036/037 setup pause, which fired at the start of every fight in a real run and left it waiting indefinitely for Next Phase or Begin Roll, is off by default: init(), New Run, every map slot entry and Restart Fight now run START_OF_TURN and go straight into ROLL_PHASE using the same autoAdvance() every later round already uses. Ticking "Pause before first roll" restores BUILD 037 exactly. One shared entry path either way — startFreshTurnPaused() is still the single function all four routes reach, gated on the checkbox in one line rather than forked. The two dev inputs that live outside the container are inert while it is closed (die-face force rolls, and the BUILD 076 map dev-jump nodes, which render fully disabled the way they already do once a run is won or lost); the BUILD 079 Load/Strengthen die-row picker is player-facing and untouched, and still works with the chrome closed. Not changed, and worth stating because the prompt drew the line there: ROLL_PHASE timing (ROLL_PHASE_PAUSE_MS still 1800), enemy numbers, the player die, the card and mod pools, run state, slot dispatch, the map's real node logic, the phase order, and the hover system. Open for the next session: the dev chrome is hidden with display:none rather than removed from the DOM — the elements still exist and refreshInspector() still writes the inspector's JSON into a hidden <pre> every time it runs, which is what lets the State Inspector toggle and every dev button keep working unchanged when the chrome is opened. Nothing a player can click or focus, but not literally absent either; if "not rendered" is later meant literally, refreshInspector() would need an early-out for the hidden inspector and the dev-button disabled lines would need guarding.

Stage 2.13 — the act grows from three lane slots to five, and the elite now grants two die actions instead of one (BUILD 082) — just landed, verified two ways: a full headless jsdom drive playing both lanes start to finish (real click path for upper, dev-jump for lower), confirming the act's shape, HP progression, elite reward count, and that both lanes correctly reach the boss after their fifth slot rather than early; and, separately, an actual Playwright/Chromium screenshot of the rendered map at both 1400x900 and 1600x1080 — 13 nodes (Start, opening, 5+5 lane slots, boss), centred to within 0px, no overflow, connections/fork/merge legible, boss preview and both dice unchanged — the first build since BUILD 076 to get real eyes-on visual confirmation instead of code-reasoning alone. Confirmed first (as asked) that the elite previously granted nothing beyond a normal fight's single reward, since SLOT_HANDLERS dispatches by type only, never by label. Found and fixed a real bug this change would otherwise have exposed: advanceRun() had a hardcoded `index < 2` bound left over from the old three-slot lane, which would have sent a five-slot lane to the boss after only three slots — now reads the lane's own live length instead of a second magic number. Map rendering and dev-jump needed no changes at all, both already fully dynamic over whatever length gameState.run.act's lanes happen to be. Next up: going back to actually eyeball BUILD 077 through 081 now that Playwright/Chromium is confirmed working (all shipped on code-reasoning/jsdom alone) would close that remaining gap; beyond that, the manual human playtest pass (Stages 2.0 through 2.13), the BUILD 074 open question about the original opening-fight bug report, and the BUILD 071 enemy_buff_poison gap.

Stage 2.12 — widened the Load/Strengthen die-row picker so the longest mod name plus a ×N badge no longer clips (BUILD 081), one CSS rule — just landed, and unlike the last two builds this one WAS visually/numerically verified against a real render: standalone Playwright/Chromium (installed via npm this session, separate from the claude-in-chrome extension declined earlier) measured Consecrate (longest mod name, ~88px at the row's actual 16px Consolas font) plus a two-digit ×N badge (~26px) needing ~122px inside .die-mod-wrap, found the container was only rendering ~134px total despite already declaring max-width: 260px (an auto-sized ancestor flex context was shrink-wrapping it well below that ceiling), added min-width: 220px to force the room open, then re-measured with getBoundingClientRect() to confirm zero clipping and all twenty rows uniformly 220px wide. Fight screen (#playerDieList/#enemyDieList) and map (#mapPlayerDieList/#bossDiePreviewList) die rows use their own separate ID selectors, confirmed not shared with #dieActionDieList, so per the prompt's own instruction they were correctly left untouched. Re-ran the standing jsdom behavioural drive afterward — Load/Strengthen flow, eligibility, and the two-line Strengthen hover all byte-for-byte identical to BUILD 079/080, zero JS errors. Next up: now that real browser tooling has been confirmed available this session, going back to actually eyeball BUILD 077 through 080 (all of which shipped on code-reasoning/jsdom alone, not a rendered screenshot) would close that standing gap; beyond that, the standing manual human playtest pass (Stages 2.0 through 2.12), the BUILD 074 open question about the original opening-fight bug report, and the BUILD 071 enemy_buff_poison gap.

Stage 2.11 — a CSS-only presentation pass fixing three browser-reported bugs in the BUILD 079 die-row picker (BUILD 080) — just landed; the fix itself could not be visually confirmed this session (browser tooling unavailable), only reasoned from the stylesheet's own box-model numbers (the BUILD 040/041 "measure before you clip" habit) and re-verified behaviourally unchanged via the same jsdom drive BUILD 079 used. Root cause of all three reports (doubled gold borders between adjacent eligible rows, mod-name-disappears-into-solid-gold-hover with a clipped ×N badge, and inconsistent row widths) traced to one thing: .die-row-pickable's outline-offset: 2px painted outside the row's own box, which flush-stacked rows with no gap never account for. Swapped to an inset box-shadow (never paints outside the box, can't be clipped, can't create a gap-separated double line) and replaced the hover's losing text-colour override (beaten by .loaded/.nat-twenty's higher-specificity colour rules — worst case literally --nat-on-under--nat for face 20) with a translucent --nat wash that never touches text colour at all. No eligibility, flow, weight arithmetic, or JS of any kind was touched. Next up: real browser verification of this build once tooling is available (this and BUILD 077-079 have all shipped without eyes-on confirmation), the standing manual human playtest pass (Stages 2.0 through 2.11), the BUILD 074 open question about what the original opening-fight bug report actually saw, and the BUILD 071 enemy_buff_poison gap.

Stage 2.10 — the die action screen's own die becomes the Load/Strengthen face picker, both separate face-button screens deleted (BUILD 079) — just landed, verified via a live jsdom drive of the real index.html (forced win → Load → mod pick → clicked a blank die row → mod landed and advanced straight to card reward; Strengthen → face 20 pickable with a two-line hover (current description, then the same description at weight+1) → click → weight 1→2; face 1 confirmed unpickable in both flows; zero JS errors) — not yet verified in an actual browser, headless or windowed, since browser tooling was unavailable this session. renderDieList() took an optional pickConfig arg with every other call site (fight screen, map die previews) passing nothing and rendering unchanged; only the die action screen's own die preview passes it, only during load_pick_face/strengthen_pick_face, using the exact eligibility filters the deleted button screens used. Strengthen's header fixed from "Choose a loaded face to strengthen" to "Choose a face to strengthen" (face 20 was always offered despite carrying no mod). Next up: the standing manual human playtest pass (now Stages 2.0 through 2.10, none eyeballed by a human, and nothing since 2.7 has had any browser verification at all), the BUILD 074 open question about what the original opening-fight bug report actually saw, the BUILD 072 load_pick_face die-preview question (now moot — load_pick_face shows and uses the die as of this build), and the BUILD 071 enemy_buff_poison gap.

Stage 2.9 — the riteStep/dieActionStep/cardRewardStep reset in devJumpToSlot() (BUILD 078) — just landed; not yet verified in any browser, headless or windowed (browser tooling was unavailable this session; verified only via a node --check syntax pass on the extracted scripts). Closes the rite-panel-abandonment gap BUILD 077 traced but did not fix: devJumpToSlot() now nulls all three module-level flow variables immediately after its run.outcome guard, so jumping away from an unresolved rite/die-action/card-reward panel dismisses it instead of leaving it rendered, clickable, and permanently blocking End Turn. Nothing else in devJumpToSlot() changed. Next up: the standing manual human playtest pass of Stages 2.0 through 2.9 (still none eyeballed by a human, and no build since 2.7 has had any browser verification at all), the BUILD 074 open question about what the original opening-fight bug report actually saw, the BUILD 072 load_pick_face die-preview question, and the BUILD 071 enemy_buff_poison gap.

Stage 2.8 — run-outcome guards on the current map node's real click path and on Restart Fight, plus a rite-panel abandonment trace (BUILD 077) — just landed; not yet verified in any browser, headless or windowed (browser tooling was unavailable this session; verified only via a node --check syntax pass on the extracted scripts). Both dev paths BUILD 076 left open are now closed: enterSlot() — the shared function every real current-node click (opening/lane/boss) calls — now no-ops once gameState.run.outcome !== 'active', matching devJumpToSlot()'s guard; devRestartFightBtn's click handler and its refreshInspector() disabled line now check run.status/run.outcome the same way BUILD 076 gated Skip to Die Action. New gap found and reported, not fixed: dev-jumping away from an unresolved rite (Heal/Die-action choice still open) leaves the rite panel stuck rendered and clickable indefinitely, because riteStep lives outside gameState and devJumpToSlot() never resets it — this also leaves endTurnBtn permanently disabled for the rest of the run, since its own guard requires riteStep === null. Smallest fix identified (reset riteStep/dieActionStep/cardRewardStep at the top of devJumpToSlot()) but intentionally not applied per this build's trace-only scope. Next up: applying that rite-abandonment fix if wanted, the standing manual human playtest pass of Stages 2.0 through 2.8 (still none eyeballed by a human, and this build has had no browser verification at all), the BUILD 074 open question about what the original opening-fight bug report actually saw, the BUILD 072 load_pick_face die-preview question, and the BUILD 071 enemy_buff_poison gap.

Stage 2.7 — a dev tool to jump to any map slot, plus the BUILD 074 Skip to Die Action guard fix (BUILD 076) — just landed, verified via a real headless-browser (Playwright/Chromium) drive of index.html; not yet eyeballed by a human in a real windowed browser. Every map node not currently standing-on is now dev-jumpable — completes the path to it and enters through the exact same enterSlot()/SLOT_HANDLERS/beginFightFromSlot() every real arrival uses, no parallel path — styled distinctly (--muted, dotted border) from a real choice/current node (--nat, solid/dashed) so it can't be mistaken for one during a normal playthrough. Both dev-jump nodes and the now-fixed Skip to Die Action button correctly no-op once the run is won or lost. Reported, not fixed, discovered while verifying that loss-guard: the genuinely-current node's own real click path (untouched by this build) has no run.outcome/status guard of its own and stays clickable even after the run has ended — a distinct, pre-existing gap from the Skip to Die Action one just closed, out of scope for "fix that Skip to Die Action gap" specifically. Next up is deciding whether that newly-found current-node gap is worth its own small fix, the standing manual human playtest pass of Stages 2.0 through 2.7 (still none eyeballed by a human), the BUILD 074 open question about what the original opening-fight bug report actually saw, the BUILD 072 load_pick_face die-preview question, and the BUILD 071 enemy_buff_poison gap.

Stage 2.6 — boss HP 150→100, and the enemy die removed from normals/elites (BUILD 075), verified via headless browser, not yet human-eyeballed — see CONFIRMED WORKING. Reported, not fixed: ENEMY_BUFF_TRIGGER/ENEMY_NAT_ONE/ENEMY_NAT_TWENTY/enemy_buff_poison remain reachable exclusively through the boss's own die.

Stage 2.5 — BUILD 074 diagnosed a reported "opening fight grants no card reward" bug and could not reproduce it; see CONFIRMED WORKING for the full four-part trace. No code was changed beyond the build stamp; there was no defect to fix.

Stage 2.5 — a shared opening fight between Start and the fork (BUILD 073), verified via headless browser at both 1400x900 and 1600x1080, not yet human-eyeballed — see CONFIRMED WORKING. A run now opens on a 50 HP/4-12-intent fight before either lane is reachable; the fork's two options are correctly inert until it's won.

Stage 2.5 — a shared opening fight between Start and the fork (BUILD 073), verified via headless browser at both 1400x900 and 1600x1080, not yet human-eyeballed — see CONFIRMED WORKING. A run now opens on a 50 HP/4-12-intent fight before either lane is reachable; the fork's two options are correctly inert until it's won.

Stage 2.4 — the player die alongside the boss die on the map, and during the die action (BUILD 072), verified via headless browser, not yet human-eyeballed — see CONFIRMED WORKING.

Stage 2.3 — hover descriptions on the die action Load list and both die rows (BUILD 071), verified via headless browser, not yet human-eyeballed — see CONFIRMED WORKING.

Stage 2.2 — hover descriptions on reward/die-action choices, plus the reward duplicate fix (BUILD 070), verified via headless browser, not yet human-eyeballed — see CONFIRMED WORKING.

Stage 2.1 — a visual pass on the act map, plus the fightScreen/controls-row overlap regression (BUILD 069), verified via headless browser, not yet human-eyeballed — see CONFIRMED WORKING.

Stages 1.13, 1.14, and 1.15 — die face weight display and the Strengthen rule change, the Nat 20 mechanic, and Nat 1's Penitence — were all browser-confirmed by Fergus on 24 August 2026. All three are fully human-verified, not just headless-verified. With this confirmation, both halves of the 24 August 2026 NAT 20 AND NAT 1 — THE ORDAINED decision are implemented and human-confirmed (Nat 20 in BUILD 066, Penitence in BUILD 067). No further items from that section remain queued.

Stage 1.13 — die face weight display plus the Strengthen rule change (BUILD 065), verified via headless browser, not yet human-eyeballed — see CONFIRMED WORKING.

Stages 1.1 through 1.10 confirmed working. The full loop is playable end to end: draw, roll, play cards, block, take enemy damage, reach win or loss. Two playtest bugs found and fixed in BUILD 013 (see CONFIRMED WORKING): win/loss now checked at the top of every phase transition instead of only at the CHECK_WIN_LOSS-named phase, and playCard() now refuses outside CARD_PHASE. Three CSS/markup-only visual passes followed: BUILD 014 (initial readability sizing), BUILD 015 (taller panels, doubled die faces, centrepiece roll display, real hand cards), and BUILD 016 (full-width divider-separated stat rows, stronger red/blue panel tints and borders, raised secondary-text contrast, centred/enlarged Start Game & Next Phase, card-play shake/pulse/pop feedback, HP-change number pops) — see CONFIRMED WORKING for the full breakdown, including the three read-only log()-parsing hooks (roll display, turn reset, card-play fx) added across these passes for live behaviour that pure CSS can't provide. Not yet re-verified in browser — needs a playtest pass confirming: the game halts immediately when enemy HP crosses 0 mid-CARD_PHASE; clicking a hand card outside CARD_PHASE is refused and logged with no state change; all twenty faces are visible on both dice with no scrolling; the roll-hero updates correctly on each roll and resets at the start of each turn; hand cards show name/cost/effect and dim correctly when unaffordable; both panels read as clearly red/blue tinted at a glance; Start Game/Next Phase are centred beneath the hand and easy to click while the inspector toggle stays small; playing Strike/Ward/Rite produces the correct panel and number feedback; HP changes visibly pop the number; and none of BUILD 014/015/016 introduced a functional regression (draw, roll, card play, enemy action, phase transitions all still work, zero console errors) while the log panel remains pixel-identical throughout.

Consecrate + die-face-clipping fixes (BUILD 018) not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: player die face 10 reads Consecrate and dev_test appears nowhere (die list, mods, log); all twenty face numbers on both dice render fully with no clipping/overlap; rolling face 10 takes soul 3→5; each card played that turn (including ones paid for with the granted soul) adds 3 block and logs correctly; advancing a turn resets soul to 3 with no carryover; ordinary card play (strike/ward/rite) still works correctly with no console errors across a full round.

Dev tool — force roll (BUILD 019) also not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: Start Game, reach ROLL_PHASE, click player face 10 → [DEV] forced roll: face 10 then Consecrate fires (soul 3→5); a second face click that same ROLL_PHASE visit does nothing; clicking a blank face on a later turn gives 3 block; clicking face 20 fires the Nat 20 routing; clicking any player face outside ROLL_PHASE is refused and logged; the same all holds for the enemy die faces gated to ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE; hover state is visibly obvious on the face buttons; zero console errors across the pass. This is dev tooling only, explicitly marked for removal before release — not a gameplay substage.

Dev tool — load mod on demand (BUILD 020) also not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: load Consecrate onto face 5, die list shows it there; force-roll face 5, soul goes 3→5 exactly as face 10 does; clear face 5, die list shows blank; force-roll face 5 again, 3 block with no soul gain and no leftover Consecrate behaviour; load a mod onto two faces at once and confirm both trigger correctly when forced; attempting to load/clear face 1 or 20 is refused and logged; zero console errors. This is dev tooling only, explicitly marked for removal before release — not a gameplay substage.

Dev mod loader readability fix (BUILD 021) not yet browser-verified — needs confirmation that the DEV LOAD MOD row is legible without leaning in, the dropdown/face-input/Load/Clear are comfortably clickable, it still visually reads as a dev tool separate from Start Game/Next Phase, load+clear still work end to end, and zero console errors.

Blank passive value change (BUILD 022) not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: force-rolling any blank face logs 2 block generated and player Block increases by exactly 2; a loaded face (e.g. Consecrate on whatever face it's currently on) still triggers its mod normally and unaffected; advancing a turn still clears block to 0 at START_OF_TURN; zero console errors.

Force-roll stacking fix (BUILD 023) not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: Start Game, reach ROLL_PHASE, force a blank face — Block reads exactly 2 (not 4), only one [BLANK] line in the log; on a later turn, force Consecrate on face 10 — soul goes 3→5 with no blank block underneath it; a turn where nothing is forced still resolves exactly one natural roll when Next Phase is clicked to leave ROLL_PHASE; the same holds for the enemy die in ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE; zero console errors.

Smite (BUILD 024) not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: dev-load Smite onto face 7, force roll it, log reads [MOD] smite: 16 damage and enemy HP drops from its (now 70) max with the HP number flashing per existing feedback; clear face 7, force roll it, back to 2 block with no residual Smite behaviour; load Smite and Consecrate onto separate faces and confirm each triggers correctly and independently; zero console errors.

Fight-length balance pass (BUILD 025) not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: enemy panel reads 70/70; hand cards show Strike as 5 damage and Rite as 5 damage 6 block; playing three Strikes in one turn drops the enemy from 70 to 55; force-rolling Smite still lands 16 damage correctly on top of the new HP total; a full fight with no mods loaded takes roughly five turns rather than three; zero console errors.

Variable enemy intent (BUILD 026) not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: Start Game shows an Intent number and "Attacks for N" label between 6 and 18 before CARD_PHASE is reached; advancing several turns shows the intent changing each turn, always in range, with one [ENEMY] intent set to N line per turn; a turn with block less than intent lands the correctly reduced damage; a turn with block exceeding intent lands zero damage; six turns show a visible spread of different intent values; zero console errors.

Penance (BUILD 027) not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: dev-load Penance onto face 4, force roll it, log reads [MOD] penance: 8 damage, 8 block, enemy HP drops by exactly 8, player Block increases by exactly 8; both the enemy HP number and player Block number show their visual feedback; advancing a turn still clears block normally at START_OF_TURN; clearing face 4 and force-rolling it returns to 2 block with no residual Penance behaviour; zero console errors.

Offering (BUILD 028) not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: dev-load Offering onto face 8, force roll it, log reads [MOD] offering: +2 soul this turn, draw 1 followed by a [DRAW] line, Soul goes 3→5, hand grows 5→6; playing five one-cost cards is all permitted; advancing a turn resets Soul to 3 and hand back to 5 with no carryover; force-rolling Offering on a turn with an empty deck triggers the reshuffle correctly; clearing face 8 and force-rolling it returns to 2 block with no residual soul or draw; zero console errors.

Turn-flow auto-advance (BUILD 029) not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: the page loads straight into Round 1, CARD_PHASE, 5 cards in hand, a roll already resolved; playing cards then clicking End Turn once runs the enemy attack and lands back in CARD_PHASE of round 2 with a fresh hand and a new roll, with every phase header present in the log; a full fight can be played to victory using only End Turn and card clicks, with the win halt still working; the relocated dev step control (bottom of page) still advances exactly one phase per click; force roll still works when the game is sitting in ROLL_PHASE/ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE (via the dev step control pausing there); Restart Fight correctly resets to round 1; zero console errors.

Enemy poison tick (BUILD 030). START_OF_TURN's phase-machine block gained an enemy poison branch (placed immediately after the existing player-poison branch): if gameState.enemy.poisonStacks > 0, damage = calculateDamage(poisonStacks) per Law 1, applied straight to enemy.hp via updateEnemy alongside poisonStacks - 1 in the same call, logged as [POISON] N damage, stacks now M; at 0 stacks it logs nothing and does nothing, matching the spec exactly. Poison bypasses block by construction — it's applied via calculateDamage() directly to hp, never routed through the intent-minus-block subtraction ENEMY_ACT_PHASE uses — with an explicit comment recorded at the call site for when enemy block exists later. Persistence needed no new code: poisonStacks already lived in gameState.enemy and resetFight() already zeroed it, so it now persists turn-to-turn within a fight and clears on Restart Fight for free. The Poison Stacks stat panel (#enemyPoisonValue) already read gameState.enemy.poisonStacks live in renderStats() from a prior stage — no rendering change was needed, it started reflecting real values the instant the tick started writing them. Added a DEV ONLY control (#devPoisonApplier: number input + "Apply to Enemy" button) beside the existing DEV LOAD MOD bar, wired to devApplyPoison() which adds the entered amount to gameState.enemy.poisonStacks via updateEnemy and logs [DEV] applied N poison to enemy — no mod or card applies poison yet; this is purely for exercising the tick ahead of Blight/Virulence. No card, mod, Consecrate/Smite/Penance/Offering, blank passive, enemy intent, or turn-flow logic was touched. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 030.

Verified via a full jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html (not a rewrite, not a unit test of an extracted function): applying 6 poison via the dev control set gameState.enemy.poisonStacks to 6 and the panel read 6; six consecutive End Turn clicks produced enemy HP drops of exactly 6, 5, 4, 3, 2, 1 (21 total) with stacks reading 5, 4, 3, 2, 1, 0 after each; a seventh End Turn produced no [POISON] log line with stacks staying at 0; Restart Fight after re-applying 9 poison brought poisonStacks back to 0 and the panel back to 0; zero JS errors were captured across the whole run. Still needs a manual hard-refresh browser pass per the standard five-consecutive-clean-fights bar before advancing past this substage — the jsdom pass covers mechanical correctness, not a human eyeballing the actual page.

Blight and Virulence (BUILD 031) not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: dev-load Blight onto face 6, force roll it, log reads [MOD] blight: applied 6 poison and Poison Stacks panel reads 6 immediately; ending the turn ticks poison down per the existing decay behaviour; on a fresh fight, dev-load Virulence onto face 6, force roll it, log reads [MOD] virulence: applied 3 poison, doubled to 6 and the panel reads 6 immediately; on another fresh fight, Blight on face 6 and Virulence on face 7, force-rolling Blight then Virulence on a later turn correctly doubles whatever poison remains from decay, not a fixed baseline; zero console errors. Mechanically confirmed via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html across all three scenarios (see CONFIRMED WORKING) with zero JS errors, but not yet eyeballed in a real browser.

Force-roll auto-advance pause fix (BUILD 032) — SUPERSEDED. Real human playtesting found the 300ms pause was consistently too short to actually click a face; clicks kept landing after auto-advance had already moved to CARD_PHASE, logged as [DEV] cannot force roll outside ROLL_PHASE. The underlying mechanism (defer via setTimeout, let nextPhase()'s existing playerRollResolved guard decide forced vs natural) was correct and was kept; only the pause length changed. See BUILD 033 below.

Force-roll pause length fix (BUILD 033) not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: ending a turn shows ROLL_PHASE visibly pause for roughly 1.5-2 seconds before resolving on its own if nothing is clicked; clicking a face during that pause forces it correctly with only one [ROLL] log line in the log and no "cannot force roll" refusal; a turn where nothing is clicked still resolves naturally after the pause with no errors; End Turn and other controls stay inert (as they already did) during the pause; ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE and every other phase transition are not slowed down; zero console errors. Mechanically confirmed via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html across four checks (see CONFIRMED WORKING) with zero JS errors, including a click landing 1000ms into the pause — the exact scenario that failed under BUILD 032 — but not yet eyeballed in a real browser; the perceptibility/feel of the pause is a human judgement the automated pass can't make.

Sanctuary (BUILD 034) not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: dev-load Sanctuary onto a face, force roll it during the ROLL_PHASE pause, log reads [MOD] sanctuary: 16 block and player Block increases by exactly 16 with the Block number's visual feedback firing; advancing a turn clears block back to 0 at START_OF_TURN as normal; clearing the face and force-rolling it again returns to 2 block (blank passive) with no residual Sanctuary behaviour; zero console errors. Mechanically confirmed via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html (see CONFIRMED WORKING) with zero JS errors, but not yet eyeballed in a real browser.

Vigil (BUILD 035) not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: dev-load Vigil onto a face, force roll it during the ROLL_PHASE pause, play some cards but leave at least 2 in hand, click End Turn, and confirm the log shows the correct card count and 5xN block with player Block increasing by exactly that amount; confirm that block survives into ENEMY_ACT_PHASE and reduces incoming damage correctly; advance to the next turn and confirm block clears normally and Vigil does not fire again without being rolled; a turn where the whole hand is played down before ending turn confirms 0 block granted with no errors; zero console errors. Mechanically confirmed via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html (see CONFIRMED WORKING) with zero JS errors, but not yet eyeballed in a real browser.

Restart Fight dev-loading pause (BUILD 036) not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: clicking Restart Fight stops the game right after drawing the hand, before rolling, with the dev tools comfortably usable in that window; two mods can be loaded onto two different faces there; clicking Begin Roll proceeds into ROLL_PHASE and the existing force-roll pause behaves normally from there (both the auto-resolve-after-~1.8s path and the force-a-face-mid-pause path); playing that fight through to End Turn shows every later turn still auto-advancing exactly as before with no extra pauses introduced anywhere else; zero console errors. Mechanically confirmed via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html (see CONFIRMED WORKING) with zero JS errors, but not yet eyeballed in a real browser.

init() dev-loading pause fix (BUILD 037) not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: a hard refresh of the page (not Restart Fight) stops the game right after drawing the opening hand, before rolling, with time to load mods; two mods load correctly during that window; clicking Begin Roll enters ROLL_PHASE with the normal force-roll pause behaving as usual; Restart Fight, tested again mid-session afterward, still pauses correctly via the same shared code path; zero console errors. Mechanically confirmed via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html, simulating a true hard refresh (see CONFIRMED WORKING) with zero JS errors, but not yet eyeballed in a real browser.

Post-fight die action loop (BUILD 038) not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: the dev shortcut ("Skip to Die Action") jumps straight to the Load/Strengthen/Skip screen without needing to win a fight, and all three paths work cleanly through it; a real fight won naturally shows the same screen automatically at victory; choosing Load offers exactly 3 mods excluding Consecrate/Zeal/Fervour/Ordain and anything already on the die, picking one loads it onto a chosen blank face; starting a new fight afterward shows the newly loaded face still present while HP/deck/hand have all reset to fresh-fight state; winning again and choosing Strengthen on an existing loaded face visibly bumps its weight; winning a third time and choosing Skip changes nothing and a new fight still starts cleanly; zero console errors. Mechanically confirmed via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html across both the dev-shortcut path and a real played-to-victory fight (see CONFIRMED WORKING) with zero JS errors, but not yet eyeballed in a real browser — this is the first genuinely player-facing (non-dev-tool) screen in the project, so a human read of its usability/legibility matters more here than on prior dev-tool-only substages.

DEV POISON target selector (BUILD 039) not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: with Player selected and 6 entered, Apply logs [DEV] applied 6 poison to player and the Active debuffs row reads poison x6 immediately; with Enemy selected (the default), behaviour is unchanged from BUILD 038. IMPORTANT — flagged for planning, not fixed here: the player poison tick itself (unmodified, per the prompt's explicit instruction) does not decay. Confirmed via jsdom by calling runPhase('START_OF_TURN') directly five times with poisonStacks pinned at 6: every call dealt exactly 6 damage and poisonStacks was still 6 afterward every time — the tick's code has no poisonStacks decrement at all, unlike the enemy poison tick. So the success scenario's expected 6/5/4/3/2/1-then-stop sequence will NOT be observed in a manual browser pass; expect a flat 6 damage every turn indefinitely instead until the tick itself is fixed in a future substage. Block is correctly never touched by the tick (confirmed by code read, not just runtime), and Restart Fight still zeroes player poison correctly regardless. Mechanically confirmed via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html (see CONFIRMED WORKING) with zero JS errors, but not yet eyeballed in a real browser.

Combat-panel align-items fix (BUILD 040) — superseded by BUILD 041, see below. Correctly fixed the internal distortion (hero-stats undistorted, Deck/Discard cleared #handRow) but left a residual overlap because it couldn't touch the outer #playerPanel min-height that was the real constraint. Kept for the historical record of the investigation; BUILD 041 closes it out.

Combat-panel min-height fix (BUILD 041) — RESOLVES BUILD 040's residual overlap; the player-panel/#handRow bleed-through bug is now closed. Verified via the same real-browser harness (Playwright driving the system's actual msedge.exe against the real index.html) at both required viewports, 1400x900 and 1600x1080: #playerPanel and #enemyPanel both render at the full measured-natural 512px, hero-stats stays undistorted at 255px, and heroStats/debuffsRow/deckRow/discardRow all test false for overlapping #handRow's bounding box — confirmed both numerically and visually via direct screenshots of the hand-row region at both viewports, showing Active debuffs/Deck/Discard fully legible with a clean gap above the hand cards and nothing visible in the card gaps. .fx-pulse-blue's glow confirmed still unclipped (same ancestor-chain check as BUILD 040, no overflow: hidden anywhere between #playerPanel and the document). .left-col now scrolls at every tested height (scrollHeight 1628 vs clientHeight 670-1050) since neither panel compresses below natural size any more — the expected, correct tradeoff. #log, .die-col, die-face row structure, and .fx-pulse-blue's own rule were untouched. Not yet eyeballed by a human in an actual windowed browser (verified via headless real-browser screenshots and measurements instead), but this closes the bleed-through item that BUILD 038-040 opened and left outstanding.

Player poison decay + calculateDamage() fix (BUILD 042) — RESOLVES the divergence flagged by BUILD 039 and reconfirmed by direct investigation before this fix. Player poison now decays exactly like enemy poison (N, N-1, ... down to 0, silent at 0) and routes through calculateDamage(), closing the last known Law 1 bypass. Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html using the real DEV POISON bar: applying 6 poison to the player and ticking seven times produced 6, 5, 4, 3, 2, 1, 0 damage (tick seven silent), 21 total damage, poisonStacks reaching 0, and the Active debuffs panel visibly stepping poison x6 down to — tick by tick; enemy poison re-verified unaffected in the same run (6 applied, one tick, correctly 6 damage/stacks now 5). Not yet eyeballed in a real browser, but this closes both items BUILD 039 left open for player poison.

Dev tool visibility sizing pass (BUILD 043) — NOT COMPLETE, needs a planning decision before this is closed out. A real ~10.3% reduction was measured and applied to the three named regions (combat panels, mid-strip) via padding/row-spacing only — no hero-number font sizes touched, no new overlap or bleed-through (re-verified against BUILD 040/041's fix), confirmed via the same real-browser method used for BUILD 040/041/042. But the actual shortfall between total left-col content (1460px after this pass) and the viewport (870px at 1400x900, 1050px at 1600x1080) was roughly 46% of total content before this pass — far too large for "roughly 10%" tightening on three regions to close. #devPoisonApplier, the last dev-tool row, still sits ~590px (1400x900) / ~410px (1600x1080) below the fold. The stated success criterion "all four dev tool rows visible without scrolling" is not met. Not pushed further because doing so would mean either touching regions the prompt didn't name (hand-row, controls-row, the dev-tool blocks' own spacing) or cutting the three named regions far more aggressively than "roughly 10%" (risking the legibility/no-overlap sub-criteria the same prompt protects) — flagged for a planning decision rather than taken unilaterally.

Zeal (BUILD 044) not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: force-load Zeal onto a face, first trigger deals exactly 10 damage and logs bonus now +4; second trigger deals 14 and logs bonus now +8; Restart Fight, trigger again — damage reflects the persisted bonus rather than resetting to 10 (verified as 18, continuing correctly from +8, not the prompt's own arithmetically-inconsistent "still 14" example — see CONFIRMED WORKING note); triggering five times total across two fights produces no duplicate log lines, no console warnings, and no listener ever registered with 'zeal' in its id; Zeal now appears in the post-fight Load reward pool (DIE_ACTION_EXCLUDED_MOD_IDS no longer includes it) alongside the other seven pool mods; zero console errors. Mechanically confirmed via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html (see CONFIRMED WORKING) with zero JS errors, but not yet eyeballed in a real browser.

Fervour (BUILD 046) not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: force-load Fervour onto a face, trigger it, log confirms the trigger with no damage dealt; play Strike (normally 5) for 10, logged distinctly as doubled; play a second Strike same turn, also 10; end turn, play a Strike next turn for 5, confirming clearOn: 'turn' expired it; apply poison during a Fervour turn and let it tick — poison damage unaffected, not doubled. Also worth a human's attention: Fervour is currently NOT reward-pool-eligible (still in DIE_ACTION_EXCLUDED_MOD_IDS, unlike Zeal) since this prompt didn't ask for that change — a planning decision on whether it should be, deferred rather than assumed. Also flagged: the prompt's tagging list named "Ordain" as an existing mod-damage call site to tag, but Ordain isn't built yet — nothing to tag, noted rather than silently skipped. Mechanically confirmed via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html (see CONFIRMED WORKING) with zero JS errors, but not yet eyeballed in a real browser.

Ordain (BUILD 047) not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: force-load Ordain onto a face at weight 1, force-trigger it, log confirms 10 damage and weight now 2; trigger again, weight now 3; Restart Fight, weight persists at 3; roll a full round of natural rolls afterward and confirm via the die-face list, State Inspector, or log that the elevated weight is genuinely reflected (die-face list itself has no numeric weight display yet — verified instead via rollDie()'s own pool and the State Inspector, both confirmed correct). Mechanically confirmed via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html, including an explicit additive-stacking check against Strengthen (see CONFIRMED WORKING) with zero JS errors, but not yet eyeballed in a real browser. (The reward-pool-eligibility gap this note originally flagged is now resolved — see BUILD 048 below.)

With Zeal, Fervour, and Ordain all built, all three previously-deferred mods named in the code audit are now complete — the mod pool stands at eleven (Consecrate, Smite, Penance, Offering, Blight, Virulence, Sanctuary, Vigil, Zeal, Fervour, Ordain).

Reward pool eligibility check (BUILD 048) — CONFIRMED AND FIXED. Fervour and Ordain were both still excluded from the post-fight Load reward pool going into this check; both are now eligible, verified via 60 real reward-pool draws showing every non-anchor mod appearing at least once and Consecrate never appearing. Not yet eyeballed in a real browser, but mechanically closed.

Restart Fight loss-loop fix (BUILD 049) — RESOLVES a real, reported bug: after a loss, Restart Fight was stuck logging [INIT] fight reset immediately followed by [LOSS] player defeated forever, never reaching START_OF_TURN. Root cause: resetFight() never restored player.hp (the documented "hp carries over" behaviour), so a loss (hp <= 0 by definition) stayed at hp <= 0 through the reset, re-tripping the win/loss guard the instant run.status flipped back to 'active'. Fix: resetFight() now restores player.hp to maxHp. IMPORTANT — FLAGGED FOR PLANNING, not decided unilaterally: this changes previously-documented behaviour (CLAUDE.md's FIGHT RESET section, which Claude Code cannot edit, still reads "hp carries over," and CORE NUMBERS documents this under a stated Permadeath design). The fix matches this prompt's own explicit success criteria (70/70 after Restart Fight following either a win or a loss) and is the only way Restart Fight can keep functioning as the repeatable dev/testing reset it's been used as throughout this entire build, but it does mean a loss is no longer permanently unrecoverable via this button. Whether that's meant to be permanent (not just a dev-period convenience) needs a planning decision and a FIGHT RESET spec update from Fergus, not assumed here. Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html across both a forced real loss and a forced real win, each followed by Restart Fight: no [LOSS]/[WIN] line reappeared, player.hp read 70/70, phase correctly reached START_OF_TURN and paused there, and the game was confirmed genuinely playable afterward (Begin Roll -> CARD_PHASE with a fresh 5-card hand, End Turn enabled) — not just superficially reset. Zero JS errors. Not yet eyeballed in a real browser.

Card reward screen (BUILD 050), Stage 1.11 not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: win a fight, resolve the die action panel, and the card reward panel appears offering three cards (gold border, "Fight won — choose a card", matching the die action panel's look); picking a card logs the add and the deck total genuinely rises by one (see the CONFIRMED WORKING note on the literal "Deck" stat vs. total card count — they're not the same number at pick time, by pre-existing design unrelated to this build); starting the next fight shows the card in the deck, drawable and playable; Restart Fight leaves the added card in the permanent collection; skipping on a later fight logs the skip and changes nothing; the dev Skip to Card Reward button reaches the panel without playing a fight. Mechanically confirmed via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html across both the dev-shortcut path and a real forced win (which correctly chains die action -> card reward, not before) with zero JS errors, but not yet eyeballed in a real browser — this is the second genuinely player-facing (non-dev-tool) screen in the project, so a human read of its usability/legibility matters more here than on dev-tool-only substages, same reasoning noted back at the original die action screen (BUILD 038). (The three TEST_A/B/C placeholder cards this note originally referenced were deleted in BUILD 051 and replaced with the real tier-1 pool — see below; the screen/flow behaviour this note describes is unchanged, only the offered cards are different now.)

Real card pool, tier 1 (BUILD 051), Stage 1.11 substage 2, not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: the reward screen after a win offers three of the eight real cards and never a TEST card; taking Censure puts it in the deck and it deals 14 on the next fight; Rebuke plays at 0 soul without being dimmed; Scripture/Communion/Censer/Judgement all behave per their stated numbers (see the Communion arithmetic note in CONFIRMED WORKING — net soul change is +1, not +2, because its own 1-soul cost is paid first); all eight survive Restart Fight once owned; zero console errors. Mechanically confirmed via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html across five scenarios (see CONFIRMED WORKING) with zero JS errors, but not yet eyeballed in a real browser. NOTE: Communion's 1-soul cost was changed to 0 in BUILD 052 (see below) — the "net +1, not +2" arithmetic this note describes no longer applies; Communion is now a clean 0-cost +2 soul.

Communion fix and tier-3 state-read conditionals (BUILD 052), Stage 1.11 substage 3, not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: Communion shows a 0-cost badge, is playable at 0 soul, and takes soul from 3 to 5; against a clean 70 HP enemy, Purge deals 6 (enemy reads 64); with 4 poison stacks (via the DEV POISON bar), Purge deals 10; with 4 stacks, Reckoning deals 11, with 0 stacks it deals 3; playing Censer then Reckoning in the same turn deals 11, confirming Reckoning reads stacks applied earlier that turn; at intent 8 or lower Interdict grants 5 block, at intent 12 or higher it grants 10; every play of the four cards prints a log line naming the branch that fired; zero console errors. Mechanically confirmed via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html across every named scenario (see CONFIRMED WORKING) with zero JS errors, but not yet eyeballed in a real browser. Retribution (the fourth flagged state-reading card) was deliberately not built this pass, per the prompt.

Mid-CARD_PHASE victory fix (BUILD 053), not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: against a 70 HP enemy reduced to 4 HP, playing a Strike for 5 immediately prints [WIN] enemy defeated, shows the VICTORY banner, and opens the die action panel, without End Turn being clicked; enemy HP reads 0 or -1, never a larger negative from cards played after the kill; remaining hand cards are visibly disabled and End Turn is inactive; exactly one [WIN] line appears in the log; choosing a die action leads to the card reward screen as before; winning normally via End Turn (kill landing in ENEMY_ACT_PHASE or on poison at START_OF_TURN) still behaves exactly as in BUILD 052. Mechanically confirmed via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html (see CONFIRMED WORKING) with zero JS errors, but not yet eyeballed in a real browser. FLAGGED, not a gap introduced by this build: playCard() itself has no run.status guard — a card played after victory (only reachable by calling playCard() directly, bypassing the disabled hand-card button a real player is limited to) would still execute its own effect, though it cannot re-trigger [WIN] or reopen anything since runPhase()'s own guard is gated on run.status === 'active'. This mirrors the pre-existing loss halt, which also relies solely on button-disabling rather than a function-level status check, and the prompt did not ask for that to change.

Logging fix, not a new substage — card reward count and Nat 1/20 not-implemented lines (BUILD 054), not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: taking a card reward with a 13-card collection logs "deck now 14 cards" (matching the true ownedCards total, not the draw pile); skipping a reward logs the unchanged true total; forcing a player roll of face 20 prints a Nat 20 not-implemented line, and face 1 the equivalent Nat 1 line; forcing an enemy roll of face 1 prints the equivalent enemy-side line; enemy face 20's existing not-implemented line is unchanged; no face gains any actual effect (HP/state unaffected); zero console errors. Mechanically confirmed via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html across all four log-line scenarios plus both card-reward count scenarios (see CONFIRMED WORKING) with zero JS errors, but not yet eyeballed in a real browser. FLAGGED: the two new player-side lines use the '[ROLL]' prefix rather than literally reusing '[ENEMY]' — see the INTERPRETATION FLAG in the CONFIRMED WORKING entry above for the reasoning; worth a planning confirmation that '[ROLL]' was the right read of "matching... in style and prefix" rather than introducing a new '[PLAYER]' tag.

Tier 4, Retribution (BUILD 055), Stage 1.11 substage 4, not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: with 0 block, Retribution deals 0; playing a Ward for 5 block then Retribution deals 5, logged uncapped; two Wards for 10 block then Retribution deals 10, logged uncapped; loading Sanctuary onto a face, forcing it, then playing Retribution at 16 block deals 12, logged with the capped variant naming the true 16; Retribution appears in a real reward draw with no exclusion list; zero console errors. Mechanically confirmed via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html across all four damage scenarios plus the pool-eligibility check (see CONFIRMED WORKING) with zero JS errors, but not yet eyeballed in a real browser. With this build, all twelve tier 1-4 cards named across BUILD 051/052/055 are complete — the card pool stands at twelve (rebuke, censure, judgement, vestment, litany, scripture, communion, censer, purge, interdict, reckoning, retribution).

Roll-result exposure (BUILD 056), Stage 1.11 substage 5, not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: force a blank roll and the State Inspector's turn object reads rollOutcome: "blank"; force a mod roll (e.g. Consecrate or Sanctuary) and it reads "mod"; force face 20 or face 1 and it reads "nat_twenty" or "nat_one" respectively; load Sanctuary onto a face at weight 1, Strengthen it twice to weight 3, force that face, and rolledFaceWeight reads 3; advancing to the next turn updates both fields to the new turn's values rather than persisting the old ones; zero console errors. Implementation: gameState.turn gained rollOutcome (null | 'blank' | 'mod' | 'nat_twenty' | 'nat_one') and rolledFaceWeight (null | number), both set via updateTurn() inside resolvePlayerRoll() — the single function both the natural (nextPhase) and forced (forcePlayerRoll) roll paths already funnel through — immediately after the existing '[ROLL] face:' log line and before the NAT_TWENTY/NAT_ONE/MOD_TRIGGER/BLANK_ROLL branch that already existed there. Weight is read straight off the face object passed in, exactly as rollDie()'s own pool-building already does. Both fields are cleared back to null in START_OF_TURN immediately after the existing clearListeners('turn') call, so they reset alongside the existing turn-listener sweep. No card reads either field yet — this substage is exposure only; Orison, Covenant, and Rapture were not built. Mechanically confirmed via a Node/vm load-and-drive of the actual index.html's script (document/window stubbed, no jsdom dependency available) across blank, mod, nat_twenty, nat_one, and a weight-1-to-3 Strengthen-style scenario, plus a simulated START_OF_TURN confirming both fields clear while round advances — zero JS errors — but not yet eyeballed in a real browser. Build stamp now STAGE 1.11 / BUILD 056.

Covenant (BUILD 057), Stage 1.11 substage 6, not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: on a turn where the rolled face has weight 1 (any untouched face), Covenant deals 5; with a face at weight 3 (double-Strengthened), Covenant deals 11; Covenant deals identically whether the rolled face resolved as blank or as a mod trigger — only weight matters, not outcome type; Covenant appears in a real reward draw with no exclusion list; zero console errors. Implementation: config.cards['covenant'] (1 soul, attack) reads gameState.turn.rolledFaceWeight directly at play time — never re-derives weight from gameState.die.faces — computes calculateDamage(2 + 3 * weight, 'attack') exactly like every other damage card, and logs [CARD] covenant: N damage (face weight M). Registered into config.cardPool with no exclusion list, same pattern as the other twelve. Added a CARD_EFFECT_TEXT entry ('2 damage + 3 per face weight') for display consistency; CARD_FX_TYPE was not touched, matching the other tier 2+ cards which also have no fx entry. BUILD 056's rolledFaceWeight/resolvePlayerRoll exposure code was not touched. Mechanically confirmed via a Node/vm load-and-drive of the actual index.html's script (document/window stubbed, no jsdom dependency available): weight-1 play deals exactly 5, weight-3 play deals exactly 11, and a full resolvePlayerRoll()-then-play pass through both a blank face and a mod-loaded face (each forced to weight 2) each deal exactly 8, confirming outcome type has no bearing on the card — zero JS errors — but not yet eyeballed in a real browser. Build stamp now STAGE 1.11 / BUILD 057.

Rapture (BUILD 058), Stage 1.11 substage 7 — the last card in the pool, not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: on a turn with no mod trigger, Rapture costs 2 soul and deals 12; on a turn where a mod face was rolled (e.g. force Consecrate or Sanctuary), Rapture is playable at 0 soul remaining and still deals 12; playing it on a Fervour turn deals 24 for 0 soul; the hand-card cost badge itself reads 0 on a mod turn, not just the play logic; Rapture appears in a real reward draw with no exclusion list; zero console errors. Implementation: config.cards['rapture'] (attack, base soulCost 2) reads gameState.turn.rollOutcome directly at play time (never re-derived from anywhere else) via a new optional card.getCost(gameState) function, returning 0 when rollOutcome === 'mod' and 2 otherwise. A new shared getCardCost(card) helper (returns card.getCost(gameState) if present, else card.soulCost) was introduced and wired into the two places that previously read card.soulCost directly for gameplay purposes: playCard()'s soul-affordability gate/spend/log, and renderCardButtons()'s affordability check and cost badge — this is the mechanism that makes the badge itself reflect the reduced cost, not just the play logic. All thirteen existing cards are unaffected: none define getCost, so getCardCost() falls through to their unchanged static soulCost. Damage always routes through calculateDamage(12, 'attack') regardless of which cost branch applied, so it composes correctly with any active DAMAGE_MULTIPLIER listener (e.g. Fervour). Effect logs [CARD] rapture: 12 damage (2 soul) on a normal turn or [CARD] rapture: 12 damage (free, mod triggered) on a mod turn, in addition to the pre-existing generic [CARD] played Rapture (cost N) line playCard() already logs for every card. Registered into config.cardPool with no exclusion list, same pattern as the other thirteen. The card reward screen's own soulCost display (line ~1600, cardRewardOptions loop) was deliberately left reading the static base soulCost, per the instruction not to touch that screen. calculateDamage(), gameState.turn.rollOutcome, and the BUILD 056 exposure code were not touched. Mechanically confirmed via a Node/vm load-and-drive of the actual index.html's script (document/window stubbed, no jsdom dependency available): a blank-turn play costs 2 soul and deals 12; a mod-turn play at 0 soul succeeds and deals 12; getCardCost() directly reports 0 on a mod turn and 2 otherwise (covering the badge path); and a mod-turn play with a registered DAMAGE_MULTIPLIER of 2 (simulating Fervour) deals 24 for 0 soul — zero JS errors — but not yet eyeballed in a real browser.
