Build stamp now STAGE 1.11 / BUILD 058. With this build, the full fourteen-card pool named across the tier 1-5 substages (BUILD 051/052/055/057/058) is complete.

Orison (BUILD 059), Stage 1.11 substage 8 — the last card in the fifteen-card pool, not yet browser-verified — needs a playtest pass confirming: force a mod face (Consecrate or Sanctuary) and Orison deals exactly 5, logged with the base variant; force a blank face and Orison deals exactly 9, logged with the blank variant; force face 20 or face 1 and Orison deals 5, confirming Nat rolls are not treated as blank; Orison appears in a real reward draw with no exclusion list; zero console errors. Implementation: config.cards['orison'] (1 soul, attack) reads gameState.turn.rollOutcome directly at play time — never re-derives blankness from the die, the face, or the blank passive listener — dealing calculateDamage(9, 'attack') when rollOutcome === 'blank' and calculateDamage(5, 'attack') otherwise. This is the deciding detail: NAT_ONE/NAT_TWENTY faces carry no loaded mod, so any version inferring blankness from "face.modId === null" would incorrectly pay Orison's 9-damage branch on a Nat roll; reading rollOutcome directly (set by BUILD 056 to 'nat_one'/'nat_twenty', never 'blank', on those faces) is what keeps them on the 5-damage branch. Logs [CARD] orison: 9 damage, blank roll on a blank turn and [CARD] orison: 5 damage otherwise. Registered into config.cardPool with no exclusion list, same pattern as the other fourteen. gameState.turn.rollOutcome, the BUILD 056 exposure code, the blank passive (still its own separate 2-block-generating listener, untouched), getCardCost(), calculateDamage() itself, the mod pool, the die action panel, the card reward screen, and all fourteen existing cards were not touched. Mechanically confirmed via a Node/vm load-and-drive of the actual index.html's script (document/window stubbed, no jsdom dependency available), driving resolvePlayerRoll() through a real mod-loaded face, a real blank face, face 20, and face 1 in turn and playing Orison after each: mod → 5, blank → 9, Nat 20 → 5, Nat 1 → 5 — zero JS errors — but not yet eyeballed in a real browser. Build stamp now STAGE 1.11 / BUILD 059. With this build, the full fifteen-card pool named across the tier 1-5 substages (BUILD 051/052/055/057/058/059) is complete — Orison, Covenant, and Rapture (the three die-interaction cards BUILD 056's roll exposure exists to serve) are all now built.

Stage 1.12 audit remediation — classId (BUILD 060). gameState.player.classId changed from null to 'ordained' as the schema's initial value (not a runtime mutation, no helper needed). The two class lookup sites that previously used the hardcoded literal config.classes['ordained'] — the onBlankRoll listener registration and the startingDeck read in init() — now read config.classes[gameState.player.classId]. The prompt's own instructions also asked, contradictorily, for the class definition assignment itself (gameState.config.classes['ordained'] = {...}) to be keyed off classId too, so that a grep for config.classes['ordained'] would return zero matches; that edit was made as well, converting the definition assignment to gameState.config.classes[gameState.player.classId] = {...} (corrected in BUILD 061, see below). onNatTwenty/onNatOne remain unregistered empty stubs, untouched; card.classRestriction remains declared and unread, untouched. Behaviour unchanged: classId equals 'ordained' at object-construction time, before init() ever runs, so every lookup resolves identically to the old hardcoded literal — blank passive still grants 2 block, starting deck still 5 Strike/4 Ward/1 Rite. Build stamp now STAGE 1.12 / BUILD 060.

One-line correction to BUILD 060 (BUILD 061). BUILD 060's own prompt contained an internal contradiction, flagged rather than silently resolved: keying the class *definition* assignment off gameState.player.classId is architecturally backwards — definitions should be keyed by the class's own id, with player.classId selecting among them, not the other way round; a second class defined the same way would silently overwrite the first under whatever classId happened to hold at construction time. The definition assignment is reverted to the literal, gameState.config.classes['ordained'] = {...}; nothing inside the object changed. Both lookup sites (the onBlankRoll registration and the startingDeck read) are untouched, still keyed off gameState.player.classId exactly as BUILD 060 left them. Grepping index.html for config.classes['ordained'] now returns exactly one match — the definition assignment itself. Behaviour unchanged from BUILD 060: classId still 'ordained' on fresh load and after Restart Fight, blank passive still 2 block, starting deck still 5 Strike/4 Ward/1 Rite. Build stamp now STAGE 1.12 / BUILD 061.

Stage 1.12 audit remediation — Zeal's counter (BUILD 062). Closes the one Law 3 exception the audit found: Zeal's effect() used to write config.mods['zeal'].accumulatedBonus directly, bypassing all five state helpers, deliberately (registerListener() has no dedup, so a per-trigger registration would have accumulated duplicates forever) but in contradiction of STATE HELPERS' "config is set once at init and never changed." The counter now lives on the triggering face's own new optional modData field in gameState.die.faces instead — the same kind of per-instance persistent value Ordain's weight already is — read and written through the identical clone-then-write updateDie() pattern Ordain already uses (slice the faces array, Object.assign a new object for the one changed entry with modData: { accumulatedBonus: N }, write the whole array back). A face with no modData (never triggered Zeal) is treated as bonus 0; modData was deliberately not pre-added to every face at init, only ever written the first time a face's mod actually triggers. Because the value is per-face rather than per-mod, Zeal loaded onto two different faces accrues independently on each. accumulatedBonus was deleted from config.mods['zeal'] entirely — grep-confirmed it no longer appears as a field on that object, only inside face.modData now; config.mods['zeal'] itself is just {id, name, effect} again, identical in shape to every other mod. effect() gained a data parameter (reading data.faceNumber, exactly as Ordain's effect(data) already does) since the MOD_TRIGGER dispatcher already forwards {modId, faceNumber} to every mod's effect() — no dispatcher change needed. Damage progression is unchanged (10, 14, 18, 22, ... — base 10 plus the pre-increment bonus, still tagged calculateDamage(N, 'attack')), and resetFight() still never touches gameState.die, so the bonus still survives Restart Fight exactly as the old config field did. Log line now shows both numbers explicitly: [MOD] zeal: N damage (bonus +B, face F now +B+4) — B is the bonus this trigger was computed from, the incremented value is the one that face carries into its next trigger. registerListener(), the listener system, Ordain's own weight mutation (confirmed byte-for-byte unchanged by direct re-read), calculateDamage(), every card, and the other nine mods were not touched. Build stamp now STAGE 1.12 / BUILD 062.

Stage 1.12 audit remediation — START_OF_TURN lethal-mid-phase halts (BUILD 063). Closes the last two of the audit's lethal-mid-phase findings, both in START_OF_TURN, same shape as the BUILD 053 CARD_PHASE fix: BUILD 013's guard only fires at the top of runPhase() on the next phase-transition attempt, so a poison tick that drops hp to 0 mid-body previously let the rest of START_OF_TURN's own logic keep running for an already-decided fight. Two new in-place checks added, no new state: immediately after the player poison tick, if gameState.player.hp <= 0, the code calls runPhase(phase) — re-entering the function fires the exact same top-of-function guard that CHECK_WIN_LOSS already relies on (updateRun({status:'loss'}), log('[LOSS] player defeated'), return — the identical BUILD 053 pattern, no second loss path written) — then the outer call itself returns immediately, so nothing past that point in START_OF_TURN executes. Checked before the enemy tick, per spec: a dead player never reaches the enemy tick at all. Symmetrically, immediately after the enemy poison tick, if gameState.enemy.hp <= 0, the same runPhase(phase)-then-return pattern fires the guard's win branch (updateRun({status:'win'}), log('[WIN] enemy defeated'), openDieActionScreen(), return) before block clear/soul reset/clearListeners('turn')/roll-outcome clear/drawCards(5) can run. Exactly-once firing needed no new guard, per spec: runPhase()'s own top check is gated on run.status === 'active', so the moment either branch flips status away from 'active', the recursive call's own guard is what actually fires the log/status change, and the outer call's return is what stops the rest of the body — nothing here adds a second status flag. Verified via direct re-read: a poison tick that does not cross 0 falls through both new checks untouched and the rest of START_OF_TURN (block clear, soul reset 3, clearListeners('turn'), drawCards(5)) still runs exactly as before; a lethal enemy tick logs the poison line then exactly one [WIN] line with no block-clear/soul-reset/draw lines after it; a lethal player tick logs the poison line then exactly one [LOSS] line with no enemy-tick or draw line after it. The poison decay model itself, the damage amounts, PHASE_ORDER, calculateDamage(), every card, and every mod were not touched — re-verified by re-reading the modified section after editing. Build stamp now STAGE 1.12 / BUILD 063.

Stage 1.12 audit remediation — shared damage/block helpers (BUILD 064). Closes the last item of the four-part audit remediation queue. Added two new shared dispatch functions, placed immediately after generateBlock(): dealDamage(amount, sourceType, sourceId) runs the exact three-step sequence every attack card/mod effect() previously repeated inline — calculateDamage(amount, sourceType), apply the result to enemy.hp via updateEnemy(), fire ON_DAMAGE_DEALT with { amount, source: sourceId } — and returns the final computed damage so callers that log it still can; dealBlock(amount, sourceId) is the equivalent for generateBlock()/player.block/ON_BLOCK_GENERATED. sourceType has no default value on dealDamage, per the prompt — every call must name its source explicitly. All 16 actual calculateDamage()-and-apply-to-enemy call sites (strike, ward→n/a, rite, rebuke, censure, judgement, vestment→n/a, litany, purge, interdict→n/a, reckoning, retribution, covenant, rapture, orison, smite, penance, zeal, ordain — 12 cards + 4 mods, one more than the audit's own count of 15, which undercounted by one) and all 10 actual generateBlock()-and-apply-to-player call sites (ward, rite, vestment, litany, interdict, the blank passive, consecrate's per-card listener, penance, sanctuary, vigil — two more than the audit's own count of 8, which missed Consecrate's ON_CARD_PLAY listener and Vigil's END_PLAYER_TURN listener, both following the identical pattern) were replaced with single dealDamage()/dealBlock() calls. Every site's own conditional logic, branch logging, and every non-boilerplate line (poison-stack reads, intent checks, weight mutation, modData mutation, flashElement feedback) was left in place around the new call — only the three-line damage/block boilerplate itself was collapsed. ENEMY_ACT_PHASE's calculateDamage(rawDamage) — the audit's one untagged call site — now reads calculateDamage(rawDamage, 'enemy_attack'); it keeps its own inline call rather than routing through dealDamage(), since dealDamage() always applies to enemy.hp and this damages the player instead. 'enemy_attack' is deliberately distinct from 'attack' so it stays outside Fervour's sourceType === 'attack' check — verified directly, enemy damage is not doubled by a player-side Fervour trigger. Two call sites were deliberately NOT touched despite the success criteria's own grep phrasing implying otherwise: the player and enemy poison ticks in START_OF_TURN still call calculateDamage(gameState.X.poisonStacks, 'poison') directly, since the prompt's own explicit constraint list said not to touch the poison decay model, and poison's shape (stack decrement bundled into the same state-helper call, no ON_DAMAGE_DEALT fire, applies to either side) doesn't fit dealDamage()'s signature anyway — flagged as a contradiction resolved toward the explicit "do not touch" instruction, the same resolution BUILD 061 used for BUILD 060's classId contradiction. calculateDamage() and generateBlock() themselves are untouched, confirmed byte-for-byte identical by direct re-read. Verified via a Node vm load-and-drive of the actual index.html's script (document/window stubbed): Strike deals 5, Censure 14, Judgement 20, Ward grants 5 block, Vestment 13, Smite 16, Sanctuary 16; Fervour still doubles a 5-damage Strike to 10 and does NOT double a 4-stack poison tick (stays 4) or a 10-intent enemy attack (stays 10); Zeal on one face across four triggers (including a resetFight() in between) reads 10, 14, 18, 22, 26, confirming both the progression and BUILD 062's persistence are intact; Ordain on a different face still deals 10 and increments only its own face's weight, with no cross-talk into Zeal's modData or Zeal's weight; Retribution still deals 12 (capped, block 20) and 5 (uncapped, block 5) — all as PASS with zero JS errors. Build stamp now STAGE 1.12 / BUILD 064. With this build, all four items of the BUILD 059 audit's remediation queue (classId, Zeal's counter, the START_OF_TURN lethal-mid-phase halts, and these shared helpers) are closed.

Correction to BUILD 064 — dealDamage() gained an explicit target parameter, same build stamp (a follow-up prompt asked specifically for dealDamage(target, amount, sourceType, sourceId) rather than the enemy-only shape above, the same kind of same-numbered-build correction BUILD 061 made to BUILD 060). Signature is now dealDamage(target, amount, sourceType, sourceId, fireListener): applies to enemy.hp when target === 'enemy', player.hp otherwise; a new fireListener parameter (default true) exists solely so ENEMY_ACT_PHASE can opt out of firing ON_DAMAGE_DEALT, which that site never fired before this build and the prompt's own constraint said not to start. All 16 existing card/mod call sites were updated to pass 'enemy' as the new first argument — no other argument or behaviour changed at any of them, confirmed by direct re-read. ENEMY_ACT_PHASE now routes through dealDamage('player', rawDamage, 'enemy_attack', null, false) instead of calling calculateDamage() inline; its own damage===0-vs-else branch (the "fully blocked" vs "dealt" log lines, and the fact that a 0-damage hit does no meaningful state change) stays exactly as it was, since dealDamage() applies its result unconditionally regardless of whether that result is 0 — this is the call site's own decision logic, not boilerplate, per the prompt's own rule that per-site decisions stay at the call site. Re-verified via a fresh Node vm load-and-drive of the actual index.html's script: Strike 5, Ward 5 block, blank passive 2 block, Smite 16, Penance 8 damage/8 block, Fervour-doubled Strike 10, a same-turn enemy_attack under Fervour still landing undoubled at intent-minus-block (10, confirmed not 20), Retribution capped at 12 (block 20) and uncapped at 5 (block 5) — the earlier capped-Retribution run showed 24 instead of 12 purely because that particular test script had not cleared a still-active Fervour listener from an earlier assertion in the same run, not a code defect; re-run with clearListeners('turn') between assertions confirmed 12 exactly. Both poison ticks re-confirmed byte-for-byte untouched and still decaying correctly (4→3, 3→2 stacks, correct damage each). grep confirms calculateDamage() is now called from exactly three places: inside dealDamage() itself, and the two untouched poison ticks — the old three-line inline sequence appears nowhere else. Zero JS errors across all checks. Not yet eyeballed in a real browser. Build stamp remains STAGE 1.12 / BUILD 064.

Stage 1.13 — die face weight display and Strengthen rule change (BUILD 065), per the 24 August 2026 NAT 20 AND NAT 1 — THE ORDAINED decision. renderDieList() now wraps each row's mod span in a new .die-mod-wrap flex container (justify-content: space-between) and, only when face.weight > 1, appends a sibling .die-weight span reading '×' + face.weight — a face at weight 1 renders identically to BUILD 064, since the span is never created. Weight is read straight off the same face object the row already renders from (gameState.die.faces / gameState.enemy.die.faces via the existing forceRollFn-parameterised renderDieList), so Ordain, Strengthen, or any future weight source shows on the very next refreshInspector() with no new state added. .die-weight is folded into the existing nat/player-mod/enemy-mod colour rules alongside .die-mod so the badge always matches its row's colour, including on face 20 (nat colour). The Strengthen face picker's eligible-face filter (in renderDieActionPanel's 'strengthen_pick_face' branch) changed from f.modId !== null && f.number !== 1 && f.number !== 20 to an explicit three-branch check: face 1 always excluded, face 20 always included, every other face included only if modId !== null — so the eligible set is now loaded faces plus face 20, face 1 still untargetable. dieActionPickStrengthenFace() and its log line ('[DIE ACTION] strengthened face N to weight W') were already mod-name-free — untouched, no no-mod special case needed there. The button label (modDisplayName(f.modId) + ' (weight ' + f.weight + ')') was also already safe for face 20 without any change, since modDisplayName() has handled the 'NAT_TWENTY' id (returning 'NAT 20') as its own branch, before ever touching config.mods, since BUILD 004 — confirmed by direct re-read, no other code path in index.html assumes a Strengthen target carries a mod (Covenant already reads gameState.turn.rolledFaceWeight directly, independent of modId, per its BUILD 057 note). Build stamp now STAGE 1.13 / BUILD 065. Verified via a real headless-browser (Playwright/Chromium) drive of the actual index.html: fresh load shows zero .die-weight badges and zero row/wrap overflow at 1600x1080; dev-loading Ordain onto face 7 and force-triggering it renders face 7's row as ×2; Skip to Die Action → Strengthen offers exactly Face 7 (Ordain), Face 10 (Consecrate, the starting face), and Face 20 (NAT 20) — Face 1 is not offered; strengthening face 20 twice renders ×2 then ×3; acquiring Covenant via Skip to Card Reward, strengthening a loaded face to weight 3, and playing Covenant against it deals exactly 11 damage (2 + 3×3); forcing a natural face-20 roll at weight 3 still logs only '[ROLL] Nat 20 not yet implemented' with no other behaviour, and a normal blank-face roll still generates its 2 block unchanged — zero console errors throughout. A stress case (longest mod name "Consecrate" loaded at weight 20, face 20 also at weight 20) confirmed no wrapping, clipping, or column shift: every row's face-btn and die-mod-wrap left edge stayed pixel-identical across all twenty rows, and no row or wrap element's scrollWidth exceeded its clientWidth. Not yet eyeballed by a human in a real windowed browser. No roll/weight mathematics, mod/card logic, poison ticks, dealDamage()/dealBlock(), or the onNatTwenty/onNatOne stubs were touched.

Stage 1.14 — the Nat 20 mechanic (BUILD 066), per the 24 August 2026 NAT 20 AND NAT 1 — THE ORDAINED decision. The Ordained's onNatTwenty stub is now real: filters gameState.die.faces for loaded faces (modId !== null, excluding the NAT_ONE/NAT_TWENTY stub ids), logs one announcement line ('[ROLL] Nat 20: N loaded face(s) trigger(s)'), then dispatches each through callListeners('MOD_TRIGGER', { modId: f.modId, faceNumber: f.number }) — the exact same call resolvePlayerRoll already makes for a single rolled mod face, so no second copy of the trigger logic exists anywhere. Ascending order falls out for free: gameState.die.faces is invariantly stored in ascending face-number order (DIE FACE OBJECT STRUCTURE), so Array.filter() preserves it with no explicit sort needed. Face 1 never participates (excluded by the modId check, it's never 'loaded'); face 20 never triggers itself (it's what's filtered against, not what's dispatched) — it is the cause, not a participant. Zero loaded faces logs the announcement ('0 loaded faces trigger') and the forEach body simply never runs — no blank passive, no block floor, confirmed already guaranteed by resolvePlayerRoll's existing modId-based branch structure (NAT_TWENTY never falls into the BLANK_ROLL branch), not something this build added. Registered as a permanent NAT_TWENTY listener ('ordained_nat_twenty_passive') in init(), mirroring onBlankRoll's own permanent BLANK_ROLL registration — registered once, not per-turn, so repeated forced Nat 20s never accumulate duplicate listeners. resolvePlayerRoll's NAT_TWENTY branch itself is unchanged except the old stub log line ('[ROLL] Nat 20 not yet implemented') is gone — callListeners('NAT_TWENTY', {}) is the same call BUILD 054 wrote, only what it now reaches has changed. onNatOne and its own stub log line are untouched, confirmed by direct re-read — Penitence is a separate build. Rapture's cost/free condition (read the existing implementation first, per the prompt): it read gameState.turn.rollOutcome === 'mod' directly, which cannot distinguish a Nat 20 with loaded faces (rollOutcome reads 'nat_twenty', never 'mod') from a blank-die Nat 20 (also 'nat_twenty') or a plain Nat 1/blank roll. Fixed with the smallest change that isn't a parallel state field: a new gameState.turn.modTriggeredThisTurn boolean, set true inside the existing permanent 'mod_dispatch' MOD_TRIGGER listener (the one dispatch path both a normal single-face mod roll and Nat 20's loop both call through) whenever a real mod effect actually runs, declared false in the STATE SCHEMA turn object and reset to false every START_OF_TURN alongside rollOutcome/rolledFaceWeight (same updateTurn() call, same clear). Rapture's getCost() and effect() both switched from rollOutcome === 'mod' to reading this flag directly — it is not a duplicate of what rollOutcome already meant, it is the same "did a mod trigger" condition rollOutcome === 'mod' used to carry, made explicit so it also survives a Nat 20 turn. Covenant (reads rolledFaceWeight, untouched) and Orison (reads rollOutcome === 'blank', untouched and still correctly false on any Nat roll) needed no change — grep-confirmed rollOutcome === 'mod' now appears nowhere in index.html outside this Rapture comment block. Build stamp now STAGE 1.14 / BUILD 066. Verified via a real headless-browser (Playwright/Chromium) drive of the actual index.html, both as isolated state-injection checks and a full BUILD 065 regression re-run: with Ordain on face 2, Fervour on face 4, and Consecrate on the starting face 10, a forced face 20 logs '[ROLL] Nat 20: 3 loaded faces trigger' then Ordain's line (10 damage, face 2 weight 1→2), Fervour's line (doubler registered), and Consecrate's line (+2 soul) in that ascending order, with enemy HP, player soul, and face 2's weight all reflecting every effect having actually applied; clearing the die to zero loaded faces and forcing face 20 logs only the announcement ('0 loaded faces trigger') with player block, soul, and enemy HP all unchanged; Rapture costs 0 on a loaded-die Nat 20 and 2 (full price) on a blank-die Nat 20, confirmed via getCardCost(); forcing face 20 twice in a row on a die with one Ordain face produces weight 1→2 then 2→3 (no doubled trigger either time) with the NAT_TWENTY listener registry staying at exactly one entry across both forces (no accumulation); the full BUILD 065 weight-display/Strengthen/Covenant regression suite still passes unchanged, with the sole expected difference being that a natural face-20 roll now actually fires loaded mods instead of the old stub line — zero console errors across every check. Not yet eyeballed by a human in a real windowed browser. The weight display, the Strengthen filter, dealDamage()/dealBlock(), the poison ticks, the card pool's own per-card logic (beyond Rapture's condition source), and every mod's individual effect() were not touched.

Stage 1.15 — Nat 1's Penitence (BUILD 067), per the 24 August 2026 NAT 20 AND NAT 1 — THE ORDAINED decision (Penitence itself decided 21 August 2026). The Ordained's onNatOne stub is now real: rolling face 1 sets a new gameState.player.penitenceActive flag (declared false in STATE SCHEMA, alongside poisonStacks — the other fight-scoped player debuff — not on gameState.turn, per the prompt's explicit instruction) and logs its onset; rolling face 1 again while it's already true changes nothing, logging '[ROLL] Penitence already active' instead — confirmed no double-flip, no stacking. Registered as a permanent NAT_ONE listener ('ordained_nat_one_passive') in init(), mirroring BUILD 066's NAT_TWENTY registration exactly — registered once, not per-turn, so repeated forced Nat 1s never accumulate duplicate listeners. resolvePlayerRoll's NAT_ONE branch is unchanged apart from the old stub log line ('[ROLL] Nat 1 not yet implemented') being gone — callListeners('NAT_ONE', {}) is the same call BUILD 054 wrote, only what it now reaches has changed, same shape as BUILD 066's NAT_TWENTY change. The actual 1-soul loss is a separate step, deliberately not inside onNatOne itself: a new block in START_OF_TURN, placed immediately after the existing 'soul reset to maxSoul' line (so a Penitence turn begins at maxSoul - 1, e.g. 2, never negative — Math.max(0, soul - 1) guards the floor), that fires only when penitenceActive is true and logs '[START] Penitence: 1 soul lost, now N' on its own line every turn it applies. resetFight() now clears penitenceActive back to false in the same updatePlayer() call that already clears poisonStacks, so Restart Fight ends Penitence exactly like it ends poison. The Active debuffs panel (playerDebuffsValue, in renderStats()) previously only ever had one possible entry (poison), shown via a single ternary — extended to a small array + join(', ') so Penitence can appear alongside poison, reusing the join(', ') shape enemyBuffsValue already uses for its own multi-entry active-effects list rather than inventing a new display format. Build stamp now STAGE 1.15 / BUILD 067. Verified via a real headless-browser (Playwright/Chromium) drive of the actual index.html, driving the real phase machine (nextPhase()/forcePlayerRoll(), no shortcuts): forcing face 1 applies Penitence with its onset line and leaves the current turn's soul unchanged (3, since that turn's reset already happened); the next START_OF_TURN logs 'soul reset to 3' then 'Penitence: 1 soul lost, now 2' as its own line, landing at 2; forcing face 1 again mid-Penitence logs only 'Penitence already active' with soul unchanged, and the following turn still loses exactly 1 soul (3→2 again, not 2→0) — confirmed no stacking across two consecutive full turns; Restart Fight resets penitenceActive to false and the next turn's soul reset lands at 3 with no loss; a Nat 20 turn with Penitence active still fires all loaded faces normally (Ordain + Consecrate both triggered, ascending order, exactly as BUILD 066 verified) and Consecrate's +2 soul composes with a same-turn Penitence tick as ordinary arithmetic (3 → 2 after the tick → 4 after Consecrate, i.e. plain addition, no special-casing anywhere); the Active debuffs panel reads 'penitence' once active — zero console errors across every check. Not yet eyeballed by a human in a real windowed browser. onNatTwenty, the BUILD 066 dispatch, modTriggeredThisTurn, the weight display, the Strengthen filter, dealDamage()/dealBlock(), the poison ticks, and every mod/card's own logic were not touched.

Human browser confirmation, 24 August 2026 — Fergus confirmed Stages 1.13 (die face weight display and the Strengthen rule change, BUILD 065), 1.14 (the Nat 20 mechanic, BUILD 066), and 1.15 (Nat 1's Penitence, BUILD 067) directly in a real windowed browser. All three moved from headless-only to fully human-verified; see CURRENT SUBSTAGE.

Stage 2.0 — the run scaffold (BUILD 068). The first system above a single fight: a seven-slot act in two lanes (a shared start, upper: fight/elite/rite, lower: fight/rite/fight, rejoining at a shared boss), a map screen shown between slots, and the lane-lock/win/loss rules the prompt specified. gameState.run gained four new fields (screen: 'map'|'fight', outcome: 'active'|'won'|'lost', lane: null|'upper'|'lower', act: built by buildAct()) alongside a fifth, currentSlot (null at the fork | {lane,index} | 'boss') — run.status itself is untouched and stays exactly what it always was, the CURRENT FIGHT's own 'active'/'win'/'loss', still driving the existing per-fight VICTORY/DEFEAT banner (renderResultBanner(), byte-for-byte unchanged) and the existing dev-button gating; outcome is the new, separate WHOLE-RUN flag. gameState.enemy gained intentMin/intentMax (default 6/18, replacing a hardcoded Math.floor(Math.random()*13)+6 in START_OF_TURN with the exact same range read from these fields) so a slot's fight can carry its own band. Slot dispatch is a lookup table, SLOT_HANDLERS = {fight, rite}, keyed by slot.type and read by enterSlot() — adding a further slot type later means one more key, never a rewritten branch, per the prompt's explicit instruction. buildAct() hardcodes the prompt's own numbers directly (not a formula): upper fight 70 HP/6-18 intent, elite 100/12-22, rite; lower fight 70/6-18, rite, fight 85/6-18; boss 150/16-28 — the two normal-fight endpoints (70, 85) are exactly the "rising to 85" the prompt named, and every fight-type slot keeps the existing 6-18 band unless the prompt named a wider one, closing the recorded gap that intent never scaled while HP did. init() no longer builds the die/enemy-die/starting-deck inline and ends by jumping into a fight (startFreshTurnPaused()) — that inline setup is now buildFreshPlayerDieFaces()/buildEnemyDieFaces() (extracted verbatim, byte-identical output), and init() ends by calling the new startNewRun() instead, which performs that setup, builds a fresh act, and lands on the map screen — a fresh session no longer starts mid-combat. startNewRun() is also what #startGameBtn now calls: that button is relabeled "New Run" and is the only player-facing way to recover from a run-ending win or loss (not gated on outcome — always available, same always-enabled UX the old Restart Fight had) or to abandon an in-progress run; it fully rebuilds HP, the die, and the deck from scratch per the prompt's explicit success criterion. The old Restart Fight behaviour (full HP heal, single-fight reset) survives verbatim as a new dev-only #devRestartFightBtn inside #devStepControl, disabled outside gameState.run.screen === 'fight' — the prompt's explicit instruction that this control must never be a player-facing way to undo a loss. resetFight() (now dev-only) and the new shared clearFightScopedState() (used by both beginFightFromSlot() and advanceRun()) split what resetFight() used to do in one block: clearFightScopedState() resets everything that must reset between fights exactly as it always did (block, soul, poisonStacks, penitenceActive, deck reshuffled from the persistent ownedCards collection, turn-scoped listeners) but never touches HP, the die, or ownedCards itself; resetFight() layers a full HP heal on top of that shared reset, HP restoration being unique to the dev tool. beginFightFromSlot(slot) sets gameState.enemy's hp/maxHp/intentMin/intentMax from the slot's own data, calls clearFightScopedState(), switches screen to 'fight', and starts the turn exactly as init() used to. advanceRun() marks the current slot completed, computes the next currentSlot (next lane index, or 'boss' after the third), returns to the map, and calls clearFightScopedState() again (deliberately redundant with beginFightFromSlot()'s own call — both idempotent, no call-order dependency) — it is never called for a boss win, which the runPhase() win branch short-circuits into outcome:'won' directly, skipping the reward flow entirely (there's no next fight to reward for) and leaving the frozen battlefield up under the existing VICTORY banner. The player-death branch in that same runPhase() guard now also sets outcome:'lost' alongside the unchanged status:'loss' — this is what makes death end the whole run, not just the current fight; both lethal-mid-phase re-entry paths BUILD 063 already wired (the player and enemy poison ticks) funnel through this same single top-of-function guard, so no other lethal path needed touching. Rite slots reuse the die action panel completely unchanged, per the prompt's explicit instruction: openDieActionScreen(origin) gained an optional origin parameter ('reward', the default, preserves every existing call site's behaviour byte-for-byte; 'rite' is new) read once by closeDieActionScreen(), which now branches — 'rite' calls advanceRun() directly, skipping the card reward screen a rite never offers; anything else keeps the exact BUILD 050 behaviour of chaining into openCardRewardScreen(). closeCardRewardScreen() now always calls advanceRun() at the end, since that screen is only ever reached via the fight-win reward flow — never the rite path. The rite screen itself (riteStep, openRiteScreen()/closeRiteScreen()/riteChooseHeal()/riteChooseDieAction()/renderRiteScreen()) is a new small panel following the exact same null/'choose' step shape and .die-action-title/.die-action-row CSS reuse the card reward screen already established; riteChooseHeal() heals Math.min(maxHp, hp+20) — exactly 20, capped at 70, never both a heal and a die action since they're mutually exclusive button choices in the same panel. The map screen (renderMapScreen(), #mapScreen) renders both lanes full-length every time — a fork click both picks the lane and enters that lane's first slot in one click (there is no separate confirm-the-lane step, since the two lanes' first slots are the only choice a fork click could mean); every other lane's nodes render disabled and without a click handler the instant a lane is chosen, so the other lane is doubly unreachable, not just visually inert. The boss node and its preview (HP/intent text plus the actual enemy die reused via renderDieList() with a null forceRollFn, so it renders identically to a real enemy die but isn't clickable) read only gameState.run.act.boss's own static config — never gameState.enemy's live in-fight values — so they're legible from the very first map render and never change regardless of what happens in the fights leading up to the boss. #fightScreen is a new wrapper around the four elements (enemy panel, mid-strip, player panel, hand row) that used to be direct .left-col flex children; it carries .left-col's own display:flex/flex-direction:column/gap:6px so every child's existing flex sizing (the BUILD 040/041 fixes) sees the identical flex-item context, just one level deeper — #dieActionPanel/#cardRewardPanel/#riteScreenPanel stay outside it as direct .left-col siblings, since a rite's die action panel needs to render correctly reached from the map, not just from a fight. refreshInspector() toggles #fightScreen/#mapScreen display based on run.screen, and the dev step-control buttons (Next Phase, Begin Roll) are now additionally gated on screen==='fight' so they can't be misused from the map; endTurnBtn and #startGameBtn's disabled checks were extended to also read riteStep, matching how they already read dieActionStep/cardRewardStep. Build stamp now STAGE 2.0 / BUILD 068. Verified via a real headless-browser (Playwright/Chromium) drive of the actual index.html, clicking through real map nodes (not shortcuts) for every transition: a full lower-lane run (fight → rite → fight → boss) with a die action taken after the first fight (Strengthen face 10 to weight 2) still reading weight 2 at the third fight and at the boss, unchanged through every intervening slot; a card picked at the third fight's reward present in ownedCards at the boss; the heal rite capping at exactly 70 from both 55 and 65 starting HP (never above, never a simultaneous die action); the lower lane's nodes staying non-clickable (0 clickable elements) both immediately after the upper lane was chosen and after returning to the map from upper's first fight; the boss preview (150 HP, 16-28 intent) identical before entering and the moment its fight actually begins; beating the boss producing outcome:'won', the existing VICTORY banner, and no reward flow (dieActionStep/cardRewardStep both null); dying to an elite (100 HP, 12-22 intent, confirmed against the slot data) producing outcome:'lost' and the existing DEFEAT banner with dev step-control buttons disabled; New Run always available (even mid-run and after a loss) and resetting HP to 70, the die to fresh weight-1 faces, and ownedCards to the exact starting 10-card list; #startGameBtn correctly labelled "New Run" and #devRestartFightBtn correctly living inside #devStepControl, disabled on the map and enabled only mid-fight — zero console errors across every check. A separate geometry pass at 1400x900 and 1600x1080 confirmed #fightScreen's four children still show zero row overflow (identical to pre-068 die-row geometry) and neither #mapScreen nor .left-col overflow horizontally at either viewport. Not yet eyeballed by a human in a real windowed browser. The fight phase order, the card pool, the mod pool, Nat 20/Nat 1 behaviour, dealDamage()/dealBlock(), the poison ticks, the weight display, and the Strengthen filter were not touched — confirmed by direct re-read of each. One scope note volunteered per the prompt's own instruction to flag anything larger than it reads: this build touches more surface area than any prior single build (new run-level state, a new top-level screen, a new slot-dispatch system, a new reusable-panel entry point, and edits to init()/resetFight()/runPhase()'s win-loss guard/refreshInspector()) — it was built and verified as one coherent system because the prompt's own success criteria required a full start-to-finish run, but it is the largest single build in this project's history and is called out here rather than silently treated as routine.

Stage 2.1 — visual pass on the act map, plus a fightScreen/controls-row overlap regression fix (BUILD 069). Presentation only, per the prompt's own explicit framing — no run state, slot dispatch, or enemy numbers were touched; confirmed by a full re-run of BUILD 068's own test suites showing byte-identical results (same paragraph below). Layout regression first, per the prompt's own ordering: #fightScreen's min-height:0 (added in BUILD 068 to mirror .left-col's own rule) let that wrapper shrink below what its children's own min-heights (.combat-panel's 436px each, the BUILD 040/041 figure) actually needed; with no overflow:hidden on the wrapper, the children then rendered past its shrunk bottom edge, and .controls-row — a later sibling positioned relative to that same shrunk box — painted directly over the overflowed hand row, exactly matching "End Turn visibly overlaps the cards." Root-caused by dumping every .left-col child's actual getBoundingClientRect() (not guessed from the CSS alone): before the fix, #fightScreen's own box read top:38/bottom:620 while its own hand-row child read top:1078/bottom:1210 — physically impossible for a normal child unless the parent's box had already been forced smaller than its content, which is exactly what min-height:0 plus the default flex-shrink:1 did. Changed #fightScreen from min-height:0 to flex-shrink:0, restoring the exact BUILD 041 contract ("let panels take their natural size, let .left-col scroll for any excess") instead of the newer wrapper silently reintroducing the shrink-to-fit failure mode BUILD 041 had already closed. Re-measured after the fix at both required viewports (1400x900, 1600x1080): #fightScreen's box now reads bottom:1210 (matching its hand-row child exactly, no overflow) and .controls-row starts at top:1216, a clean 6px gap (the existing .left-col gap value) below it, zero overlap, all 5 hand cards individually confirmed fully within #handRow's bounds and not covered by any later element. Map centring and connections: renderMapScreen()'s DOM was restructured into a single centred .map-composition flex row — [START marker] [fork column] [.map-lanes, two rows] [merge column] [BOSS node] — with the fork/merge columns using align-self:stretch so their one vertical line's height is simply "however tall .map-lanes is" (recomputed by the browser every layout, no per-viewport pixel math anywhere in this build), and each lane row carrying its own absolutely-positioned ::before/::after stubs reaching into the zero-gap space next to those columns to visually complete the fork/merge join. Inter-node .map-connector elements (a fixed 20x2px bar) were added between each of the 3 nodes in a lane row, and both the stubs and connectors read one of three states via a lane-level modifier class (map-lane-committed/map-lane-abandoned/neutral, computed once per lane in the new mapLaneStateClass() and applied to both the row and every connector inside it) — the chosen lane's whole path (fork stub through every connector to the merge stub) reads --nat, the abandoned lane reads --muted, and before any lane is picked everything reads the plain --line neutral, so "a completed or committed path reads differently from one no longer available" is literal, not just implied by the nodes at its ends. Measured centring directly (not eyeballed): .map-composition's own centre-x lands exactly on #mapScreen's centre-x at both 1400px and 1600px widths (0px offset, computed via getBoundingClientRect on both elements) — the boss preview row (HP/intent/die) sits directly beneath the composition as its own centred block, sharing the same horizontal centre rather than trailing to one side. Node restyling reuses the existing colour vocabulary only, no new palette, via a new mapNodeStateClass() shared by lane nodes and the boss node alike: completed (--blank border/text plus a "✓ " prefix, the same tone already used for "present but inactive" elsewhere), current (solid 2px --nat border — the single next slot on an already-chosen lane), choice (dashed 1px --nat border — the fork's two pre-choice options, same colour as current since both are genuinely clickable now, dashed vs solid is the only distinction), inert (dim --muted, opacity 0.45 — the abandoned lane, and any not-yet-unlocked slot on the chosen one; the label itself is never hidden or truncated in any state, only the frame changes). The START marker reuses map-node-current/map-node-completed as a plain (non-button) div reading "you are here" before a lane is chosen and "already behind you" after. Boss preview: byte-identical HTML/text to BUILD 068 (HP, intent band, the full 20-row die via the same renderDieList() call with a null forceRollFn) — confirmed via direct string/count comparison, only its container position moved, into the new dedicated .map-boss-preview-row beneath the composition. End Turn: refreshInspector() now also sets #endTurnBtn's own style.display to 'none' outside gameState.run.screen === 'fight' (previously only .disabled was toggled, so the button stayed visible-but-inert on the map) — confirmed absent from the map's rendered DOM display, present and functional on the fight screen. Build stamp now STAGE 2.1 / BUILD 069. Verified via a real headless-browser (Playwright/Chromium) drive of the actual index.html: the fightScreen/controls-row geometry check above at both viewports; a full re-run of BUILD 068's own two test suites (the complete lower-lane run — fork choice, a die action persisting to the boss, a card reward persisting to the boss, the heal rite capping at exactly 70, the boss preview's static config, the VICTORY banner with no reward flow after a boss win — and the upper-lane elite death, DEFEAT banner, New Run recovery) both reproducing results identical field-for-field to their BUILD 068 runs, confirming no run behaviour changed; direct computed-style checks on a chosen lane's nodes and connectors showing exactly the designed rgb values for --nat/--blank/--muted on the committed vs abandoned lane; #endTurnBtn confirmed display:none on the map and visible on the fight screen — zero console errors across every check. Not yet eyeballed by a human in a real windowed browser. Run state, slot dispatch, enemy numbers, the fight phase order, the card/mod pools, and everything inside a fight beyond the button row's position were not touched — confirmed by direct re-read of every changed section.

Stage 2.2 — hover descriptions on reward/die-action choices, plus a reward duplicate fix (BUILD 070). Card reward hover: each card reward button now carries a child .hover-tip span reading CARD_EFFECT_TEXT[cardId] — the exact constant renderCardButtons() already reads for hand-row cards, byte-identical strings, no new copy written for any of the 15 pool cards (all 15 confirmed present in that constant by direct cross-reference against config.cardPool's own key list). Die action hover: explicitly NOT implemented, per the prompt's own stop-rather-than-invent instruction — grepped the whole file for a mod description/effect-text source (a MOD_EFFECT_TEXT-style constant, or a description: field on any of the eleven mod objects) and found neither; every mod's only effect text lives inside its effect() function's own log() calls, which is exactly the "log lines only" case the prompt named as out of scope. This covers both places the prompt asked about — the Load list (mod name only, via modDisplayName()) and the Strengthen face picker (same modDisplayName() call, same gap) — both are equally blocked by the same missing data, not two separate gaps. The hover mechanism itself (.hover-tip: position:absolute under its own button, opacity/visibility toggled by button:hover, pointer-events:none, max-width:220px so a long effect string wraps instead of stretching sideways into a neighbour) is built generically enough to cover mods the moment a description source exists, but no dieActionPanel button was actually given one. Reward duplicate fix: openCardRewardScreen() drew 3 cards via three independent Math.random() picks with replacement — the stale comment above it ("duplicates... allowed, since the pool is only 3 entries") was true when written but the pool has since grown to 15 (BUILD 051-059), leaving the draw logic never updated to match, producing exactly the observed Purge/Orison/Purge case. Fixed with shuffle(poolIds.slice()).slice(0, 3) — the identical shuffle-then-slice pattern dieActionChooseLoad() already uses for its own 3-mod offer — so all three reward slots are now guaranteed distinct; pool, DIE_ACTION_EXCLUDED_MOD_IDS-equivalent (there is none for cards, unchanged), and the offer count (3) were not touched. Checked the die action Load list for the same bug and found it did not have it: dieActionMods = shuffle(eligible.slice()).slice(0, 3) was already shuffle-based since it was first written, confirmed by direct re-read — nothing there needed fixing, reported here rather than silently left unmentioned. Build stamp now STAGE 2.2 / BUILD 070. Verified via a real headless-browser (Playwright/Chromium) drive of the actual index.html: hovering a reward card shows its tooltip with the correct CARD_EFFECT_TEXT string (e.g. Retribution → "damage = block, capped at 12"); all four reward-row buttons' bounding rects captured before and after the hover are byte-identical (JSON-equal), confirming no layout shift; the hovered tooltip's own rect checked against every sibling button's rect shows zero overlap with any of them; 10 consecutive openCardRewardScreen() calls each produced 3 distinct card ids (zero duplicates across all 10); 10 consecutive dieActionChooseLoad() calls likewise showed zero duplicates, confirming the Load list's pre-existing correctness; Load list buttons confirmed to carry no .hover-tip child (correctly not implemented) — zero console errors across every check. Not yet eyeballed by a human in a real windowed browser. Run state, slot dispatch, enemy numbers, the map, the fight phase order, and every card/mod effect were not touched — confirmed by direct re-read.

Stage 2.3 — hover descriptions on the die action Load list and both die rows (BUILD 071). Mod descriptions (the blocker BUILD 070 left open) were derived from each mod's own log() text, not authored: a new MOD_DESCRIPTION constant, one entry per pool mod, built by stripping each effect()'s '[MOD] name: ' log prefix and, only where a number is genuinely dynamic per-trigger (Zeal's stacking bonus, Virulence's doubled total), substituting the same static base figures already written into that mod's own dealDamage()/updateEnemy() call — e.g. Zeal reads '10 damage (bonus +B...)' in its log but the code's own base is a hardcoded 10 and its own growth is a hardcoded +4, so the description reads '10 damage, permanently gains +4 damage per trigger' with no new fact introduced. Vigil's registration log ('registered, block scales with cards held at end of turn') is used almost verbatim, dropping only the log-specific 'registered, ' prefix — this is the exact phrase the prompt itself quoted as an example, confirming the derivation approach. All eleven pool mods had usable log text; none needed to be left undescribed. A parallel NAT_DESCRIPTION constant covers the player's two Nat faces: face 20 reused the NAT 20 AND NAT 1 — THE ORDAINED section's own wording (every loaded face triggers, ascending order); face 1 reused BUILD 067's own Penitence onset log line almost verbatim. The enemy's Nat faces were deliberately left out of NAT_DESCRIPTION and get no hover text: both ENEMY_NAT_ONE and ENEMY_NAT_TWENTY still log 'not yet implemented' (BUILD 054), so there is no real behaviour to describe, per the prompt's own instruction to leave stubs alone. A genuine pre-existing gap was found and is reported here rather than fixed: enemy_buff_poison (the enemy's POISON face) has no registered listener for ENEMY_BUFF_TRIGGER anywhere in the file — grep-confirmed the hook is dispatched (callListeners('ENEMY_BUFF_TRIGGER', {buffId: face.modId}) in resolveEnemyRoll()) but nothing is listening, so rolling that face currently does nothing at all despite being coloured and labelled as a real buff; the only text describing its intended behaviour ('on trigger, apply 1 poison stack to player') lives in this file's own TEST ENEMY section as design intent that was never implemented in code. Describing that unrealized intent as if it were real behaviour would have been inventing a description for something that doesn't happen, so it was left with no hover text instead — this is flagged as a discovered implementation gap, not fixed, since fixing it would be a real behaviour change and out of scope for a presentation-only build. Blank faces were a deliberate judgement call, reported rather than decided silently: left un-hoverable. Eighteen identical '2 block on roll' tooltips across two dice would be repetitive noise for a fact the row's own blank '—' already communicates passively, and the die rows are dense (the exact BUILD 040/041/065 density concern) — quiet was chosen over complete. A shared faceHoverText(face) helper (new) resolves a face to its description or null (never inventing one), appending the face's own '×N' when weight is above 1, in the same notation the row's own weight badge already uses — 'the row already shows ×N and the two belong together' is now literal, not just adjacent. This flows into renderDieList() (used by both dice and the map's boss-preview die, so all three benefit uniformly from the same logic with no extra code) via the exact same .hover-tip component and CSS class BUILD 070 built for the card reward panel — the box's own appearance (background/border/text/font) is untouched, only its position differs: die-action buttons still pop the tip downward (nothing important below them), but a die row has another die row immediately beneath it, so its tip instead pops out to the row's right, vertically centred, via a new .die-row .hover-tip positioning override — .die-col has room next to .stats-col, and position:absolute means this can never affect document flow regardless of direction. The Load list and Strengthen face picker both gained the same .hover-tip child on their buttons, reading MOD_DESCRIPTION (Load list) or MOD_DESCRIPTION-or-NAT_DESCRIPTION (Strengthen picker, since face 20 can appear there with no mod at all, per BUILD 065's rule change). Build stamp now STAGE 2.3 / BUILD 071. Verified via a real headless-browser (Playwright/Chromium) drive of the actual index.html: face 6 loaded with Smite at weight 3 shows a hover tooltip reading '16 damage (×3)' with zero overlap against the row directly beneath it, and all twenty player-die rows' bounding rects are byte-identical with and without the hover active, confirming no layout shift; face 20 and face 1 show the designed Nat wording; a blank face and both enemy Nat faces and the enemy POISON face all confirmed to carry no .hover-tip child at all; the Load list forced to offer Virulence/Fervour/Ordain each showed their correct MOD_DESCRIPTION text on hover with the button's own bounding box unchanged before vs after — zero console errors across every check. Not yet eyeballed by a human in a real windowed browser. The roll/weight mathematics, every mod's actual effect(), run state, the map, the card pool, and the fight phase order were not touched — confirmed by direct re-read of every changed section.

Stage 2.4 — the player die alongside the boss die on the map, and during the die action (BUILD 072). Map: the boss-only .map-boss-preview-row was generalised to .map-die-preview-row (renamed, CSS otherwise untouched) and now holds a player-die half before the existing boss half — #mapPlayerPreview ("YOUR DIE" + live player HP, the exact same innerHTML shape #bossPreview already used for HP/intent) and #mapPlayerDieList, separated by a plain 1px .map-die-preview-divider hairline (var(--line), no new colour). Both halves render via the identical renderDieList(containerId, faces, null) call the boss preview already used — no third die renderer, confirmed by grep showing renderDieList() has exactly one implementation, called from three sites now instead of two (fight screen, map — both dice, die action screen). Because it's the same function, both dice on the map keep everything BUILD 065/071 already gave them — face numbers, mod names, ×N weights, and every hover-tip description — for free, with no extra code; verified directly by hovering a mod face on the map's own player die and confirming the same MOD_DESCRIPTION text (and same .hover-tip box) the fight screen already shows. Die action screen: renderDieActionPanel() gained a showDiePreview flag, set only inside the 'load_pick_mod' and 'strengthen_pick_face' branches (the two steps the prompt named explicitly — "choosing a mod to load, or a face to strengthen"), which appends a third .die-col ("YOUR DIE", id dieActionDieList) above the choice row via the same renderDieList() call, before that step's own buttons; the initial 'choose' (Load/Strengthen/Skip) step and the 'load_pick_face' step (choosing which blank face, once a mod is already picked) do not show it, since the prompt named only the other two steps — flagged in the completion message as a possible narrow reading worth a second look, not decided silently. Confirmed and reported per the prompt's own request, not fixed: Load can never overwrite an already-loaded face — dieActionChooseLoad()'s picker at the 'load_pick_face' step filters gameState.die.faces to f.modId === null only (read directly, this was never a bug, it's the literal implementation of "Load: place a mod on a blank face" from SCOPE — V1), confirmed by loading a mod onto face 2 and re-checking the offered face list, which no longer included Face 2. Build stamp now STAGE 2.4 / BUILD 072. Verified via a real headless-browser (Playwright/Chromium) drive of the actual index.html: the map shows both 20-row dice side by side (sideBySide confirmed via bounding-rect comparison), the composition's own centring is unchanged from BUILD 069 (0px off-centre, re-measured); hovering a loaded face on the map's player die shows its real MOD_DESCRIPTION text with zero layout shift across the whole preview row (every child's bounding rect byte-identical with/without the hover); the die action screen shows a 20-row die during both load_pick_mod and strengthen_pick_face, shows none during the initial choose step, and the die preview never overlaps the button row beneath it; loading a mod onto face 2 then re-opening the Load face picker correctly omits Face 2 from the offer — zero console errors across every check. Not yet eyeballed by a human in a real windowed browser. Run state, slot dispatch, enemy numbers, roll/weight mathematics, mod effects, the card pool, and the fight phase order were not touched — confirmed by direct re-read of every changed section.

Stage 2.5 — a shared opening fight between Start and the fork (BUILD 073). buildAct() gained an eighth slot, opening: { type: 'fight', label: 'Fight', enemy: { hp: 50, intentMin: 4, intentMax: 12 } } — the exact 'fight' type every normal/elite/boss slot already uses, dispatched through the same unmodified SLOT_HANDLERS table, so it grants a card reward and a die action on victory with zero special-casing, exactly as the prompt required. gameState.run.currentSlot gained a third possible value, the string 'opening' (alongside null and {lane,index}/'boss'), meaning "the shared opening fight is next" — startNewRun() now sets currentSlot: 'opening' instead of null, so a fresh run opens on this fight rather than at the fork. enterSlot() gained an 'opening' branch (mirroring its existing 'boss' branch — both are single slots living directly on act, not inside an upper/lower array) and advanceRun() gained a matching branch that marks act.opening.completed and sets currentSlot back to null (the fork) — lane stays null throughout, so resolving the opening fight is what first makes the fork's two options real choices. That gating is explicit: renderMapScreen()'s isForkChoice condition, previously just run.lane === null && index === 0, now also requires act.opening.completed, closing what would otherwise have been a visible bug (both lane-1 nodes reading as immediately clickable from the very first render, before the opening fight had even happened). The Start marker's own completed/current state moved from being keyed off run.lane to being keyed off act.opening.completed, since the opening fight — not the fork — is now the thing directly ahead of Start. Map rendering: a new Start→Opening .map-connector (the same plain node-to-node connector every lane segment already uses, left at its default neutral colour — there's no "abandoned" alternative for a mandatory shared segment, so no committed/abandoned styling was added here) plus the Opening node itself (button, .map-node-opening, using the same mapNodeStateClass() every other node already reads) were inserted between the existing Start and fork elements; the margin that used to create the Start→fork visual gap moved from .map-start-node to .map-node-opening, so the fork's own spacing is pixel-identical to before, just one stop further along. Both dice, the boss preview, and every hover-tip are untouched — renderMapScreen()'s die-preview row and renderDieList() itself were not touched at all, confirmed by direct re-read. Report, not fix, per the prompt's explicit request: the "Choose a blank face for Zeal" panel visible at −10 HP with the run already logged lost is reachable only through the dev Skip to Die Action button, never through real play. Traced directly: runPhase()'s loss branch (gameState.player.hp <= 0) only calls updateRun({status:'loss', outcome:'lost'}) and log() — it never opens dieActionStep or cardRewardStep, and once a real win opens the die action screen, phase progression halts (the top-of-runPhase guard no longer re-enters, since run.status leaves 'active'), so a player cannot take further lethal damage while a real post-win panel is open. #devSkipToDieActionBtn, by contrast, calls openDieActionScreen() completely unconditionally — grep-confirmed it has no disabled-state entry anywhere in refreshInspector() (unlike devNextPhaseBtn/devBeginRollBtn/devRestartFightBtn, which are all gated on run.screen/run.status) — so it remains clickable at any time, including after a logged loss, and does exactly this when clicked. Confirmed empirically: forcing player.hp to −10 and running the loss branch left dieActionStep at null (loss opens nothing); clicking #devSkipToDieActionBtn immediately afterward set dieActionStep to 'choose' while run.outcome stayed 'lost' and player.hp stayed −10 — reproducing the exact observed scenario through the dev shortcut alone. Nothing about this was changed. Build stamp now STAGE 2.5 / BUILD 073. Verified via a real headless-browser (Playwright/Chromium) drive of the actual index.html at both 1400x900 and 1600x1080: a fresh run's map shows exactly 9 .map-node elements and 5 .map-connector elements (the new Start→Opening link plus the two pre-existing links inside each 3-slot lane), only the Opening node clickable, both fork options correctly disabled/inert until Opening is won; winning the Opening fight (50/50 HP, intent 4-12, confirmed against the slot data) flips currentSlot to null and both fork options become clickable; by the time the elite is reached (opening fight + one lane fight, each resolved with a card pick and a Strengthen), ownedCards has grown by exactly 2 and the Strengthened face's weight has risen by exactly 2, and the elite's own stats are untouched (100 HP, intent 12-22); the composition stays perfectly centred (0px off, re-measured) with no horizontal overflow at either viewport; both die previews (20 rows each) and the boss preview text are byte-identical to BUILD 072 — zero console errors across every check. Not yet eyeballed by a human in a real windowed browser. The card pool, mod pool, roll/weight mathematics, the fight phase order, the hover system, and every other enemy's numbers were not touched — confirmed by direct re-read of every changed section.

Stage 2.5 — BUILD 074, diagnosis of a reported opening-fight reward bug. Reported: winning the opening fight showed the die action screen but no card reward. This did not reproduce. Four findings, gathered before any code was touched, per the prompt's own ordering: (1) line-by-line trace — the opening slot's post-victory flow was compared directly against a normal lane fight's and the boss's by driving all three through the identical instrumented sequence (force the win, read state, click the real Skip button in the real DOM, read state, click the real reward Skip button, read state) and diffing the results: opening and the lane fight produced byte-identical dieActionStep/cardRewardStep transition sequences (null→'choose'→null then null→'choose'→null), differing only in currentSlot's own value (which slot you're on, not whether the flow ran) — zero divergence found anywhere in beginFightFromSlot(), the runPhase() win guard, openDieActionScreen(), closeDieActionScreen(), openCardRewardScreen(), or closeCardRewardScreen(); none of these five functions contain any conditional keyed on slot identity except the one named in finding (3) below. (2) None of the four listed failure modes occurred: the card reward was not skipped (cardRewardStep genuinely reached 'choose' with 3 real distinct options), not rendered-and-dismissed (it stayed open and interactive until explicitly clicked), not hidden behind another panel (direct DOM inspection showed #cardRewardPanel at display:flex with its real card buttons and hover-tips present), and the order was unchanged (die action first, then card reward, same as every other fight). (3) The BUILD 068 boss short-circuit (runPhase()'s win branch) reads if (gameState.run.currentSlot === 'boss') — a strict string comparison against the literal 'boss', grep-confirmed as the only such check gating the reward flow anywhere in the file (four other 'boss' string comparisons exist, all in map rendering or slot dispatch, none gating reward/die-action). The opening slot's own currentSlot value is the distinct string 'opening'; the two can never compare equal, so this condition structurally cannot fire for the opening slot — confirmed directly in the same instrumented trace, which showed dieActionStep correctly reaching 'choose' (not being skipped) immediately after the opening fight's win. (4) BUILD 073's own test, re-read directly: it force-won each fight, then — gated on dieActionStep !== null and cardRewardStep !== null actually being true, not assumed — clicked a real Strengthen target and called the real cardRewardPickCard() with a card actually drawn from the real cardRewardOptions array, then asserted gameState.player.ownedCards.length had grown by exactly 2 across two fights. This counts actual completed picks, gated on the real screen states, not deck size or "a reward panel exists somewhere" — if the reward had been skipped for the opening slot, the pick would have been skipped too (the test's own if (cardRewardOpen) guard), ownedCards would have grown by only 1, and the assertion would have failed. It did not fail because the flow genuinely worked, re-confirmed here three independent ways: a fully natural multi-turn combat playthrough (no forced HP, no scripted clicks — actual playCard() calls and phase advancement) reaching dieActionStep: 'choose' after a real card-play kill; a forced win resolved via real DOM button clicks (not scripted state pokes) reaching a fully rendered, populated card reward panel; and the side-by-side diff against a lane fight and the boss described in (1) and (3). Conclusion: no bug exists in the current code, and no fix was applied — "the smallest change that makes the opening fight grant a card reward exactly like any other fight" is zero changes, since it already does, byte-for-byte. Applying a change here would have been a change without a defect to justify it, which the prompt's own instructions rule out. Build stamp updated to STAGE 2.5 / BUILD 074 as instructed; no other index.html logic was touched. One process note: the prompt referenced a "PROCESS AUDIT" section of this file to read before starting; no such section exists here (grep-confirmed against every # heading in the file) — flagged rather than silently proceeding as if it had been read. Zero console errors across every diagnostic run.

Stage 2.6 — boss HP 150→100, and the enemy die removed from normals/elites (BUILD 075). Two independent dials, per the prompt's own ordering: buildAct()'s boss.enemy.hp changed from 150 to 100; boss.enemy.intentMin/intentMax (16/28), the elite's hp/intent (100/12-22), and every normal fight's hp/intent (70-85/6-18) are untouched, confirmed by direct re-read of the whole buildAct() body. Die-carrying is now a property of the enemy: gameState.enemy gained a hasDie boolean (default true, matching the pre-075 always-has-a-die behaviour at the schema level), and every slot's enemy config in buildAct() gained an explicit hasDie field — false for opening/every normal fight/the elite, true only for the boss. beginFightFromSlot() now reads hasDie: !!slot.enemy.hasDie straight off the slot into gameState.enemy, the same pattern every other per-slot enemy field (hp, intentMin/Max) already uses — a future enemy gets a die by setting this one flag true on its own slot config, not by restoring deleted roll/render code. Neither die.faces itself nor buildEnemyDieFaces() nor buildFreshPlayerDieFaces() were touched — the enemy die system and its 20-face structure are fully intact and still built once per run in startNewRun(), exactly as before; hasDie only gates whether that structure is ever rolled or rendered for the current enemy. Phase order: PHASE_ORDER itself is byte-for-byte unchanged (still seven phases, ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE still among them) — nextPhase()'s ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE handling gained a single if (gameState.enemy.hasDie) guard around its existing rollDie()/resolveEnemyRoll() call; for a dieless enemy the phase still fires ([PHASE] ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE and the [LISTENER] calling hook line still log, confirmed in the captured log below), it simply has nothing to roll, so neither rollDie() nor resolveEnemyRoll() — and none of their log lines — run. forceEnemyRoll() (dev-only) gained the same guard for robustness, though it can no longer be reached via a real click since no die panel exists to click on. Rendering: refreshInspector()'s enemy die-list handling now branches on hasDie — true renders exactly as before (renderDieList('enemyDieList', ...), unchanged call); false hides #enemyDieList (style.display = 'none', not innerHTML-cleared, so a stale panel-title/rows sit inert rather than being destroyed and needing rebuilding if hasDie ever flips back) and adds a new combat-panel-no-die class to #enemyPanel, which centres the panel and caps .stats-col at a fixed 320px (was flex:1, which would otherwise stretch to fill the entire panel width left behind by the hidden die-col — noticeably wider than every other stats-col, reading as a hole rather than a deliberate layout) — no new colours, just flex/width rules reusing the panel's own existing structure. Report, not fix, per the prompt's explicit request: with normals/elites/the opening fight all dieless, ENEMY_BUFF_TRIGGER, ENEMY_NAT_ONE, ENEMY_NAT_TWENTY, and enemy_buff_poison do NOT lose their caller outright — buildEnemyDieFaces() (unchanged, grep-confirmed byte-identical) still puts ENEMY_NAT_ONE on face 1, enemy_buff_poison on faces 5/10/15, and ENEMY_NAT_TWENTY on face 20, and the boss is still the one enemy with hasDie: true, so resolveEnemyRoll() still runs for the boss every ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE and can still reach all four exactly as before. What changed is who can reach them: previously any enemy (normal, elite, or boss) could roll into these four; now only the boss can, since normal/elite/opening enemies never roll at all. ENEMY_BUFF_TRIGGER itself still has zero registered listeners anywhere (the BUILD 071-discovered gap, unaffected by this build — enemy_buff_poison's dispatch through it still fires into a void regardless of which enemy rolls it), and ENEMY_NAT_ONE/ENEMY_NAT_TWENTY remain the same "not yet implemented" stubs they were before. None of the four were deleted or altered. Build stamp now STAGE 2.6 / BUILD 075. Verified via a real headless-browser (Playwright/Chromium) drive of the actual index.html across a full run: the opening fight (dieless) shows hp 50/hasDie false, its die panel hidden and combat-panel-no-die applied; a captured turn's full log shows [PHASE] ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE and its [LISTENER] line firing normally, followed immediately by [PHASE] ENEMY_ACT_PHASE with zero [ROLL]/[ENEMY ROLL] lines in between, and the enemy still deals its normal intent-based damage; both lane fights confirmed dieless with HP unchanged (70, 85); the boss confirmed hp 100/maxHp 100/intent 16-28/hasDie true, its die panel visible with all 20 rows, combat-panel-no-die absent from its panel; a fresh run's map preview shows the updated 'HP 100' text with its own die still rendering all 20 rows, confirming the boss's map preview is untouched by the HP change — zero console errors across the entire run. Not yet eyeballed by a human in a real windowed browser. The player die, the card pool, the mod pool, run state, slot dispatch, the map's node logic, and the hover system were not touched — confirmed by direct re-read of every changed section.

Stage 2.7 — a dev tool to jump to any map slot, plus the BUILD 074 Skip to Die Action guard fix (BUILD 076). Dev chrome only, per the prompt's own framing — no game logic, no numbers, no player-facing change. Every node the run isn't currently standing on (already completed, not yet unlocked, or on the lane never committed to) is now clickable via a new devJumpToSlot(laneName, index), reached from a new shared attachDevJumpIfEligible(node, laneName, index) wired into all three node-creation sites in renderMapScreen() (opening, the two lane loops, boss) in place of their old node.disabled = true fallback — the genuinely-current/fork-choice branch (the real click path) is completely untouched. devJumpToSlot() writes exactly the run state a played run would have left — every slot strictly before the target on its path marked completed, run.lane set to the target's own lane (reassigning it if a different lane was previously committed, which the prompt explicitly allows), run.currentSlot set to the target — then calls the exact same enterSlot(laneName, index) every real arrival calls, which dispatches through the same unmodified SLOT_HANDLERS table (beginFightFromSlot() for a fight, openRiteScreen() for a rite) — no parallel path, no bypass, confirmed by direct re-read that neither function was touched. Jumping to the boss with no lane yet chosen defaults to completing the whole 'upper' lane (an arbitrary but deterministic choice, since reaching the boss legitimately requires some lane to be fully walked); jumping to a lane slot always completes that same lane's own earlier slots, never the other lane's. No fight along the way actually runs, so ownedCards/die/HP are left exactly as they already are — confirmed empirically (jumping straight to the boss from a fresh run left playerHp at 70 and ownedCards at the starting 10, with the boss itself arriving at exactly 100 HP/16-28 intent/hasDie true/its full 20-row die, byte-identical to a boss reached by playing three real slots). Guarded properly: devJumpToSlot() itself no-ops when gameState.run.outcome !== 'active' (checked first, before any state is touched — confirmed by calling it directly against a lost run and diffing gameState.run before/after, byte-identical), and attachDevJumpIfEligible() renders the node fully disabled (no listener attached at all, not just visually) under the same condition, so a won/lost run shows every non-current node as plainly inert, not dev-jumpable. Visual distinction, reusing only existing vocabulary: dev-jump nodes get a new .map-node-dev-jump class — --muted (the same colour .map-node-inert already used for "not a real option") with a dotted border and a pointer cursor, deliberately never the --nat gold that means "pick me" on a genuine choice or current node — plus a .hover-tip (the same BUILD 070 component, reused, not reinvented) reading 'Dev: jump here — skips earlier slots, grants no rewards'. Verified directly: with a fresh run, every non-opening node reads isChoice:false, isDevJump:true (0 real choices exist yet, since the opening fight hasn't been won); once the opening fight is won, exactly 2 real .map-node-choice nodes exist (the fork) alongside 6 .map-node-dev-jump nodes; after a real lane pick, every node on the un-chosen lane reads isChoice:false/isCurrent:false/isDevJump:true — the unchosen lane is never a real choice, exactly as BUILD 075 left it, only ever reachable via the dev tool. Skip to Die Action: the BUILD 074-found gap (fired unconditionally, no run.status/outcome check, no disabled entry — what produced the phantom "Choose a blank face for Zeal" panel at −10 HP) is closed with exactly the two lines the prompt asked for: a guard at the top of its click handler (if (gameState.run.status !== 'active' || gameState.run.outcome !== 'active') return;) and a matching disabled entry in refreshInspector() mirroring the pattern devRestartFightBtn already uses. Skip to Card Reward was deliberately left untouched — the prompt named only Skip to Die Action, and fixing the other would have been unrequested scope. Report, not fixed, discovered while verifying the loss-guard: the genuinely-current node's own real click path (the unmodified branch — e.g. the opening fight while it's still gameState.run.currentSlot) has no run.outcome/status guard of its own and remains clickable even after the run is already won or lost, since that branch was never touched by BUILD 068-075 and isn't a "dev skip" this build's guard requirement named. This is a distinct, pre-existing gap from the one just closed — flagged here rather than fixed, since it falls outside "fix that Skip to Die Action gap" and outside "do not change any other logic." Build stamp now STAGE 2.7 / BUILD 076. Verified via a real headless-browser (Playwright/Chromium) drive of the actual index.html: dev-jumping straight to the boss from a fresh run produces lane 'upper', currentSlot 'boss', all three upper slots completed, opening completed, screen 'fight', enemy hp/maxHp 100/100, intent 16-28, hasDie true, 20 die rows — force-winning that boss then produces outcome 'won'/status 'win' with no reward flow, identical to a boss reached by playing; dev-jumping to a rite slot (upper index 2) correctly marks upper[0]/upper[1] completed (not the target itself), opens the real rite panel (riteStep 'choose', #riteScreenPanel visibly flex) via openRiteScreen(), and leaves screen at 'map' (rites never switch to the fight screen); after a forced loss, devSkipToDieActionBtn is disabled and a forced click leaves dieActionStep at null, every non-current map node is disabled, and calling devJumpToSlot() directly leaves gameState.run byte-identical before and after — zero console errors across every check. Not yet eyeballed by a human in a real windowed browser. Enemy numbers, the player die, the card and mod pools, run state beyond marking skipped slots complete, and the fight phase order were not touched — confirmed by direct re-read of every changed section.

Next action is a planning decision on the FIGHT RESET spec change BUILD 049 made (does hp-restore-on-Restart-Fight stay permanent, or was permadeath-with-no-recovery the intended final design), a planning decision on how to actually get the dev tools above the fold (BUILD 043 closed the gap by ~10% but left ~590px/~410px still below it), plus the standing manual browser playtest across the outstanding BUILD 014-029 UI/turn-flow passes, the BUILD 030 poison tick, the BUILD 031 Blight/Virulence pass, the BUILD 033 force-roll timing fix (which supersedes BUILD 032), the BUILD 034 Sanctuary mod, the BUILD 035 Vigil mod, the BUILD 036 Restart Fight pause, the BUILD 037 init() pause fix, the BUILD 038 die action loop, the BUILD 039 dev tool addition, the BUILD 040-041 hand-row bleed-through fix, the BUILD 042 player poison decay fix, the BUILD 043 sizing pass, the BUILD 044 Zeal mod, the BUILD 046 Fervour mod, the BUILD 047 Ordain mod, the BUILD 048 reward-pool fix, the BUILD 049 Restart Fight fix, the BUILD 050 card reward screen, the BUILD 051 tier-1 card pool, the BUILD 052 Communion fix plus tier-3 conditionals, the BUILD 053 mid-CARD_PHASE victory fix, the BUILD 054 logging fix, the BUILD 055 Retribution card, the BUILD 056 roll-result exposure, the BUILD 057 Covenant card, the BUILD 058 Rapture card, the BUILD 059 Orison card, the BUILD 060 classId fix, the BUILD 061 correction, the BUILD 062 Zeal counter migration, the BUILD 063 lethal-mid-phase fix, and the BUILD 064 shared-helpers pass (plus its same-build target-parameter correction) — the full four-item audit remediation queue is now closed, no further audit-remediation items queued — not further building.

# CONFIRMED WORKING lines, full text, moved 21 Sep 2026
